X_{\alpha+1}\to X_{\alpha}
has fiber in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
)
.

(b)

For a limit ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
the map
\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}\to X_{\lambda}

has cofiber in
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

(
the map
X_{\lambda}\to\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}

has fiber in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
)
.

Note this includes sequential colimits
(
limits
)
growing in weight coconnectivity

(
connectivity
)
.

Proof.

This is a special case of Lemma
A.5
.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a weighted category
then it satisfies the above saturation properties,
see e.g.
[
17
, Lemma 3.1.2]
.
As a consequence,
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
respectively
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}

is automatically closed under finite limits
respectively finite colimits,
so a weakly
0
-weighted category is just a weighted category.

Lemma 3.12
.

The following are convenient consequences of saturation:

(1)

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a fully faithful weight exact functor of weakly weighted categories.
Let
X\in\mathcal{C}
and suppose that
f(X)
is connective
(
coconnective
)
.
If
\mathcal{C}
is left
(
right
)
saturated, then
X
is connective
(
coconnective
)
.

(2)

Let
L\colon\mathcal{C}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{D}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}R

be an adjunction between weakly weighted categories.
If
\mathcal{C}
is right saturated and
R
preserves connectives,
then
L
preserves coconnectives.
If
\mathcal{D}
is left saturated and
L
preserves coconnectives,
then
R
preserves connectives.

(3)

In particular, if
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a weight
exact functor of saturated weakly weighted categories which is
an underlying equivalence (i.e. in
Cat
),
then also
f^{-1}
is weight exact.

Proof.

For (1) the “only if” follows from weight exactness.
If
f(X)
is connective (the coconnective case being analogous),
then
\hom(Y,X)\simeq\hom(f(Y),f(X))\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}

for all
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
, so saturation of
\mathcal{C}
implies
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
The proof for (2) is similarly based on saturation of both
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
,
and (3) is a direct consequence of (2) and the fact that
f^{-1}

will be both left and right adjoint to
f
.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

All of these can go wrong in the non-saturated setting.
For example, if
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})

is weakly weighted but not saturated
then
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq-1})

is also weakly weighted,
and
\Sigma\colon(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq-1})\to(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})
is a weight exact functor which is an underlying
equivalence, but its inverse is not weight exact.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be weakly
\ell
-weighted
and define
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0}={(\mathcal{C}_{\leq-1})}^{\perp}

and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0}={}^{\perp}{(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 1})}
.
Then
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\coloneqq(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0})

is still weakly
\ell
-weighted.
The identity functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is weight exact,
and the identity functor
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}
has amplitude
[-\ell,\ell]
.
In particular:

(1)

if
\ell<\infty
then
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent in
wWCat
to a saturated weakly
\ell
-weighted category.
11
11

11

Observe that this also proves that saturation is not invariant under equivalence in
wWCat
, though of course it is in
\textsf{wWCat}_{=0}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{D}
is weakly
\ell^{\prime}
weighted and
\ell,\ell^{\prime}<\infty

and
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a map of bounded below
(
bounded
)
amplitude
which is an equivalence on underlying categories,
then
f^{-1}
also has bounded below
(
bounded
)
amplitude,
so
f
is an equivalence in
wWCat
.

Proof.

Clearly
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0}
are still orthogonal,
and
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0}
.
By Lemma
3.10
it also follows
that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0}
have the correct closure properties.
The weight decompositions of
\mathcal{C}
also give weight decompositions in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
Hence
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a saturated weakly
\ell
-weighted category,
and the identity
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is weight exact.

Now suppose that
X\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0}
.
Picking a weight decomposition
X_{\leq-1}\to X\to X_{>-(\ell+1)}
in
\mathcal{C}
,
the first map vanishes, exhibiting
X
as retract of the last term,
thus proving
X\in\mathcal{C}_{>-(\ell+1)}
.
Similarly, if
Y\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0}
, we can pick a weight decomposition
Y_{<\ell+1}\to Y\to Y_{\geq 1}

in
\mathcal{C}
and again the map
Y\to Y_{\geq 1}
vanishes since
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 1}
.
Thus
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{<\ell+1}
.
This proves that
\id\colon\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{C}

has weight amplitude
[-\ell,\ell]

(note that
\ell+1=\ell
for
\ell=\infty
).
In the case
\ell<\infty
, it follows that
\id
is an equivalence in
wWCat
,
showing (1).
Finally, (2) follows from (1) and Lemma
3.12
(3).
∎

Example 3.15
.

Let
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq b}
for some
b\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then
\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,-)\colon\mathcal{C}\to\textsf{Sp}
has weight amplitude
[-b,\infty]

by the axioms of a weakly weighted category,
where we equip
Sp
with the standard weight structure (whose connectives are the
t
-connectives).
In particular, by Example
2.28
and Corollary
2.22

this functor sends sequential cones growing in (weight) connectivity to colimiting cones.

3.1.
Weight complexes in weakly weighted categories

Much of the theory of weight structures carries over to this weakened setting.
Importantly, we have a good notion of weight complexes,
which we want to introduce in slightly larger generality.

Definition 3.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category.
We call a full stable subcategory
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

left dense in
\mathcal{C}
if for every
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
n\geq 0
there is a map

f\colon U\to X
with
U\in\mathcal{U}
and
\cofib(f)\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}
.
Note that
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
is left dense in
\mathcal{C}
.

Given an object
X\in\mathcal{C}
, a
\mathcal{U}
-weight complex for
X

is a diagram
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}=\mathcal{U}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}_{/X}

so that
\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{/X}
is a cone growing in connectivity
in the sense of Definition
2.12
,
meaning that for every
n\geq 0
the cofibers of
U_{k}\to X

(and hence of
U_{k}\to U_{k+1}
) are eventually
n
-connective.

Remark 3.17
.

A left dense subcategory
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is always dense.
In fact, given
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq n}

we can choose a cofiber sequence
U\to X\to X_{w\geq n+1}

with
U\in\mathcal{U}
. Since
X\to X_{w\geq n+1}
vanishes,
this exhibits
X
as retract of
U
.
Note also that if
X\in\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}
then
U\in\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}
.
Dealing with retract / idempotent completeness issues is precisely the purpose of this definition.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category
and
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
be left dense in
\mathcal{C}
.
Given
X\in\mathcal{C}
, there exists a strictly increasing
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

and a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\leq f(0)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\leq f(1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\leq f(2)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq f(0)+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq f(1)+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

where each column is a cofiber sequence, with
X_{\geq n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}

and
U_{\leq f(n)}\in\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\leq f(n)}
.
In particular, the first two rows define a
\mathcal{U}
-weight complex for
X
.
Moreover, if
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}
is any
\mathcal{U}
-weight complex
for
X
, then this functor
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}

is colimit-cofinal.

Proof.

We construct the diagram inductively. To begin, choose a cofiber
sequence
U\to X\to X_{\geq 0}
. Since
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
,
we define
f(0)
so that
U\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq f(0)}

and let
U_{\leq f(0)}=U
.
For notational convenience, let
f(-1)\coloneqq-1
.
Suppose by induction we have constructed the diagram up to the
n
-th column.
Again we can find
f(n+1)\geq f(n)

and some
U_{\leq f(n+1)}\in\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\leq f(n+1)}

with a cofiber sequence
U_{\leq f(n+1)}\to X\to X_{\geq f(n)+1}
.
Since the composite
U_{\leq f(n)}\to X_{\geq f(n)+1}
vanishes,
we obtain factorizations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\leq f(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\leq f(n)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq f(n-1)+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq f(n)+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

which give us the desired maps to extend the diagram to the
(n+1)
-st column.
By induction, we obtain the desired diagram.

For the addendum, we argue using Quillen’s Theorem A.
Given
U\in\mathcal{U}_{/X}
we have a pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{N}_{U/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{U}_{/X})_{U/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}_{/X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\bullet}}

We need to show that
\mathbb{N}_{U/}
is weakly contractible, i.e.
|\mathbb{N}_{U/}|\simeq*
.
Note that
\mathbb{N}_{U/}\to\mathbb{N}
is a left fibration classifying the functor

\mathbb{N}\to\textsf{An},n\mapsto\map_{\mathcal{U}_{/X}}(U,U_{n})
.
Therefore

|\mathbb{N}_{U/}|\simeq\colim_{n}\map_{\mathcal{U}_{/X}}(U,U_{n})\simeq\colim_{n}\map_{\mathcal{C}}(U,U_{n})\times_{\map_{\mathcal{C}}(U,X)}*

Since
U\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
, Example
3.15

yields
\colim_{n}\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(U,U_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(U,X)
.
Using that
\tau_{\geq 0}\colon\textsf{Sp}\to\textsf{Sp}
preserves sequential colimits,
we conclude that
\mathbb{N}_{U/}
is weakly contractible, so
U_{\bullet}

is colimit-cofinal.
∎

Example 3.19
.

In the case
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
, and
\ell=0
,
we can pick
f(n)=n
and obtain the classical weight complex,
with
n
-th column
X_{\leq n}\to X\to X_{\geq n+1}
,
and where
X_{\leq n}\to X_{\leq n+1}
has cofiber in
\mathcal{C}_{=n+1}
.
Similarly, for
1\leq\ell<\infty
we can pick
f(n)=\ell n
,
where the
n
-th column looks like
X_{\leq\ell n}\to X\to X_{\geq 1+\ell(n-1)}
,
and the cofiber of
X_{\leq\ell n}\to X_{\leq\ell(n+1)}

lies in
\mathcal{C}_{[1+\ell(n-1),\,\ell(n+1)]}
.

The following lemma is an analogue of the classical cellular approximation theorem.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly
\ell
-weighted category for
1\leq\ell<\infty

and
X_{\leq\ell\bullet}
a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex for
X\in\mathcal{C}

as in the above example. Given a map
f\colon X\to Y
,
there exists a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex
Y_{\leq\ell\bullet}

as above and a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq\ell n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq\ell(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell(n+2)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

In the case
\ell=0
where
\mathcal{C}
is weighted, we even have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Proof.

We only show the case
\ell\geq 1
, the other one being analogous and easier.
Picking a weight decomposition
Y_{\leq\ell}\to Y\to Y_{\geq 1}
,
we note that
X_{\leq 0}\to Y_{\geq 1}
vanishes,
and hence we obtain a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq 1-\ell}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\geq 1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

The left square yields the base case of our induction on
n
.
Suppose now we have already constructed the diagram up to
f_{n}
,
and we wish to construct
f_{n+1}
making the squares with
f_{n}

and
f
commute.
Consider the following commutative diagram, where
P
is defined as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq\ell n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq\ell(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Since
\ell\geq 1
, we have
\cofib(Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}\to P)=\cofib(X_{\leq\ell n}\to X_{\leq\ell(n+1)})\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq\ell(n+1)}
,
and hence also
P\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq\ell(n+1)}
.
Now pick a weight decomposition
Y_{\leq\ell(n+2)}\to Y\to Y_{\geq 1+\ell(n+1)}
,
which yields a factorization of
P\to Y
through
Y_{\leq\ell(n+2)}\to Y
,
ultimately giving the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq\ell n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq\ell(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{\leq\ell(n+2)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

By induction, we can thus build the desired diagram,
and it remains to show that the sequential diagram
Y_{\leq\ell\bullet}

constructed in this way is indeed a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex for
Y
.
To this end, it suffices to check that the cofiber of the composite

Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}\to P\to Y_{\leq\ell(n+2)}
grows in connectivity
with
n
. Using that
\cofib(Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}\to P)=\cofib(X_{\leq\ell n}\to X_{\leq\ell(n+1)})\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1+\ell(n-1)}

and that we can similarly control the connectivity
of the cofibers of the maps
Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}\to Y

and
Y_{\leq\ell(n+2)}\to Y
,
a straightforward diagram chase shows that
the cofiber of the above composite is indeed in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1+\ell(n-1)}
.
∎

Remark 3.21
.

The above proof shows that we can even pick the
cofiber sequences
Y_{\leq\ell n}\to Y\to Y_{\geq 1-\ell(n-1)}
a priori, however the maps
Y_{\leq\ell n}\to Y_{\leq\ell(n+1)}
will depend on
X
and
f
.

4.
Left completion of weak weight structures

We now come to the heart of this paper.
The section is divided into three subsections.
In subsection
4.1

we define left completeness of weak weight structures
in terms of the completeness conditions we saw in Section
2
,
and draw some consequences from the theory developed there.
We conclude by showing that the connective part of a left complete
weighted category is freely generated by its weight heart under geometric realizations
(Proposition
4.7
).

Next, in subsection
4.2

we define left completions
and state our main theorem (Theorem
4.9
) on their existence and properties.
This identifies the left completion
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
of
\mathcal{C}
as a certain full subcategory
of
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
and discusses functoriality of the association
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
.
We also draw many useful consequences of this Theorem,
such as the functoriality of weight complexes when considering them as
\Ind
-objects
(Corollary
4.10
)
and that left completion interacts well with the dual notion of right completion
(Proposition
4.16
).

Finally, subsection
4.3

is devoted to the proof of Theorem
4.9
.
Along the way, we will see a criterion
for recognizing left completions (Corollary
4.20
)
and how to generate a weight structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
for any weakly weighted
\mathcal{C}

(Proposition
4.25
).

4.1.
Left completeness of weak weight structures

Definition 4.1
.

A weakly weighted category
\mathcal{C}
is left (weight) complete
if its underlying connectivity structure is colimit complete (cf. Observation
2.18
), meaning:

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}\coloneqq\bigcap_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}=0
.

(2)

\mathcal{C}
admits sequential colimits growing in (weight) connectivity.

(3)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is closed under such sequential colimits.

We let
\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}\subseteq\textsf{wWCat}
be the full subcategory on left complete weakly weighted categories,
and similarly for
\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq 0},\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}_{=0}
from Definition
3.8
.

Remark 4.2
.

As mentioned in Observation
2.18
,
parts (2) and (3) together are equivalent to
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
admitting
and
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
preserving geometric realizations.

Observation 4.3
.

The property of a weakly weighted category
\mathcal{C}
being left complete
does not depend on
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
. In particular, if
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0})

is another weak weight structure on
\mathcal{C}
with the same connectives (e.g.
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\leq 0}={}^{\perp}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})
), then it is also left complete.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{C}
is left complete then
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
is left complete and idempotent complete by Corollary
2.31
,
and
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
admits the “finite type” colimits described in Lemma
2.30
.
In particular, also
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{=0}
are idempotent complete.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{U}
is left dense in
\mathcal{C}
,
then any
\mathcal{U}
-weight complex
X_{\bullet}
of
X
converges, i.e.
\colim_{n}X_{n}\simeq X
.
In this sense, any object in
\mathcal{C}
may be obtained as the colimit
of a sequential diagram in
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
growing in connectivity.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category which also admits a
t
-structure with
\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0}
.

(1)

If the
t
-structure is left complete, then also the weight structure is left complete.

(2)

Suppose that

(a)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}=0
.

(b)

\mathcal{C}
admits sequential limits growing in connectivity.

(c)

\mathcal{C}
is right saturated in the sense of Definition
3.9
(e.g. it is a non-weak weight structure).

Then the
t
-structure and hence also the weight structure is left complete.

Proof.

Point (1) is a direct consequence of Lemma
2.25
.
For (2), by the same lemma it suffices to show that the underlying connectivity
structure is limit complete. The first two requirements are exactly the hypotheses (a) and (b),
and the closure of
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
under sequential limits growing in connectivity
follows from the right saturation and Lemma
3.10
.
∎

Example 4.5
.

Since the usual
t
-structure on
Sp
is complete, also the standard weight structure on
Sp
is left complete.

Lemma 4.6
.

(1)

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be of amplitude
[a,b)

for
-\infty<a\leq b\leq\infty

between left complete weakly weighted categories.
If its restriction
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}

is fully faithful
(
an equivalence
)
, then so is
f
.

(2)

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a weight exact functor of left complete weakly weighted categories.
If the restriction
\mathcal{C}_{=0}\to\mathcal{D}_{=0}
is fully faithful
(
an equivalence
)
,
then so is the restriction
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{>-\infty}
.

Proof.

For (1), we note that
f
has bounded below amplitude
and thus preserves sequential colimits growing in connectivity by Lemma
2.21
.
By the existence of convergent
\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
-weight complexes in
\mathcal{D}
,
essential surjectivity of the restriction implies essential surjectivity of
f
.
Now suppose the restriction is fully faithful and let
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
have
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complexes
X_{\bullet}
and
Y_{\bullet}
.
Then
\hom(X,Y)\to\hom(fX,fY)
identifies with
\lim_{k}\colim_{n}\hom(X_{k},Y_{n})\simeq\lim_{k}\colim_{n}\hom(fX_{k},fY_{n})
, so we conclude.
For (2), exactness of
f
implies that it restricts to a fully faithful functor
on weight bounded objects which is essentially surjective if
f|_{\mathcal{C}_{=0}}
is.
We noted in Observation
4.3
that
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty},\mathcal{D}_{>-\infty}
are still left complete,
so the claim now follows from (1).
∎

We now want to present another description
of the bounded below part of a left complete weighted category (i.e. the case
\ell=0
).
This expresses that in a left complete weighted
category
\mathcal{C}
, the category
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is in a sense
“freely generated” by the colimits of weight complexes.
To make this formal, it is convenient to use the Dold-Kan correspondence
to recast sequential diagrams growing linearly in connectivity
into geometric realizations of diagrams landing in the weight heart.
To this end, recall the properties of the Dold-Kan correspondence
mentioned in Example
2.16
,
which lets us make our above intuition precise in the following way.
Let
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(-)
denote the operation of freely adding geometric realizations
in the sense of
[
24
, Prop. 5.3.6.2]
.

Proposition 4.7
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a left complete weighted category,
then the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{=0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}

induces equivalences
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}

and
\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0}))\simeq\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
.
Moreover, every object in
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C})
is a geometric realization
of a simplicial object in
\mathcal{C}_{=0}
.

Proof.

For this proof, we let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})

denote the full subcategory
on geometric realizations of simplicial objects in
\mathcal{C}_{=0}

(the point being that we don’t allow iterated geometric realizations).
We will first show that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
,
and then conclude that actually
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\simeq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})
.

By Example
2.16
, the category
\mathcal{C}

admits geometric realizations of uniformly bounded below diagrams.
In particular,
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
admits geometric realizations,
and we obtain a comparison functor

\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\to\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
taking the colimit
and restricting to the inclusion on
\mathcal{C}_{=0}
.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\to\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
denote its restriction.
We claim that
f
is an equivalence.
To see that it is essentially surjective,
let
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
and consider an associated weight complex

X_{\leq\bullet}
constructed as in Lemma
3.18
,
so that
X_{\leq n}\in\mathcal{C}_{[0,n]}
.
The sequential diagram
X_{\leq 0}\to X_{\leq 1}\to\cdots

corresponds under Dold-Kan to a simplicial object
X^{\prime}

with
|X^{\prime}|=\colim_{n}X_{\leq n}=X
.
Moreover,
X^{\prime}
is really a simplicial object in
\mathcal{C}_{=0}
,
since
X^{\prime}_{n+1}
is a direct summand of
\Omega^{n+1}\cofib(X_{\leq n}\to X_{\leq n+1})\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}
.
Hence
f
is essentially surjective.

For
X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}
we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\map_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})}(``\colim_{m}"X_{m},``\colim_{n}"Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{m}\map_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})}(X_{m},``\colim_{n}"Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{m}\colim_{n}\map_{\mathcal{C}_{=0}}(X_{m},Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\map_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}}(\colim_{n}X_{m},\colim_{n}Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{m}\map_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}}(X_{m},\colim_{n}Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{m}\colim_{n}\map_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}}(X_{m},Y_{n})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

which reduces proving fully faithfulness of
f
to showing
that if
X\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}
, then the colimit interchange map

|\map_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}}(X,Y_{\bullet})|\to\map_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}}(X,|Y|)

is an equivalence.
Since
X
is weight coconnective and
|Y|
and all the
Y_{n}

are weight connective, we may identify the above mapping spaces
(which are canonically connective spectra by additivity
of
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
) with the corresponding mapping spectra in
\mathcal{C}
.
Then we note that
|Y|=\colim_{n}|Y|_{n}
is a sequential colimit growing in connectivity,
and that
\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,-)
preserves finite colimits and sequential colimits growing in connectivity
by Example
3.15
.

This proves that
f
is an equivalence.
But then, since this is true in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
,
this implies that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})
admits geometric realizations (since
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
does)
and for
X\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}

the functor
\hom_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})}(X,-)
preserves them.
From this it follows that the inclusion
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0})

preserves geometric realizations, and hence, by the universal property of
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}
,
that the inclusion is an equivalence.
The equivalence
\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{=0}))\simeq\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}

is then an instance of Lemma
2.6
.
∎

4.2.
Left completion of weak weight structures and consequences

Now that we have discussed the notion of left completeness,
the next goal will be to define and proof existence of a left completion.

Definition 4.8
.

A weight exact functor of weakly weighted categories

\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

exhibits
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
as the left completion of
\mathcal{C}

if
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
is left complete and
for any other left complete weakly weighted category
\mathcal{D}
,
precomposition by
\eta
induces an equivalence

(1)

\eta^{*}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow},\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}).

In other words, if
\eta
exhibits
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
as the left adjoint object to
\mathcal{C}

under the inclusion
\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{wWCat}_{=0}
.

To state the main theorem of this section,
let us mention that we will see in Proposition
4.25
below
that if
\mathcal{C}
is weakly weighted, then
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
always admits a weight structure
where the Yoneda embedding
j\colon\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\Ind(\mathcal{C})
is weight exact.

Theorem 4.9
.

Any weakly
\ell
-weighted category
\mathcal{C}
admits a weakly
\ell
-weighted left completion
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
.
This yields a left Bousfield localization
\smash{(-)}^{\uparrow}\colon\textsf{wWCat}_{=0}\to\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}_{=0}
such that

(1)

There exists a fully faithful weight exact functor
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\hookrightarrow\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

which lets us identify

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})\mid\forall n\geq 0\text{ there is $c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}$ and a map
$jc\to X$ with $n$-connective cofiber}\}

\displaystyle=\{``\colim_{n}"c_{n}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\text{ growing in connectivity}\}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})\mid X\text{ admits a $\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}$-weight complex}\}

which is the largest full stable subcategory of
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

in which
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is left dense,
and also the smallest full stable subcategory of
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

containing
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and closed under sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
Moreover, we have

\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq 0}=\{``\colim_{n}"c_{n}\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}\text{ growing in connectivity}\}.

(2)

The inclusion induces a weight exact equivalence
\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})}^{\uparrow}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
. Moreover:

(a)

The unit
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

is weight exact and the left Kan extension
of
\eta_{\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}}
along
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
.

(b)

\eta_{\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}}

agrees with the Yoneda embedding

j\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
, so is always fully faithful.
In fact, for
X\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
we have
\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}}(\eta X,\eta Y)
.

(c)

\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

is left dense,
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq 0}
is dense,
and we have
\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}=\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq 0}
.

(d)

\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
agrees with the Verdier projection

\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}
whenever
\mathcal{C}
already
admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity
and
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is closed under them.

(3)

For any
-\infty\leq a<\infty
and
a\leq b\leq\infty
,
restriction along
\eta
induces an equivalence

\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow},\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

with inverse given by left Kan extension along
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
.
In particular, if
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a functor in
wWCat
of amplitude
[a,b]
,
then
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}=(\eta_{\mathcal{C}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{D}}f)\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}

again has amplitude
[a,b]
.
Analogously for
[a,b)
instead of
[a,b]
.

(4)

In particular, left completion also defines a left adjoint
to the inclusions
\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\textsf{wWCat}_{\geq 0}

respectively
\smash{\textsf{wWCat}}^{\uparrow}\subseteq\textsf{wWCat}
.

Corollary 4.10
.

There is a “weight complex functor”

w=\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
so that

(1)

w
is weight exact and
restricts to the Yoneda embedding on
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.

(2)

if
X_{\bullet}
is any
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex
for
X
, then
wX\simeq``\colim_{n}"X_{n}
.

(3)

In particular,
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complexes are unique when viewed as
\Ind
-objects.

(4)

If
\mathcal{C}
is stably symmetric monoidal and the weak weight structure
is compatible with the monoidal structure
in the sense that both
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}

are symmetric monoidal subcategories, then
w\colon\mathcal{C}\to\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

is unital and lax symmetric monoidal,
and the structure map
wX\otimes wY\to w(X\otimes Y)
is an equivalence
when
X
and
Y
are either both bounded above or both bounded below.
This is an analogue of Aoki’s monoidal weight complex functor
[
2
]
.

Proof.

Points (1)-(3) are immediate consequences of the theorem.
For (4), we equip
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
with the canonical induced presentably symmetric monoidal structure
making the Yoneda embedding
j
symmetric monoidal.
Now since
i\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is symmetric monoidal,
also the left Kan extension
i_{!}\colon\textsf{Fun}(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty},\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}))\to\textsf{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}))
attains a symmetric monoidal structure
when we equip both source and target with the Day convolution monoidal structure
(for target category
An
this holds by symmetric monoidality of
\mathcal{P}(-)\colon\textsf{An}\to\textsf{Pr}^{L}

[
22
, Section 4.8.1]
, and then we can tensor in
\CAlg(\textsf{Pr}^{L})
with
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
).
Commutative algebras in the day convolution are equivalently lax symmetric monoidal functors

[
22
, Example 2.2.6.9]
,
hence
\eta=i_{!}j
is lax symmetric monoidal.
It restricts to
j
hence is symmetric monoidal on
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
If
X,Y
are bounded below with weight complexes
X_{\bullet}
respectively
Y_{\bullet}
,
then it is easy to check that also
(X_{n}\otimes Y_{n})_{n}
is a weight complex for
X\otimes Y
.
Thus
\colim_{n}w(X_{n}\otimes Y_{n})\simeq w(X\otimes Y)
, and the canonical structure map

wX\otimes wY\to w(X\otimes Y)
is the map induced on colimits by the equivalences

wX_{n}\otimes wY_{n}\xrightarrow{\simeq}w(X_{n}\otimes Y_{n})
.
∎

Corollary 4.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category. Then:

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{wb}\hookrightarrow(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{wb}

is an idempotent completion.
In particular, we recover a special
case of
[
8
, Theorem 0.1.(2)]
,
that bounded weight structures always extend to the idempotent completion.

(2)

\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{<\infty}=\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})\mid\text{ there is some bounded below $Y$ with }X\oplus Y\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\}
.

(3)

\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}=(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}

if and only if
\mathcal{C}_{wb}
is idempotent complete.
In particular, if
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is idempotent complete
then also
(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}
is.

Proof.

For (1), note that
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{wb}
is idempotent complete by Observation
4.3
,
so it remains to show that
\mathcal{C}_{wb}\hookrightarrow(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{wb}
is dense.
By Theorem
4.9
(2c)
the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
is left dense,
so
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{<\infty}
is dense.
Now let
X\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{[a,b]}
for some
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then there is some
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and a map
c\to X
with cofiber in
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq b+1}
.
It follows that
X
is a retract of
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq a}=\mathcal{C}_{[a,\infty)}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{wb}
, as desired.

To see (2), note that by density we have an inclusion
(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})^{\textsf{idem}}
.
Now suppose that
X\in(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq n}
.
We find
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and a map
jc\to X

with cofiber
C
in
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq n+1}
.
So with
Y=\Omega C
we get a splitting
jc\simeq X\oplus Y
.

Conversely, suppose that
X,Y\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

with
X\oplus Y\simeq jc
, where
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

and
Y
is bounded below. We show that then
Y\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
(so also

X\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
) using the explicit description of
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
from Theorem
4.9
(1).
We have the cofiber sequences
jc\to Y\to\Sigma X

and
\Sigma jc\to\Sigma X\to\Sigma^{2}Y
,
which together yield cofiber sequences

jd\to Y\to\Sigma^{2}Y
and
c\to d\to\Sigma c
so that
d\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
Continuing inductively, we obtain cofiber sequences

je_{n}\to Y\to\Sigma^{2n}Y
for some
e_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
Since
Y
was assumed bounded below, this shows
Y\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
.

Finally, let us show (3). If
\mathcal{C}_{wb}
is idempotent complete,
then combining (1) and (2) shows that for any
X\in(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}

there is some
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{wb}
for which
X\oplus Y\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
But then clearly also
X\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
Conversely, suppose that
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}=(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}

and let
X\in(\mathcal{C}_{wb})^{\textsf{idem}}\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})^{\textsf{idem}}
.
Then there is some
Y\in(\mathcal{C}_{wb})^{\textsf{idem}}
such that
X\oplus Y\in\mathcal{C}_{wb}
,
and hence
X\in(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}=\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
by (1).
∎

Remark 4.12
.

Even if
\mathcal{C}
is left complete,
the question of idempotent completeness of the entire
category
\mathcal{C}
is a lot more subtle.
We will see a concrete example of a left (and right!)
complete weighted category
whose non-idempotent-completeness we learned from
[
8
]

in Remark
5.8
below.
Their paper discusses the interaction of weight structures and idempotent completions, and their methods show that if
\mathcal{C}
is a left complete (weakly) weighted category, then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
is idempotent complete.

(2)

There exists a (weak) weight structure on
\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{idem}}
so that
the inclusion
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{idem}}
is weight exact.

(3)

\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{idem}}
is generated as a stable category by
(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})^{\textsf{idem}}
and
(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})^{\textsf{idem}}
(
=\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
).

Indeed, it is not hard to see a priori that if
\mathcal{C}
is left complete
and the weak weight structure extends to
\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{idem}}
,
then the latter is also left complete, by noting that
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{idem}}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
.
Moreover, by
[
25
,
Tag 03YQ
]

if
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is any category and
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
a dense full subcategory,
then any functor
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{E}
is both left and right Kan extended from
\mathcal{D}
.
In particular, since the definition of the left completion of a weakly weighted category
\mathcal{C}

is essentially “the maximal left complete weakly weighted category so that every map of bounded below amplitude
is left Kan extended from
\mathcal{C}
”,
this yields the equivalence of (1) and (2).
In the notation of
[
8
]
,
we always have
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}=\mathrm{Kar}^{w}_{\mathsf{max}}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
.
The equivalence with point (3) follows from
[
8
, Theorem 2.2.2(I.2)]
.

Corollary 4.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be weakly weighted
and
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
of amplitude
[a,\infty)
for
a\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(1)

\smash{f}^{\uparrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{>-\infty}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}_{>-\infty}

is fully faithful
(
an equivalence
)

exactly if
f^{\textsf{idem}}\colon(\mathcal{C}_{wb})^{\textsf{idem}}\to(\mathcal{D}_{wb})^{\textsf{idem}}
is.

(2)

\smash{f}^{\uparrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
is fully faithful
exactly if
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
is.

(3)

\smash{f}^{\uparrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
is an equivalence
exactly if
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
is a fully faithful left dense inclusion.

Here ‘equivalence’ means ‘equivalence of underlying categories’
as opposed to ‘equivalence in
wWCat
’.
12
12

12

Though in the saturated case this is implied
by Lemma
3.12
(3),
and for
\ell<\infty
it also follows from Lemma
3.14
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.9
also
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}
has amplitude
[a,\infty)
.
For (1), note that if
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{>-\infty}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}_{>-\infty}

is an equivalence, then also its restriction to weight bounded objects is an equivalence,
so by Corollary
4.11
(1) also
f^{\textsf{idem}}
is.
For the converse assume that
f^{\textsf{idem}}\colon(\mathcal{C}_{wb})^{\textsf{idem}}\to(\mathcal{D}_{wb})^{\textsf{idem}}

is an equivalence. By Observation
4.3
and Lemma
4.6
it suffices to show
that
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}
restricts to an equivalence on weight bounded objects.
We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{wb}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{wb}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}_{wb}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow})_{wb}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{f}^{\uparrow}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{D}}}

and by Corollary
4.11
(1)
the horizontal maps are idempotent completions,
which allows us to identify
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}|_{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{wb}}

with the equivalence
(f|_{\mathcal{C}_{wb}})^{\textsf{idem}}
.
This shows (1).

Point (2) is a consequence of Lemma
4.6
,
the natural fully faithful inclusions

\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{<\infty}\subseteq(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})^{\textsf{idem}}
,
cancellation properties of fully faithful functors,
and that
(-)^{\textsf{idem}}
preserves fully faithful functors.

For (3), suppose first that
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
is fully faithful left dense.
Since also
\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
is fully faithful left dense,
one easily checks that the composite
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
is left dense as well.
In particular, if
X\in\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
, it admits a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex
c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})_{/X}
, which converges to
X
in
\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
by left completeness.
Since
c_{\bullet}
is growing in connectivity, it admits a colimit in
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

and
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}
preserves this colimit.
This proves that
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}
is essentially surjective,
and fully faithfulness follows from (2).

For the converse, suppose that
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}
is an equivalence.
Then
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
is fully faithful.
Let
d\in\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
and
n\geq 0
.
There exists
X\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
with
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}(X)=\eta_{\mathcal{D}}(d)\in\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}
.
Now by left density of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
,
there is some
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and a map
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}c\to X

with cofiber in
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq n+a}
.
Hence
\eta_{\mathcal{D}}(fc)=\smash{f}^{\uparrow}(\eta_{\mathcal{C}}c)\to\smash{f}^{\uparrow}(X)=\eta_{\mathcal{D}}(d)
has cofiber in
\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq n}
.
By fully faithfulness of
\eta_{\mathcal{D}}
on bounded above objects,
we get that there is a map
fc\to d
in
\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
whose cofiber is in
\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}\cap\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\uparrow}_{\geq n}=\mathcal{D}_{[n,\infty)}
(cf. Theorem
4.9
(2c)).
This proves that
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
is also left dense.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be a weakly weighted category, and suppose that
\mathcal{D}
is left complete
and we have a fully faithful
weight exact
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}
.
Then the induced
F=(\eta_{\mathcal{C}})_{!}f\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\to\mathcal{D}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{<\infty}
is dense,
this follows from Lemma
4.6
(1).
∎

Remark 4.15
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be weakly
\ell
-co-weighted in the sense of Warning
3.2
,
so that
\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}}
is weakly
\ell
-weighted.
We say that
\mathcal{C}
is right complete if
\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}}
is left complete,
i.e. if its underlying coconnectivity structure
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})
is limit complete.
The results in this section
then give an analogous good theory of right (weight) completion
on the category
\textsf{wWCat}^{\prime}
of weakly co-weighted categories and exact functors
of bounded above amplitude.
For example, Theorem
4.9
(1)
dualizes to exhibit the right completion
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}

of some weakly co-weighted category
\mathcal{C}
as the full subcategory
of
\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
on those objects
X

where for each
n\geq 0
there exists some
c\in\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}

and a map
X\to c
with
(-n)
-coconnective fiber
(with respect to the weight structure induced by the dual of Proposition
4.25
).

We now show that the procedure of left completing weakly bi-weighted categories
(cf. Warning
3.2
) commutes with the dual procedure of right completion
(cf. Remark
4.15
).

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly bi-weighted category
(e.g.
\mathcal{C}
is weakly
\ell
-weighted for some
\ell<\infty
).

(1)

If
\mathcal{C}
is right
(
left
)
complete
then
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

(
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
)
is both left and right complete.

(2)

There is an equivalence
\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\downarrow}\simeq\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})}^{\uparrow}
in
(\textsf{wWCat}_{=0})_{\mathcal{C}/}
.

Proof.

We begin with (1).
Let
\mathcal{C}
be right complete, so that
\mathcal{C}_{\leq-\infty}=0
,

\mathcal{C}
admits sequential limits growing in coconnectivity
and
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is closed under such limits.
Then
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is also right complete, and in particular idempotent complete
by the dual of Corollary
2.31
.
Hence we get
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}=(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}

and in particular
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}=\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq 0}

by Corollary
4.11
(3).
Therefore
(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq-\infty}=\mathcal{C}_{\leq-\infty}=0
.

Now let
X_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}^{\textsf{op}}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
be a sequential diagram
growing in coconnectivity.
By cofinality we can assume that
\fib(X_{n+1}\to X_{n})\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq-(n+1)}
for all
n\geq 0
.
Now let
X=\lim_{n}X_{n}
computed in
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
,
and
k\geq 0
. We need to show that there exists a
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

and a map
c\to X
with
k
-connective cofiber.
There is a weight decomposition
X_{0,\leq b}\to X_{0}\to X_{0,\geq k}

for some
0\leq b<\infty
and an extension

\fib(X_{1}\to X_{0})\to\fib(X_{1}\to X_{0,\geq k})\to X_{0,\leq b}

which shows that also

X_{1,\leq b}\coloneqq\fib(X_{1}\to X_{0,\geq k})
lies in
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})_{\leq b}
.
Continuing inductively, we can build the sequential diagram of vertical fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{2,\leq b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{1,\leq b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0,\leq b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0,\geq k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0,\geq k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0,\geq k}}}

Note also that
\fib(X_{n+1,\leq b}\to X_{n,\leq b})=\fib(X_{n+1}\to X_{n})
,
so that the top row is also a sequential diagram growing in coconnectivity.
Since it also lives entirely in
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq b}=\mathcal{C}_{\leq b}

and the latter admits limits of such diagrams
(and the inclusion into
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
preserves them),
we see that passing to the limit yields a fiber sequence

X_{\leq b}\to X\to X_{0,\geq k}
with
X_{\leq b}\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq b}
,
as desired.
Thus
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
admits limits of sequential diagrams growing in coconnectivity.
Since
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\simeq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq 0}
also admits such limits
and the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
preserves limits
we deduce that
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
is still right complete.
Applying this result to
\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}}
,
we also see that if
\mathcal{C}
is left complete, then
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
is both left and right complete.

We now show (2).
Let
\mathcal{B}
be a (left and right) complete weakly (co-)weighted category.
Denote by
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
the left completion
and by
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
the right completion.
Then

\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})}^{\uparrow},\mathcal{B})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eta_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}}^{*}}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow},\mathcal{B})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})

and similarly
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\downarrow},\mathcal{B})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{B})
.
By Yoneda (applied to the full subcategory of
\textsf{wWCat}_{=0}
on the complete weakly (co-)weighted categories),
this yields an equivalence
\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\downarrow}
in
(\textsf{wWCat}_{=0})_{\mathcal{C}/}
.
∎

Notation 4.17
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is weakly bi-weighted we will simply write
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\updownarrow}\coloneqq\smash{(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\downarrow}
.

4.3.
The proof of Theorem
4.9

We now work towards the proof of Theorem
4.9
,
and begin by showing that functors
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
in
wWCat

where
\mathcal{D}
is left complete are uniquely determined by their restriction to a left dense subcategory.
It will be convenient to state this in the slightly larger generality of a colimit complete
connectivity structure
(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0})
as target.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category and
(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0})

a colimit complete stable connectivity structure.
Suppose that
i\colon\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is left dense
and equip
\mathcal{U}
with connectives
\mathcal{U}_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
For
a\in\mathbb{Z}
, Kan extension and restriction along
i
induce an equivalence

i_{!}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\geq a}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{D})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\geq a}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}i^{*}

where
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\geq a}\subseteq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}
is the full subcategory
of functors of amplitude
\geq a
, i.e. restricting to
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\to\mathcal{D}_{\geq a}
.

Proof.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{D}
be an exact functor of amplitude
\geq a
.
We begin by showing that
i_{!}f
exists.
Using the pointwise formula for left Kan extensions,
we need to show that for
X\in\mathcal{C}
, the colimit of the diagram
\mathcal{U}_{/X}\to\mathcal{U}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{D}
exists.
Pick a
\mathcal{U}
-weight complex
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}
for
X
.
This is colimit-cofinal by Lemma
3.18
,
so it suffices to check that the colimit
of the composite diagram
f(U_{\bullet})\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{D}
exists.
But by assumption on
f
this is again a sequential diagram growing in connectivity,
hence admits a colimit by left completeness of
\mathcal{D}
.
Thus the left Kan extension
i_{!}f
exists.

Now let
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
Picking a
\mathcal{U}
-weight complex
U_{\bullet}
for
X
as in Lemma
3.18
,
we see that after (without loss of generality) discarding the first term,
we have
U_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
Since by definition
\mathcal{U}_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
,
it follows that also
U_{n}\in\mathcal{U}_{\geq 0}
.
But then
(i_{!}f)(X)=\colim_{n}f(U_{n})
is a colimit of a sequential diagram
growing in connectivity in
\mathcal{D}_{\geq a}
.
By colimit completeness of
\mathcal{D}
the colimit is still
a
-connective.
Thus
i_{!}f
sends connectives to
a
-connectives,
so has amplitude
\geq a
again.

Hence
i_{!}
exists and is fully faithful since
i
is fully faithful.
Clearly
i^{*}
also exists and is right adjoint to
i_{!}
.
We now check that
i_{!}
is also essentially surjective.
Suppose that
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
has amplitude
\geq a
.
Then
i_{!}i^{*}f
exists and is of amplitude
\geq a
by the above,
and we have a counit
\varepsilon\colon i_{!}i^{*}f\Rightarrow f
.
For
X\in\mathcal{C}
with weight complex
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}
,
the above shows that
\varepsilon_{X}
is precisely map
\colim_{n}f(U_{n})\to f(X)
,
which is an equivalence by Lemma
2.21
.
∎

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
\mathcal{U},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be weakly weighted categories.
Suppose that

(1)

\mathcal{D}
is left complete.

(2)

There is a fully faithful weight exact inclusion
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

so that
i\colon\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is left dense.

(3)

\mathcal{U}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is dense.

(4)

\mathcal{U}_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
13
13

13

For example, by Lemma
3.12
this holds if
\mathcal{U}
is right saturated (e.g.
\ell=0
).

Then for
a\in\mathbb{Z}
and
a\leq b\leq\infty

the equivalence of Lemma
4.18
restricts to

i_{!}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{D})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}i^{*}

and similarly for
[a,b)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.18
,
it remains to check that if
f\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{D}
has amplitude
[-\infty,b]
,
then also
i_{!}f
does.
But by density of
\mathcal{U}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
any
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

is a retract of some
U\in\mathcal{U}_{\leq 0}
.
Hence
i_{!}f(X)
is a retract of
i_{!}f(U)=f(U)\in\mathcal{D}_{\leq b}
,
showing that
i_{!}f
has amplitude
[-\infty,b]
again.
The same argument works for
[-\infty,b)
.
∎

Corollary 4.20
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be a weakly weighted categories.
Suppose that

(1)

\mathcal{D}
is left complete.

(2)

There is a fully faithful weight exact
j\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}

which is left dense,

(3)

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}
is dense.

(4)

\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}=\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}

(e.g.
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is right saturated, e.g. weighted).

Then
j
and the left Kan extension
i_{!}j\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

of
j
along
i\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
are left completions.

Remark 4.21
.

In particular, if
\mathcal{C}
is left complete, then
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is a left completion.

Proof.

That
j
is a left completion is immediate from Proposition
4.19
.
Similarly, since
i\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
also satisfies these assumptions,
the same result shows that
i_{!}j\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is again weight exact,
and that for any left complete weakly weighted
\mathcal{E}

also
i^{*}
is an equivalence in the following commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty},\mathcal{E})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{!}j)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Thus by 2-out-of-3 we see that also
i_{!}j
has the correct universal property.
∎

Example 4.22
.

Consider
\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}
with its standard weight structure (restricted from
Sp
).
Applying Corollary
4.20
,
we recognize
\smash{(\textsf{Sp}^{\omega})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}
as the full subcategory of
Sp

consisting of those spectra which are bounded
below and have finitely generated homotopy groups.
We will see more examples in Sections
5
and
10
.

Lemma 4.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be weakly
\ell
-weighted and
\mathcal{D}
weakly weighted.
Suppose that:

(i)

\mathcal{D}
admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity and
\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
is closed under them.

(ii)

There is a fully faithful weight exact embedding
j\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}
.

Denote by
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\subseteq\mathcal{D}

the smallest full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}
which contains
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

and is closed under sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
Then:

(1)

If
c_{\bullet},d_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
are growing in connectivity
then any map
\colim c_{\bullet}\to\colim d_{\bullet}
in
\mathcal{D}

lifts to a map of diagrams
c_{\bullet}\Rightarrow d_{f(\bullet)}
for some
strictly increasing
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

As full subcategories of
\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle=\

\displaystyle\{\colim_{n}c_{n}\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\text{ growing in connectivity}\},

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}\coloneqq\

\displaystyle\{\colim_{n}c_{n}\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}\text{ growing in connectivity}\}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0},

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq\infty}=\

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}=\

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}.

Moreover,
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}

is closed under sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
In case that
\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}=\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}

(e.g. if
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is right saturated)
we have
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
.

(3)

\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is a stable subcategory of
\mathcal{D}

and with
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}\coloneqq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}
we have:

(a)

\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is a left complete weakly
\ell
-weighted category.

(b)

Both inclusions
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\subseteq\mathcal{D}
are weight exact, and the first is left dense.

(c)

If
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}

is dense, then both
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

and its Kan extension
\mathcal{C}\to\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

are left completions.

(4)

If
\mathcal{D}_{\geq\infty}=0
so that
\mathcal{D}
is left complete,
then
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is the largest stable subcategory
of
\mathcal{D}
in which
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is left dense, i.e.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle=\

\displaystyle\{X\in\mathcal{D}\mid\text{for $n\geq 0$ there is $c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}$ and a map
$c\to X$ with cofiber in $\mathcal{D}_{\geq n}$}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{X\in\mathcal{D}\mid X\text{ admits a $\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}$-weight complex}\}.

Proof.

(1)

Let us first show the addendum, that we can lift maps between colimits of such diagrams.
To this end, let
c_{\bullet},d_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
be sequential diagrams growing in connectivity
and define
c_{\infty}=\colim_{n}c_{n}
and similarly for
d_{\infty}
.
We show that given a map
c_{\infty}\to d_{\infty}
,
there is a strictly increasing functor
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}

and a natural transformation
c_{\bullet}\Rightarrow d_{f(\bullet)}
,
which on colimits induces the given map.
Recall from Example
3.15
that since
c_{0}\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}

we have
\colim_{n}\hom(c_{0},d_{n})\simeq\hom(c_{0},d_{\infty})
.
It follows that
c_{0}\to c_{\infty}\to d_{\infty}
factors through some
d_{f(0)}\to d_{\infty}
.
Now suppose by induction on
k\geq 0
that we have constructed a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{d_{f(0)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{d_{f(1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{d_{f(k)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{d_{\infty}}}

for some strictly increasing sequence
f(0),f(1),\dots,f(k)
.
We can then consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{k+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{d_{f(k)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{d_{\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where
P
is defined as the pushout of the left square, and thus lies in
\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
.
Hence the same argument as for
c_{0}
above shows that
P\to d_{\infty}

factors through some
d_{f(k+1)}\to d_{\infty}
.
By induction, this proves the claim that we can lift the given map
c_{\infty}\to d_{\infty}

to a map of diagrams.

(2)

For the first equality,
we prove that the right side already admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
So suppose that we have a sequential diagram growing in connectivity

c_{\infty}^{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{D}
where for each
n\geq 0
there is some sequential
diagram
c^{n}_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and an equivalence
c^{n}_{\infty}\simeq\colim_{k}c_{k}^{n}
.
We can first pass to a subsequence of
c_{\infty}^{\bullet}
to assume
that
\cofib(c_{\infty}^{n}\to c_{\infty}^{n+1})\in\mathcal{D}_{\geq n+1}
for all
n\geq 0
.
By the claim we just showed, we can moreover inductively pass to suitable subsequences
and in this way lift this to a diagram
c^{\bullet}_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

where also for each
n,k\geq 0
we have that
\cofib(c^{n}_{k}\to c^{n}_{k+1})\in\mathcal{C}_{[k+1,\infty)}
.
Since the diagonal
\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
is cofinal,
we see that
\colim_{n}c_{\infty}^{n}=\colim_{n}c_{n}^{n}
,
so it remains to check that
(c_{n}^{n})_{n}
is growing in connectivity.
Now we have an extension
\cofib(c^{n}_{n}\to c^{n+1}_{n})\to\cofib(c_{n}^{n}\to c_{n+1}^{n+1})\to\cofib(c^{n+1}_{n}\to c^{n+1}_{n+1})

where the last term lies in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+2}
, so it suffices to show that the first term
lies in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+1}
.
But we can form the diagram of cofiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{n}^{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{\infty}^{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{\geq n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c^{n+1}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c^{n+1}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B_{\geq n+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{\geq n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\geq n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C_{\geq n+2}}}

where
A_{\geq n+1},B_{\geq n+2}
and
D_{\geq n+1}
have indicated weight connectivity
by construction of the diagram
c_{\bullet}^{\bullet}
.
But then the claimed weight connectivity of
C_{\geq n+2}
and
E_{\geq n+1}
follows.

The inclusion
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}

is clear by the assumption on
\mathcal{D}
.
Let us show that the reverse inclusion also holds under the assumption
that
\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}=\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
.
Indeed, given
X\in\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
,
we can write
X=\colim_{n}c_{n}
for
c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

satisfying
\cofib(c_{n}\to c_{n+1})\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+1}
for all
n\geq 0
.
Note also that by the assumption on
\mathcal{D}
we have
X/c_{n}=\colim_{k\geq n}X/c_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{\geq n+1}
.
Now we have fiber sequences
c_{n}\to X\to X/c_{n}
where the middle term lies in
\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}

and the right term in
\mathcal{D}_{\geq 1}
, hence the left term also lies in
\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
.
Thus
c_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}
, as desired.

To see that
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq\infty}=0
,
suppose that
c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is growing in connectivity
and
c_{\infty}=\colim_{n}c_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{\geq\infty}
.
Then each map
c_{n}\to c_{\infty}
vanishes, which upon taking colimits
shows that
0\simeq\id_{c_{\infty}}
, and hence that
c_{\infty}=0
.

Next we show that
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\cap\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}
.
The inclusion
\supseteq
is clear,
so suppose that
c\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq b}
and there exists some
c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}

so that
\colim_{n}c_{n}=c
and
\cofib(c_{n}\to c_{n+1})\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+1}
for all
n\geq 0
.
Then
c/c_{b}\in\mathcal{D}_{\geq b+1}

so the map
c_{b}\to c
admits a retraction.
Since
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is a full subcategory,
c
is a retract of
c_{b}
in
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
,
and since
\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}
is closed under retracts, we deduce
c\in\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}
.

Finally, to see that
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}
is closed under sequential
colimits growing in connectivity,
we can use the same argument as in the proof of the first equality
to lift a sequential diagram growing in connectivity in
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}

to a
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
indexed diagram in
\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}
, and show that the resulting diagonal
is still growing in connectivity. Taking the colimit, we deduce the result from the assumption on
\mathcal{D}
.

(3)

To see that
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is a stable subcategory of
\mathcal{D}
,
note that the equivalent description from (2)
is closed under finite sums and shifts,
so it remains to check closure under cofibers.
Since we can lift maps on colimits to maps of diagrams by (1),
this reduces to checking that the (pointwise) cofiber of a map
c_{\bullet}\Rightarrow d_{\bullet}

of sequential diagrams growing in connectivity is itself still growing in connectivity,
but this is straightforward.
From this description of computing finite colimits
it is also clear that
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}
is closed under finite colimits and extensions.
Moreover, since it admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity,
it is idempotent complete by Corollary
2.31
, hence closed under retracts.
On the other hand,
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}
is closed under finite limits,
extensions, and retracts because
\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
is.
It is also clear that the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is weight exact.
The orthogonality follows from
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
.

Next, we provide a weight decomposition of
X\in\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
.
We can choose
c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}

with
\colim_{n}c_{n}=X
and
\cofib(c_{n}\to c_{n+1})\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+1}
for
n\geq 0
.
In particular, we get
X_{\geq 1}\coloneqq X/c_{0}=\colim_{n}c_{n}/c_{0}\in\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 1}
.
Pick a weight decomposition
(c_{0})_{<\ell+1}\to c_{0}\to(c_{0})_{\geq 1}

where
(c_{0})_{<\ell+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{<\ell+1}
and
(c_{0})_{\geq 1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1}
.
Then the cofiber of
(c_{0})_{<\ell+1}\to X
is an extension of
(c_{0})_{\geq 1}
and
X_{\geq 1}
,
hence still lies in
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle_{\geq 1}
.
This proves that
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
has weight decompositions of defect
\ell
.
The argument also shows that
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is left dense.

This proves that
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
is weakly
\ell
-weighted,
and by the results in (2) it follows that it is left complete, showing (a).
We have also already observed (b),
and hence (c) follows from Corollary
4.20

in view of the last equality of (2).

(4)

The two new proposed descriptions of
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
agree
since given an
X
in the first one, we can build a weight complex
as in Lemma
3.18
,
and admitting a
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
-weight complex also evidently implies lying in the first one.
Clearly every object in
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
admits a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex.
Conversely, if
X\in\mathcal{D}
admits a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex,
then it converges by left completeness of
\mathcal{D}
,
and hence
X\in\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
.
It is also clear that the first new description is the largest stable subcategory
of
\mathcal{D}
in which
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is left dense.

∎

In the case
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}
of the above Lemma there is more to be said.

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly
\ell
-weighted category
and suppose that
\mathcal{C}
admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity and
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is closed under them.
Let
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
be as in Lemma
4.23
(
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}
).
Then:

(1)

The full stable subcategories
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle,\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}
determine a semiorthogonal decomposition of
\mathcal{C}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where both
j
and
j^{R}
are weight exact
and commute with the inclusions of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.

(2)

Given a
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex

X_{\bullet}
of
X\in\mathcal{C}
we have an identification
of cofiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\colim_{n}X_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\colim_{n}X/X_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{jj^{R}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p^{R}pX}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{X}^{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{p}_{X}}

where
\varepsilon^{j}\colon jj^{R}\Rightarrow\id
is the counit
and
\eta^{p}\colon\id\Rightarrow p^{R}p
the unit.

(3)

j^{R}
agrees with the left Kan extension of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

along
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}

and exhibits
\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}\simeq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
as left completion of
\mathcal{C}
.

(4)

If
\mathcal{C}
is already left complete,
then
j
and
j^{R}
are mutually inverse weight exact equivalences.

Proof.

Clearly
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle\subseteq{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty})
.
Moreover, given any
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex
X_{\bullet}
of
X\in\mathcal{C}

there is a cofiber sequence as depicted
in the first row of (2). The left term lies in
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

and the right one in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}
by stability
of each
\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}
under sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
From this we automatically obtain the claimed semiorthogonal decomposition
and identification as in (2), see e.g.
[
23
, Section 7.2.1]
.

We already know from Lemma
4.23
that
j

is weight exact and commutes with the inclusions of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
But then the fact that
\id\simeq j^{R}j
shows that also
j^{R}

commutes with the inclusions of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
,
and hence that
j^{R}
preserves coconnectives.
Now let
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
Then by Lemma
3.18

we can choose the
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
-weight complex

X_{\bullet}
for
X
,
and after possibly discarding some terms, we may assume that
X_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
for all
n
.
This shows that
j^{R}X=\colim_{n}X_{n}
is again connective
in the weak weight structure on
\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

defined in Lemma
4.23
.
Thus
j^{R}
is also weight exact,
and is therefore left Kan extended from its restriction by Proposition
4.19
.
Since
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is dense in itself, the rest of (3)
follows from Lemma
4.23
(3c).
Also (4) is clear by Lemma
4.23
(4).
∎

Proposition 4.25
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly
\ell
-weighted category.
There is a weight structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})

so that the Yoneda-embedding
j\colon\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\Ind(\mathcal{C})
is weight exact. Moreover:

(1)

The weight structure is compactly generated by
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

in the sense of Definition
6.1
. Concretely:

(a)

X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})
is weight connective
if and only if
\hom(c_{\leq 0},X)

is connective for all
c_{\leq 0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.

(b)

\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
is the smallest class
containing
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
and closed under
finite limits, extensions, retracts,
sums, and sequential colimits of maps whose
cofiber is already in the class.

(2)

\Ind(\mathcal{C})
admits an adjacent
t
-structure
(i.e.
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{t\geq 0}=\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{w\geq 0}
).
In particular,
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{w\geq 0}
is closed under all colimits.

(3)

We have
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq-\ell})
.
14
14

14

For
\ell=\infty
, the second inclusion is vacuous. If
\mathcal{C}
is also a weakly co-weighted category
(cf. Warning
3.2
), i.e. we also have weight decompositions

X_{\leq 0}\to X\to X_{>-\infty}
,
then the same proof shows that
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
.

In particular, for a weight structure on
\mathcal{C}
(i.e. for
\ell=0
) we have equality.

(4)

If
\ell=0
,
then the weight heart
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{w=0}

coincides with the idempotent completion
of
(\mathcal{C}_{=0})^{\oplus}
, the full subcategory on sums of objects in
\mathcal{C}_{=0}
.

(5)

There exists a stable recollement

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{RR}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{R}}

where
j_{!}
is induced by the inclusion via
\Ind(-)
,
and
j_{!},j^{*}
are weight exact and
j_{*}
has amplitude
[0,\infty]
,
and
j^{*}
is a left completion.
Both the weight structure and adjacent
t
-structure
on
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
are left complete.

(6)

If
\mathcal{D}
is another weakly weighted category
and
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
a map of amplitude
[a,b)

(
[a,b]
)

for
-\infty\leq a\leq b\leq\infty
,
then
f_{!}=\Ind(f)\colon\Ind(\mathcal{C})\to\Ind(\mathcal{D})

is of weight amplitude
[a-\ell,b)

(
[a-\ell,b]
)
.

Proof.

To show (1), we could just cite Theorem
6.3

(which in this specific case was well known before,
cf. Remark
6.4
),
though we find it instructive to give the short proof here:
The only nontrivial part is to check the existence of weight decompositions,
which are constructed via a small object argument.
Given
X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})
, define
X^{\prime}
via the cofiber sequence

\bigoplus_{c\in(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})_{/X}}c\to X\to X^{\prime}
.
Now let
X_{0}=X
and define
X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
as
X_{n}\to(X_{n})^{\prime}
.
The weight decomposition is then
\colim_{n}F_{n}\to X\to\colim_{n}X_{n}

where inductively each
F_{n}=\fib(X\to X_{n})

and thus also
\colim_{n}F_{n}

is weight coconnective (since
\cofib(F_{n}\to F_{n+1})
is a coproduct
of objects in
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
).
On the other hand, clearly
\pi_{0}\hom(c,\colim_{n}X_{n})=0

for
c\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
, and since
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is closed under
\Omega

we see
\colim_{n}X_{n}
is 1-connective, as desired.

For (2), we note that by (1a) the subcategory
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{w\geq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C})

is closed under colimits and extensions, so the claim follows from
[
22
, Proposition 1.4.4.11]
.

To see (3), note that clearly
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}

since Yoneda is weight exact and the latter is closed under colimits.
Now let
X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
.
The canonical map
\colim_{c\in\mathcal{C}_{/X}}c\to X
is an equivalence
by compact generation. Note also that
\mathcal{C}_{/X}
is filtered since
it admits finite colimits.
The full subcategory
(\mathcal{C}_{\geq-\ell})_{/X}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{/X}

is still filtered for the same reason,
and for every map
c\to X
,
we can pick a weight decomposition
c_{\leq-1}\to c\to c_{\geq-\ell}

and note that
c_{\leq-1}\to X
vanishes, so that
c\to X

admits a map to
c_{\geq-\ell}\to X
in
\mathcal{C}_{/X}
.
Thus the inclusion of filtered categories
(\mathcal{C}_{\geq-\ell})_{/X}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{/X}

is weakly cofinal in the sense of
[
25
,
Tag 06CK
]
,
and thus
[
25
,
Tag 06CP
]
shows that it is already cofinal.
Thus
\colim_{c\in(\mathcal{C}_{\geq-\ell})_{/X}}c\simeq X
, which proves (3).

For (4), suppose that
\ell=0
and
X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{w=0}

and consider the map
f\colon\bigoplus_{c\in(\mathcal{C}_{=0})_{/X}}c\to X
.
It suffices to check that the cofiber of
f
is 1-connective,
since then
f
admits a retraction.
We can check this by mapping in from
d\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.
But
d\to\Sigma c
vanishes, so any such map
d\to\cofib(f)

factors through
d\to X
. Picking a weight decomposition

d_{\leq-1}\to d\to d_{=0}
, we get that
d\to X

actually factors through
d\to d_{=0}\to X
.
But then by definition of
f
the map
d_{=0}\to X

factors through
f
, and hence the entire composite

d\to\cofib(f)
vanishes. This shows
\cofib(f)\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 1}

so that
f
admits a retraction, as desired.

For point (5) we want to apply Lemma
4.24

to the weighted category
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
.
To this end, we first need to argue that
\langle\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\rangle=\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

in the notation of Lemma
4.23
.
It suffices to show that
\langle\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\rangle
is closed under all colimits,
since it contains
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.
As it is a stable subcategory, it suffices to consider coproducts.
Let
X^{i}\in\langle\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\rangle
for
i\in I
.
Since
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}
is left dense and we are in the
\ell=0
case,
we can use Example
3.19

to find
X^{i}_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}
with
\colim_{n}X^{i}_{n}\simeq X^{i}

and
X^{i}_{n}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq n}
for all
n
.
Since
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
is saturated, each
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq n}
is closed under arbitrary sums,
and we see that
\bigoplus_{i}X^{i}_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}

is still a well defined sequential diagram growing in connectivity.
Clearly its colimit is
\bigoplus_{i}X^{i}
, so this proves that
\langle\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\rangle=\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
.

Let us also note that
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})_{\leq 0}=\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
,
which agrees with the definition of
\langle\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}
of Lemma
4.23
.
But then the weight structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
defined here
necessarily agrees with the weight structure on
\langle\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\rangle

defined in Lemma
4.23
, since both are saturated
and the coconnectives agree.

Now
p^{RR}
exists for formal reasons;

j_{!}
is a functor of compactly generated stable categories
that preserves colimits and compact objects,
hence admits adjoints
j_{!}\dashv j^{*}\dashv j_{*}
,
