and thus the functor
p
admits analogous adjoints
so that each row fits into a Verdier sequence,
see e.g.
[
13
, Corollary A.2.10]
.
Moreover, it follows from Lemma
3.12
that
j_{*}
preserves connectives.

Finally, for (6), note that
f_{!}
preserves the operations
under which
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
generates
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}

and restricts to
f
on
\mathcal{C}
.
From this we see that if
f
has amplitude
[-\infty,b)
, then also
f_{!}
has.
In the case
\ell=\infty
, there is now nothing left to do,
so let
\ell<\infty

and suppose that
f
has amplitude
[a,\infty]
for some
a\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then by (3) the restriction of
f_{!}
to
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
factors as

\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq-\ell})\to\Ind(\mathcal{D}_{\geq a-\ell})\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{D})_{\geq a-\ell}
.
Thus
f_{!}
has amplitude
[a-\ell,\infty]
.
∎

Remark 4.26
.

It is also easy to see directly that the above weight structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
is left complete.
By (2) it only remains to observe that if
X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})_{\geq\infty}

then any map
c\to X
vanishes for
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
, hence
X=0
.

With this, we have all the necessary ingredients to prove Theorem
4.9
.

Proof of Theorem
4.9
.

Let us begin by showing that every weakly weighted category
\mathcal{C}

admits a left completion.
To this end, we apply Lemma
4.23

to the Yoneda embedding
j\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

and let
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\coloneqq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle
in the notation of said lemma.
We need to show that
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq 0}
is dense.
This is a consequence of the special description we have for
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}

from Proposition
4.25
(1b).
Indeed, by left density of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
,
it follows that a given
X\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}_{\leq 0}

is a retract of some
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and hence compact in
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
.
Now
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
is generated by
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

under finite limits, extensions, retracts,
sums and certain sequential colimits.
In particular, since
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is already closed under finite limits and extensions,
this means that
X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
.
Since this inclusion preserves filtered colimits and
X
is compact in
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})

it is also compact in
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})
, and hence
X\in(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})^{\textsf{idem}}
,
as desired.

Thus Lemma
4.23
(3c) now shows that the (codomain-)restricted
Yoneda embedding
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

as well as its left Kan extension
\mathcal{C}\to\langle\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\rangle

are left completions.
Now the points (1), (2a), and (2c) follow from the Lemma,
and (2d) is just Lemma
4.24
.

The fully faithfulness in (2b) is also clear.
For the rest of (2b),
consider
X\in\mathcal{C}_{w\leq n}
and
Y\in\mathcal{C}

with
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
-weight complex
Y_{\bullet}
.
We have the following commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\colim_{n}\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\colim_{n}\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}}(\eta X,\eta Y_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}}(\eta X,\eta Y)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Here the left vertical map is an equivalence by fully faithfulness
of
\eta
on
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
,
and the bottom map is an equivalence by definition of
\eta

and since
\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}}(\eta X,-)
has bounded below weight amplitude by Example
3.15
.
The cofiber of the top map is given by
\colim_{n}\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y/Y_{n})
,
which lies in
\textsf{Sp}_{w\geq\infty}=0
by another application
of Example
3.15
.
Thus also the top map and hence the right map is an equivalence,
proving the rest of (2b).

To see (3), note that as in the proof of Corollary
4.20
we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow},\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty},\mathcal{E})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\eta_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
i^{*}
and
j^{*}
are equivalences due to Proposition
4.19
,
and similarly for
[a,b)
in place of
[a,b]
.
Since Kan extensions compose, also the inverse
of
(\eta_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}
is given by left Kan extension along
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
.
Finally, (4) is an immediate consequence of (3).
∎

5.
Generating complete weight structures from their hearts

In this section, we see what the theory of completions of (weak) weight structures
can say about one of the motivating examples of a weight structure,
the category of chain complexes in an ordinary additive category modulo chain homotopy equivalences
(or more specifically, its
\infty
-categorical incarnation).
Concretely, we will show that this category can be obtained as the (left and right) weight completion
of the category of bounded chain complexes of
\mathcal{A}
with its canonical weight structure.
We will also give an explicit description of the (unique!) left complete and right bounded
weight structure with a given heart.

To begin, let us recall the following result of Sosnilo, which tells us how to generate
bounded weight structures from their hearts.
Given an additive category
\mathcal{A}
, denote by
\Stab(\mathcal{A})
the stable envelope of
\mathcal{A}
,
which is the smallest full stable subcategory of
\textsf{Fun}^{\times}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}},\textsf{Sp})

containing the image of the Yoneda embedding.
This assembles into a left adjoint
\Stab(-)\colon\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}\to\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{ex}}

of the forgetful functor from
\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{ex}}\to\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}
.
Recall also that
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{widem}}
denotes the weak idempotent completion (cf. the notational conventions).

Theorem 5.1

(
[
34
, Prop. 3.3 and Cor. 3.4]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category.

(1)

\Stab(\mathcal{A})
admits a bounded weight structure
whose weight heart identifies with
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{widem}}
,
the weak idempotent completion of
\mathcal{A}
.

(2)

Let
\textsf{WCat}_{=0}
be the category of weighted categories and weight exact functors.
The above construction upgrades to an adjunction

\Stab(-)\colon\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}\rightleftarrows\textsf{WCat}_{=0}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}(-)_{=0}

where the right adjoint
(-)_{=0}
associates to a weighted category its weight heart.

(3)

Let
\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{WCat}_{=0}
be the full subcategory on bounded weighted categories.
Then the functor
(-)_{=0}\colon\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}\to\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}
is fully faithful
with essential image consisting of the weakly idempotent complete additive categories,
so the above adjunction restricts to a left Bousfield localization

\Stab(-)\colon\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}\rightleftarrows\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}(-)_{=0}
.

(4)

In particular, restricting to bounded idempotent complete weighted categories

\textsf{WCat}^{b,\textsf{perf}}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}

and idempotent complete additive categories
\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}\subseteq\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}

the above adjunction becomes an equivalence
\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}\simeq\textsf{WCat}^{b,\textsf{perf}}_{=0}
.

We obtain the following as a corollary of Proposition
4.7
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category. Then:

(1)

\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})
is idempotent complete,
and the map
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}})
is an equivalence.
The full subcategory
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}\subseteq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})

consists precisely of the projective objects,
i.e. those
X\in\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})
where
\map(X,-)\colon\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})\to\textsf{An}

preserves geometric realizations.

(2)

\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})
is prestable,
with
\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))\simeq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\uparrow}
.
In particular,
\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))

always admits a left complete weight structure with heart
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
.

(3)

Every object in
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})

is a geometric realization of a simplicial object that is levelwise projective
i.e. a simplicial object in
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
.

Proof.

Here (1) is a consequence of Lemma
2.30
.
By Observation
4.3
and Theorem
5.1

we have
(\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\uparrow})_{w=0}=\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
,
so Proposition
4.7
and (1)
yield
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})\simeq(\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\uparrow})_{w\geq 0}
,
which gives (2) by applying
\SW(-)
, see Lemma
2.6
(4).
Also (3) is a direct consequence of Proposition
4.7
.
∎

Definition 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category. We will denote by

K(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\updownarrow}\simeq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))}^{\downarrow}

the (left and right) weight completion of
\Stab(\mathcal{A})

(by Proposition
4.16
the order does not matter).
Note also that
K(\mathcal{A})_{wb}=\Stab(\mathcal{A})^{\textsf{idem}}=\Stab(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}})
and

K(\mathcal{A})_{=0}=\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
by Corollary
4.11
and its dual.

We will see below that the terminology / notation of
K(\mathcal{A})
is justified
by comparing it to the classical case of chain complexes in an ordinary additive category up to chain homotopy.
For now, we deduce the following result from our theory.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\textsf{WCat}^{b,\textsf{perf}}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{WCat}_{=0}
be as in Theorem
5.1
.
Consider also the full subcategory
\textsf{WCat}^{\updownarrow}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{WCat}_{=0}

on (left and right) complete weighted categories.

(1)

We have adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{WCat}_{=0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{WCat}_{=0}^{\updownarrow}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Stab(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inc_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{wb}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inc_{\updownarrow}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

(2)

By composing and restricting we obtain an adjoint equivalence

\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}\colon\textsf{WCat}^{b,\textsf{perf}}_{=0}\simeq\textsf{WCat}_{=0}^{\updownarrow}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}(-)_{wb}.

In particular, composing further with Theorem
5.1
(4),
we also obtain

K(-)\colon\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}\simeq\textsf{WCat}_{=0}^{\updownarrow}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}(-)_{=0}

(3)

The composite functor
(-)^{\textsf{idem}}\circ(-)_{=0}\colon\textsf{WCat}_{=0}\to\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}

admits the fully faithful right adjoint
K(-)\colon\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}\hookrightarrow\textsf{WCat}_{=0}
.
In particular, we can think of
K(\mathcal{A})
as the terminal weighted category
whose weight heart is
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
.

Proof.

For (1) the left adjunction is Theorem
5.1
,
the middle one is obvious, and the right one follows from Theorem
4.9
.

For (2), we simply note that the unit and counit maps are equivalences.
Indeed, the unit map at some
\mathcal{C}\in\textsf{WCat}^{b}_{=0}
is given by
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\updownarrow})_{wb}

which is adjoint to the canonical map
\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\updownarrow}
using that
\mathcal{C}
is bounded.
By the fact that
\mathcal{C}
is already idempotent complete,
it follows from Corollary
4.11
(1) and its dual
that
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
is an equivalence.
Similarly, the counit map at some
\mathcal{D}\in\textsf{WCat}_{=0}^{\updownarrow}

is given by
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\smash{(\mathcal{D}_{wb})}^{\updownarrow}\to\mathcal{D}
which is adjoint to the inclusion
\mathcal{D}_{wb}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
.
In particular, we know that
(\varepsilon_{\mathcal{D}})_{wb}
is an equivalence,
so it follows from Lemma
4.6
and its dual that
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
is an equivalence.

For (3), note that under the equivalence
\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}\simeq\textsf{WCat}^{\updownarrow}_{=0}

from (2), the functor
K\colon\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}\to\textsf{WCat}_{=0}

identifies with the right adjoint
\inc_{\updownarrow}\colon\textsf{WCat}^{\updownarrow}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{WCat}_{=0}
.
The left adjoint is then the top right composite in the following square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{WCat}_{=0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{WCat}^{\updownarrow}_{=0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{add},\textsf{idem}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{=0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\textsf{idem}}}

so it remains to see that the square commutes.
In other words, we need to construct a natural transformation
(-)_{=0}\Rightarrow(-)_{=0}\circ\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}

and show it is an idempotent completion.
The natural transformation
(-)_{wb}\Rightarrow\id_{\textsf{WCat}_{=0}}
yields an equivalence

\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}\circ(-)_{wb}\simeq\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}
by using Corollary
4.13
and its dual.
But now the unit yields a natural map
(-)_{wb}\Rightarrow\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}\circ(-)_{wb}\simeq\smash{(-)}^{\updownarrow}

which is an idempotent completion by Corollary
4.11
.
Postcomposing with
(-)_{=0}
then yields the claim.
∎

Let us now discuss the example of chain complexes in more detail
and show that the above notation matches up with the classical
meaning of
K(\mathcal{A})
for an ordinary additive category.
For an ordinary additive category
\mathcal{A}

we let
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq N_{dg}(\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A}))

be the dg-nerve (in the sense of
[
22
, Section 1.3.1]
)
of the ordinary category of chain complexes in
\mathcal{A}
,
see also
[
22
, Remark 1.3.2.2]
.
This is stable by
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.2.10]
,
and its homotopy category is the classical triangulated category
K(\mathcal{A})
,
whose objects are chain complexes in
\mathcal{A}
up to chain homotopy equivalence,
and whose morphisms are chain homotopy classes of chain maps.
As mentioned in the paragraph above
[
22
, Corollary 1.3.2.18]
,
any full subcategory of
\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
closed under shifts and formations of mapping cones
defines a (full) stable subcategory of
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.
In particular, the categories
\textsf{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}^{-}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}^{+}(\mathcal{A})
of bounded, bounded below, and bounded above
chain complexes determine full stable subcategories
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}^{-}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}^{+}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.

One of the motivating examples of weight structures in
[
9
]

(see also
[
32
, Proposition 4.6]
)
is the weight structure on
K(\mathcal{A})
where
K(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0}
consists of those chain complexes chain homotopy equivalent to ones concentrated in non-negative degrees, and similarly for
K(\mathcal{A})_{w\leq 0}
.
The weight decompositions are achieved by the “stupid” / “naive” truncations.
Of course, this also yields a weight structure on
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
,
which turns out to restrict to
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
,
and yield a weight exact equivalence
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\Stab(\mathcal{A})
.
For the convenience of the reader, we will reprove the existence
of this weight structure on the way to the following theorem.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an ordinary additive category.
There is a weight structure on
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
so that

(1)

\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0}
is the full subcategory on those objects equivalent
to chain complexes concentrated in non-negative degrees,
and dually for
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\leq 0}
.
The “naive” truncations of a chain complex
provide a weight decomposition.
The weight heart is
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
.

(2)

There is a weight exact equivalence
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}})\simeq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})^{\textsf{op}}
.

(3)

The weight structure is both left and right complete.

(4)

The weight structure restricts to a bounded weight structure on
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

with heart
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{widem}}
, so there is a weight exact equivalence
\Stab(\mathcal{A})\simeq\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.
We have
\textsf{Ch}^{-}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})=\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w>-\infty}
,
so
\textsf{Ch}^{-}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
admits a left complete and right bounded weight structure with weight
heart
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
, and dually for
\textsf{Ch}^{+}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.

(5)

We have a weight exact equivalence

K(\mathcal{A})\simeq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}),

which justifies the notation
K(\mathcal{A})
.

(6)

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a (left and right) complete weighted category.
If
\mathcal{C}_{w=0}
is an ordinary category,
then there exists a weight exact equivalence
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})\simeq\mathcal{C}
.

Warning 5.6
.

We always have an inclusion
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb}

of the dg-nerve of the category of bounded chain complexes
into the weight-bounded objects in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.
This is generally
not
an equivalence
(note that definitionally
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{[-n,n]}
contains objects
X\in\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

which can be represented by a
(-n)
-connective complex and also represented by an
n
-coconnective
complex in
\mathcal{A}
, but not necessarily by a simultaneously
(-n)
-connective and
n
-coconnective
complex). This comes down to an idempotent completeness issue.

As a consequence, we recover a Theorem of Schnürer
15
15

15

This is stated in the language of triangulated categories, but if
\mathcal{A}
is an additive
\infty
-category, then it is idempotent complete if and only if its homotopy category is idempotent complete, see e.g.
[
35
, Proposition 2.3.2]
.
on the idempotent completeness of bounded below / above chain complexes.

Corollary 5.7

(
[
32
, Theorem 1.2]
)
.

The categories
\textsf{Ch}^{-}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})=\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w>-\infty}
and
\textsf{Ch}^{+}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})=\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w<\infty}

are idempotent complete. In particular, they are closed under retracts in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

By the above
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{\geq 0}
admits finite colimits and sequential colimits
growing in connectivity, so that it is idempotent complete by Corollary
2.31
.
By shifting we deduce that
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}
is idempotent complete,
and dually
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{<\infty}
is idempotent complete.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

Coming back to Warning
5.6
,
the above corollary of course also implies that each
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w[-n,n]}

and hence
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w[-n,n]}
are idempotent complete.
On the other hand, the dg-nerve
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
of the category of bounded chain complexes in
\mathcal{A}

is generally not idempotent complete, see
[
32
, Remark 3.2]
for a concrete example.
However, the inclusion
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\subseteq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb}
is always an idempotent completion.
Moreover, as detailed in
[
8
, Section 3.1]
,
any commutative ring
R
which has
K_{-1}(R)\neq 0

yields an example of an ordinary additive category
\mathcal{A}=\Proj^{\textsf{fg}}(R)

for which
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
is not idempotent complete.
See also the discussion at Remark
4.12
.

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian category with enough projective objects.
Let
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}^{-}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})
be the bounded-below
derived category of
\mathcal{A}
as defined in
[
22
, Definition 1.3.2.7]
.
Then
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
admits a weight structure such that

(1)

X\in\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
is weight connective
if and only if it can be represented
by a connective complex of projectives in
\mathcal{A}
.
Equivalently, if
H_{n}(X)=0
for
n<0
.
In particular, the weight structure is adjacent
to the left complete
t
-structure of
[
22
, Prop. 1.3.2.19, 1.3.3.16]
.

(2)

X\in\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
is weight coconnective
if and only if it can be represented
by a complex of projectives concentrated
in non-positive (though bounded below) degrees.

(3)

\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})_{w=0}=\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}}
.

(4)

The weight structure is left complete and right bounded.

(5)

It follows that

\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})_{\geq 0}\simeq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})

and we have weight exact equivalences

\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})).

In particular, this recovers the universal property
of
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
shown in
[
22
, Theorem 1.3.3.8]

(see also
[
22
, Lemma 1.3.3.17]
).

Proof.

We take the weight structure provided to us by Theorem
5.5
,
which makes (2) and (4) clear (note that (4)
also follows from (1) via Lemma
2.25
).
Since abelian categories are idempotent complete
and projectives are closed under retracts, (3) follows.
For (1) it remains to see the homological characterization
of weight connectives, which was shown in the proof
of
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.2.19]
.
Finally (5) follows from Proposition
5.2
.
∎

We will also discuss the unbounded derived category of an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
in Section
9
below.
For now, we work towards proving Theorem
5.5

and begin with the naive truncations.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an ordinary additive category
and
X\in\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
be a chain complex in
\mathcal{A}
.
For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, the “naive truncations” yield a short exact sequence
of chain complexes
\tau_{\leq k}X\to X\to\tau_{\geq k+1}X

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{k+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

This is a cofiber sequence in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.
16
16

16

Note that this is not a
split
short exact sequence, in which case the claim would be trivial.

Warning 5.11
.

Despite the notation, the naive truncations are
not
functorial on
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

By
[
22
, Remark 1.3.2.17]
, the cofiber of the map
f\colon\tau_{\leq k}X\to X
in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

is computed by the mapping cone
C(f)
.
It thus suffices to show that there is a chain homotopy equivalence from
C(f)
to
\tau_{\geq k+1}X
.
This follows from the above sequence of chain complexes being termwise split exact,
see e.g.
[
36
,
Tag 014L
]
.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

Note that the classical equivalence
\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}})\simeq\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\textsf{op}}

induces an equivalence
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}})\simeq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})^{\textsf{op}}

which restricts to
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}})_{w\geq 0}\simeq(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\leq 0})^{\textsf{op}}
.
In other words, endowing
\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\textsf{op}}
with the evident dual (co)connectivity structures,
the equivalence
\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}})\simeq\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\textsf{op}}

has (co)connectivity amplitude
0
.

Lemma 5.13
.

The connectivity structure
(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0})
is colimit complete,
and for any complex
X_{*}
the naive truncations
determine a sequential cone
\tau_{\leq\bullet}X_{*}
over
X

which is growing in connectivity and also a colimit cone.
By the duality of Remark
5.12
, it follows that
(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\leq 0})

is limit complete.

Proof.

That the sequential cone
\tau_{\leq\bullet}X_{*}
over
X_{*}
is growing in connectivity
follows from Lemma
5.10
.
To see that it is a colimit cone, we verify the universal property.
Recall from
[
22
, Construction 1.3.1.13, Proposition 1.3.1.17]

that the mapping anima in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
between two complexes
A_{*},B_{*}

in
\mathcal{A}
is computed as the underlying anima of the object in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})
represented by
the
\textsf{Ch}(\textsf{Ab})
-enriched hom
\underline{\hom}_{\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})}(A_{\bullet},B_{\bullet})

whose definition is recalled in
[
22
, Definition 1.3.2.1]
.
Important for us will only be that the
p
-th term of this complex
is given by
\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{n},B_{n+p})
for
p\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Namely, given any
A_{*}\in\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
,
the above cone yields a (strict) limit cone

\underline{\hom}_{\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})}(X_{*},A_{*})\xrightarrow{\cong}\underline{\hom}_{\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})}(\tau_{\leq\bullet}X_{*},A_{*})
in
\textsf{Ch}(\mathbb{Z})
.
We claim that this also presents the limit in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Recall that there is a model structure on
\textsf{Ch}(\mathbb{Z})
(constructed e.g. in
[
20
, Theorem 2.3.11]
)
whose fibrations are the levelwise surjections and whose weak equivalences are the quasi-isomorphisms
so that the model structure presents
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Now we see that every map
\underline{\hom}_{\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})}(\tau_{\leq n+1}X_{*},A_{*})\to\underline{\hom}_{\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})}(\tau_{\leq n}X_{*},A_{*})
is levelwise just projecting away one factor.
Thus our inverse sequential diagram in
\textsf{Ch}(\mathbb{Z})
consists entirely of fibrations in this model structure, and is thus fibrant in the injective model structure for inverse sequential diagrams in
\textsf{Ch}(\mathbb{Z})
(whose weak equivalences and cofibrations are pointwise).
We conclude that the strict limit indeed models the limit in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Overall, since the forgetful functor
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})\to\textsf{An}
also preserves limits,
this verifies that
\tau_{\leq n}X_{*}\Rightarrow\const X_{*}

is a colimit cone in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.

From this it is easy to see that any
X_{*}\in\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq\infty}

has to be chain-homotopy equivalent to
0
.
Indeed, given any other chain complex
A_{*}
,
we note that
\hom_{\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}}(\tau_{\leq n}A_{*},X_{*})
is
k
-connective for all
k\geq 0

since
X_{*}
is equivalent to an
(n+k)
-connective complex for all
k
.
Hence the hom spectrum vanishes, and by taking the limit also
\hom(A_{*},X_{*})=0
,
which shows
X_{*}=0
.

Now let
X^{0}\to X^{1}\to X^{2}\to\cdots
be a sequential diagram growing in connectivity in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
,
where by cofinality we may assume that
\cofib(X^{n}\to X^{n+1})\in\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq n+1}

for all
n\geq 0
.
We claim that we can inductively choose representatives
X^{n}_{*}
of
X^{n}

and of the maps so that for all
n\geq 0
and
k\leq n
the map
X^{n}_{k}\to X^{n+1}_{k}
is the identity.
Indeed, begin by choosing any representative
X^{0}_{*}
of
X_{0}
.
Having chosen representatives for the objects and maps up to
X^{n}_{*}
,
we note that we have a cofiber sequence
F\to X^{n}_{*}\to X^{n+1}

with
F\in\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq n}
,
so we can pick a model
F_{*}
for
F
with
F_{k}=0
for
k<n
,
as well as any chain map
F_{*}\to X^{n}_{*}
of the right chain homotopy class.
We have
X^{n+1}\simeq\cofib(f)
, but the cofiber of
f
in
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

can be computed by a mapping cone construction.
Thus the explicit formula for the mapping cone yields the desired representative
X^{n+1}_{*}\coloneqq C(f)
.

We now define the complex
X^{\infty}_{*}
by
\tau_{\leq n}X^{\infty}_{*}=\tau_{\leq n}X^{n}_{*}

and compute for any
A_{*}
that

\lim_{n}\hom(X_{*}^{n},A_{*})\simeq\lim_{n,k}\hom(\tau_{\leq k}X_{*}^{n},A_{*})\simeq\lim_{n}\hom(\tau_{\leq n}X_{*}^{n},A_{*})\simeq\lim_{n}\hom(\tau_{\leq n}X^{\infty}_{*},A_{*})\simeq\hom(X^{\infty}_{*},A_{*})

which proves
\colim_{n}X^{n}_{*}\simeq X^{\infty}_{*}
.
Thus
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity,
and it is clear from the inductive construction of the
X^{n}_{*}
and
X^{\infty}_{*}
that

\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0}
is closed under such colimits.
This concludes the proof that
(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}),\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0})
is colimit complete.
∎

With this, we can now prove Theorem
5.5
.

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

The proposed weight connectives and coconnectives
clearly satisfy the desired orthogonality,
are closed under retracts by Corollary
5.7
,
and naive truncations provide us with weight decompositions
by Lemma
5.10
.
To determine the weight heart, note that
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w=0}
clearly contains
\mathcal{A}

and hence
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
by the idempotent completeness of
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

and retract-closure of the weight heart.
Conversely, suppose that
X\in\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{w=0}
.
Then
X
is represented by
a connective chain complex
X_{*}
,
and we obtain a weight decomposition

X_{0}[0]\to X\to\tau_{\geq 1}X
.
Since the map
X\to\tau_{\geq 1}X
vanishes,
this exhibits
X
as a retract of
X_{0}[0]\in\mathcal{A}\subseteq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
.
This proves (1).

Now (2) is clear from Remark
5.12
,
and (3) follows from Lemma
5.13
.

It is also clear from the construction that the weight structure restricts to
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

and
\textsf{Ch}^{\pm}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
, so by Theorem
5.1
,
for (4) it remains to identify the weight heart of
\textsf{Ch}^{b}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
as
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
.
This was done in
[
17
, Example 3.1.3(1)]
.

For (5), note that by (3) we have
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\smash{(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb})}^{\updownarrow}
.
The inclusion
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}\subseteq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb}

induces by Theorem
5.1
a weight exact functor
f\colon\Stab(\mathcal{A})\to\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb}

which restricts to the inclusion
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
on weight hearts.
In particular,
f
is an idempotent completion, and we deduce from Corollary
4.13

that
\smash{f}^{\uparrow}\colon\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\uparrow}\to\smash{(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb})}^{\uparrow}=\textsf{Ch}^{-}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

is an equivalence.
But then of course also
\smash{f}^{\updownarrow}=\smash{(\smash{f}^{\uparrow})}^{\downarrow}\colon K(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\updownarrow}\to\smash{(\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{wb})}^{\updownarrow}=\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
is an equivalence (recall also from Proposition
4.16
that the order of left and right completion does not matter here, since we are in the
\ell=0
case).
Finally, (6) follows via a similar argument as in (5).
∎

6.
\kappa
-compactly generated weight structures

As we saw in the last section and specifically Theorem
5.1
,
it is well known how to generate bounded weight structures from their heart.
Another result in this vein is
[
9
, Theorem 4.3.2(II)]
, which shows how a set of generators
for a stable category with mutually connective hom spectra determine a bounded weight structure.
The goal of this section is to instead focus on generating weight structures
on (large) cocomplete stable categories by a collection of objects.
Namely, in the proof of Proposition
4.25

we saw how given a weakly weighted category
\mathcal{C}

we can construct a weight structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})

which is in a certain sense generated by the Yoneda-image of
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.
In this section, we will generalize
this to construct weight structures generated by
\kappa
-compact generators
for some regular cardinal
\kappa
,
see Theorem
6.3
below.
We also give some examples in this section and analyze their completeness properties,
although the main application of the theorem is in constructing a certain exotic “Anderson weight structure”
in Section
10
.

We will again focus on non-weak weight structures.
Recall from Remark
3.11
that these are saturated,
and hence the weight (co)connectives satisfy the closure properties listed in Lemma
3.10
.
It is the fourth closure property about ordinal-indexed diagrams,
or more specifically the results in Appendix
A

which enable us to generalize away from the compact case discussed in Proposition
4.25
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete weighted category,

\kappa
a regular cardinal, and
S\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}

a small collection of
\kappa
-compact objects.
We say the weight structure is
\kappa
-compactly generated by
S

if
X\in\mathcal{C}
is weight connective
precisely if
\hom(s,X)\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}
for all
s\in S
.

Remark 6.2
.

If
\mathcal{C}
admits a weight structure
\kappa
-compactly generated by
S
,
then the same weight structure is also
\lambda
-compactly generated by
S

for any regular cardinal
\lambda\geq\kappa
.
Recall also that if
\mathcal{C}
is presentable, then
\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}
is small for each
\kappa
.
In this case, if
\mathcal{C}
admits a
\kappa
-compactly generated weight structure,
then the weight structure is also
\kappa
-compactly generated by
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\cap\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}
.

Recall that by
S^{\oplus}
we denote the full subcategory on arbitrary sums of objects in
S
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete stable category,
\kappa
a regular
cardinal, and
S\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}
a small collection of
\kappa
-compact objects.
Then there exists a weight structure on
\mathcal{C}
that is
\kappa
-compactly generated by
S
. This satisfies:

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}=\{X\in\mathcal{C}\mid\hom(s,X)\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}\text{ for all $s\in S$}\}
.

(2)

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is the smallest class
\langle S\rangle
containing
S

and closed under finite limits, arbitrary sums, retracts, and colimits over diagrams

X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma\to\mathcal{C}
such that
\gamma\leq\kappa
is an ordinal,

X_{\bullet}
preserves colimits
17
17

17

This means that the canonical map
\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}\to X_{\lambda}

is an equivalence for all limit ordinals
\lambda<\gamma
.
,
and
\cofib(X_{\alpha}\to X_{\alpha+1})
is already in the class for any
\alpha+1<\gamma
.

(3)

Any
X\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}
is a retract of an object
\colim_{\alpha<\kappa}X_{\alpha}

for some diagram
X_{\bullet}\colon\kappa\to\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
which preserves colimits
and where
X_{0},\cofib(X_{\alpha}\to X_{\alpha+1})\in S^{\oplus}
for all
\alpha<\kappa
.

(4)

If shifts of objects in
S
jointly detect equivalences,
then
\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}=0
.

(5)

If
\kappa=\omega
, then
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is closed under all colimits.

(6)

In particular, if
\kappa=\omega
and the (shifts of)
s\in S
jointly detect equivalences, then
\mathcal{C}
is left complete.

Remark 6.4
.

If
\kappa=\omega
, this has previously appeared as
[
30
, Theorem 5]

or
[
10
, Theorem 2.3.4]
.

Proof.

We define
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
as in (1)
and
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}=\{X\in\mathcal{C}\mid\hom(X,Y)\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}\text{ for }Y\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\}
for now.
Clearly both are closed under retracts and orthogonal,
so to establish a weight structure it remains to prove the existence of weight decompositions, which we shall achieve via a small object argument.
Let
X\in\mathcal{C}
. We now use transfinite induction to construct a diagram
X_{\bullet}\colon\kappa+1\to\mathcal{C}
as follows:

(1)

Let
X_{0}=X
.

(2)

Having constructed the diagram up to
X_{\alpha}
, we define
X_{\alpha+1}
via the cofiber sequence

\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\bigoplus_{s\in S_{/X_{\alpha}[n]}}s[-n]\to X_{\alpha}\to X_{\alpha+1}.

(3)

For limit ordinals
\lambda\leq\kappa
we let
X_{\lambda}\simeq\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}
.

Now consider the
(\kappa+1)
-indexed diagram of fibers

F_{\bullet}\coloneqq\fib(\const X\Rightarrow X_{\bullet})
.
Taking colimits, this yields a fiber sequence
F_{\kappa}\to X\to X_{\kappa}
,
and we claim that
X_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1}
and
F_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.
For the former, let
n\geq 0
and note that since each
s\in S
is
\kappa
-compact
and
\kappa
is
\kappa
-filtered, we have

\pi_{-n}\hom(s,X_{\kappa})\simeq\colim_{\alpha<\kappa}\pi_{0}\hom(s[-n],X_{\alpha})

However, for each
\alpha<\kappa
, the map
\hom(s[-n],X_{\alpha})\to\hom(s[-n],X_{\alpha+1})

induces the zero map on
\pi_{0}
by construction.
Thus
\hom(s,X_{\kappa})
is 1-connective, showing
X_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1}
.

To see that
F_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
,
we let
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
and show that
\hom(F_{\kappa},Y)\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}
.
By construction of
F_{\bullet}

we have
F_{0}=0
,
F_{\lambda}=\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}F_{\alpha}

for limit ordinals
\lambda\leq\kappa

and also
\cofib(F_{\alpha}\to F_{\alpha+1})=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\bigoplus_{s\in S_{/X_{\alpha}[n]}}s[-n]
for
\alpha<\kappa
.
It follows that
\hom(F_{\bullet},Y)\colon\kappa^{\textsf{op}}\to\textsf{Sp}

satisfies the assumptions
of Corollary
A.3
for
n=0
,
so that
\hom(F_{\kappa},Y)=\lim_{\alpha<\kappa}\hom(F_{\alpha},Y)

is connective.

Next, to obtain the desired description of
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

from (2), suppose that
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
. Taking a weight decomposition

F_{\kappa}\to X\to X_{\kappa}
as above,
the second map vanishes
so
X
is a retract of
F_{\kappa}\in\langle S\rangle

and hence also lies in
\langle S\rangle
, as desired.

To see (3), take
X\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}
and form a weight decomposition
F_{\kappa}\to X\to X_{\kappa}

as above, where we don’t require any shifts (so always
n=0
) since
X
is already connective.
The map
X\to X_{\kappa}
vanishes, so
X
is a retract of
F_{\kappa}
, as desired.
Finally, the points (4)-(6) are immediate from the definitions.
∎

With this, we can prove the claim of Example
3.6
.

Example 6.5
.

We can deduce Example
3.6
from the results of
[
21
]

and Theorem
6.3
(in fact, this only requires the previously known version for compactly generated weight structures) as follows.
The category
\Perf(X)
is generated as a stable category by
\Perf(X)_{\leq 0}

(since every object has bounded Tor-amplitude).
In fact, since
X
is qcqs,
\QCoh(X)
is stably compactly generated by a single perfect complex
(see
[
7
, Theorem 3.3.1]
), which by shifting we may assume lies in
\Perf(X)_{\leq 0}
.
Now endow
\QCoh(X)
with the weight structure compactly generated by
\{G\}
.
Equivalently, this is the weight structure compactly generated by
\Perf(X)_{\leq 0}
,
which is the definition of the “local weight structure” on
\QCoh(X)
given in
[
21
, Construction 6.23]
.
Since
X
has finite cohomological dimension
we also have
G\in\QCoh(X)_{\geq-n}
for some
n

(this is equivalent to the fact that
\Perf(X)
is a pre-
c
-category).
By
[
21
, Theorem 6.28]
, this data makes
\Perf(X)
into a
c
-structure.
Now
[
21
, Proposition 3.8]
yields the desired conclusion
(up to translating their notion of heart category of cohomological dimension
\leq\ell

into our notion of weakly
\ell
-weighted category via Remark
3.4
)
once we can check the assumption that there is some
\ell<\infty

for which every object in
\QCoh(X)_{=0}
can be written as a filtered
colimit of objects in
\Perf(X)\cap\QCoh(X)_{\geq-\ell}
.
But by Theorem
6.3
(3) every object
\mathcal{F}\in\QCoh(X)_{=0}
is a retract
of an object admitting an (exhaustive)
\mathbb{N}
-indexed filtration
with associated graded a sum of copies of
G\in\QCoh(X)_{\geq-n}
.
In particular,
\mathcal{F}\in\Ind(\Perf(X)\cap\QCoh(X)_{\geq-n})\subseteq\QCoh(X)
,
so it can also be expressed as a single filtered colimit of objects in
\Perf(X)\cap\QCoh(X)_{\geq-n}
.

We now apply Theorem
6.3
to construct a weight
structure on the category of (left-)modules over a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring.
For
R=\mathbb{S}
, the resulting weight structure on spectra was originally constructed by Bondarko in
[
9
, Section 4.6]
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
R
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring.
There is a weight structure on the category
\textsf{LMod}(R)

compactly generated by
\{R\}
, satisfying:

(1)

the weight structure is adjacent to the canonical complete
t
-structure
of
[
22
, Proposition 7.1.13]
.
We let
\textsf{LMod}(R)_{\geq 0}\coloneqq\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w\geq 0}=\textsf{LMod}(R)_{t\geq 0}
.

(2)

If
A
is an ordinary ring,
then
\textsf{LMod}(A)_{w\leq 0}
consists of those
A
-modules
M

which are
t
-coconnective and satisfy that
\hom_{\textsf{LMod}(A)}(M,N)

is connective for all ordinary
A
-modules
N
.
Equivalently,
M
is
t
-coconnective and for all
n\geq 0

the ordinary
A
-module
\pi_{-n}M

has projective dimension
\leq n
as an
A
-module.
In the general case, an
R
-module
M
is weight coconnective
if and only if
\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M
is weight coconnective in
\textsf{LMod}(\pi_{0}R)
.
Note that if
R
is
n
-truncated, then
\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w\leq 0}\subseteq\textsf{LMod}(R)_{t\leq n}
.

(3)

The weight heart
\textsf{LMod}(R)^{w\heartsuit}=\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w=0}

is given by
\Proj(R)=\{R\}^{\oplus,\textsf{idem}}
, the idempotent completion
of the full subcategory of
\textsf{LMod}(R)
on direct sums of
R
.

(4)

The weight structure is left complete.
If
A
is an ordinary ring of finite left global dimension,
then the weight structure on
\textsf{LMod}(A)=\mathcal{D}(A)
is also right complete.

(5)

If
R\to S
is a map of connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-rings,
then
S\otimes_{R}-\colon\textsf{LMod}(R)\to\textsf{LMod}(S)
is weight exact.

(6)

If
R
is commutative, then the weight structure
is compatible with the monoidal structure in the sense of
Corollary
4.10
(4).

Proof.

Applying Theorem
6.3
,
point (1) is clear.
For (4), the left completeness follows from left completeness of the
t
-structure
and Lemma
2.25
.
Given an ordinary ring
A
of left global dimension
\leq d
,
then by (2) we have inclusions
\textsf{LMod}(A)_{w\leq 0}\subseteq\textsf{LMod}(A)_{t\leq 0}\subseteq\textsf{LMod}(A)_{w\leq d}
.
Since the
t
-structure on
\textsf{LMod}(A)
is right complete,
we deduce that also the weight structure is right complete
via Remark
2.24
and the dual of Lemma
2.25
.

For point (5) it is clear that the basechange preserves weight connectives.
But since the functor is an exact left adjoint,
it also preserves all the operations under which
\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w\leq 0}
is generated
from
R
. Since
S\otimes_{R}R=S\in\textsf{LMod}(S)_{w\leq 0}
,
we conclude that
S\otimes_{R}-
is weight exact.
For (6), the only possibly non-trivial part is the closure of
\textsf{Mod}(R)_{w\leq 0}
under tensor products.
However,
\otimes_{R}
preserves the operations under which
\textsf{Mod}(R)_{w\leq 0}
is generated from
R

in both variables separately, and
R\otimes_{R}R\simeq R\in\textsf{Mod}(R)_{w\leq 0}
,
which allows us to conclude.

Supposing (2) for the moment, let us argue for (3).
Consider
M\in\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w=0}
. Then by (2) we find that there is a discrete
projective
\pi_{0}R
-module
P
so that
P\oplus\pi_{0}(\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M)
is free.
Picking a basis, we find a map
\bigoplus_{I}R\to\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M

which is a split epimorphism on
\pi_{0}
. Since
\cofib(M\to\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M)\simeq\Sigma\tau_{\geq 1}R\otimes_{R}M
is 2-connective and
\bigoplus_{I}R
lies in the weight-heart,
we see that this lifts to a map
f\colon\bigoplus_{I}R\to M

which is a split epi on
\pi_{0}
.
But then
\cofib(f)
is 1-connective,
and we see that
f
admits a retraction, as desired.

It remains to show (2).
We know that
\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w\leq 0}
is generated by
R

under finite limits, retracts, extensions, sums and sequential colimits
of maps whose cofiber is already weight coconnective.
Denote by
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\textsf{LMod}(R)
the class of
R
-modules
M
described in (2),
i.e. where
M_{0}\coloneqq\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M
is coconnective with
\pi_{-n}M_{0}

of projective dimension
\leq n
for all
n\geq 0
.
Clearly
\mathcal{C}
is closed under extensions, retracts and sums.
It is also closed under loops, and hence under finite limits.
We claim it is also closed under sequential colimits of maps with cofiber already in
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
C=\colim(C_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{0}}C_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}C_{2}\to\cdots)
be such a colimit
and write
C^{\pi_{0}}=\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}C
and
C^{\pi_{0}}_{n}=\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}C_{n}
.
Then
\pi_{0}C^{\pi_{0}}=\colim_{n}\pi_{0}C^{\pi_{0}}_{n}

is a sequential colimit of projective
\pi_{0}R
-modules
and split injections, so one can inductively make them into summand inclusions
and conclude that
\pi_{0}C^{\pi_{0}}
is a sum of projectives, hence projective.
For
n\geq 1
, the Milnor sequence shows that for any ordinary
A
-module
M
,
we have

\Ext^{n+1}_{\pi_{0}R}(\pi_{-n}C^{\pi_{0}},M)\cong\pi_{-(n+1)}\hom_{\mathcal{D}(\pi_{0}R)}(C^{\pi_{0}},M)\cong\lim_{k}\!{}^{1}\,\pi_{-n}\hom_{\mathcal{D}(\pi_{0}R)}(C^{\pi_{0}}_{k},M).

But the condition on the cofibers of
C_{k}\to C_{k+1}
guarantees that the
\lim^{1}
vanishes
by the Mittag-Leffler criterion. This proves
\textsf{LMod}(R)_{w\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
.

For the converse inclusion,
consider first the case of
A\coloneqq\pi_{0}R
.
So suppose that
M\in\textsf{LMod}(A)\simeq\mathcal{D}(A)
is
t
-coconnective
with
\pi_{-n}M
having projective dimension
\leq n
.
We show that
\hom_{\mathcal{D}(A)}(M,N)
is connective for all connective
N
,
which yields that
M
is weight coconnective.
If
N\in\mathcal{D}(A)_{t=0}
is an ordinary
A
-module, then by assumption we get
that
\hom_{A}(\Sigma^{n}\pi_{-n}M,N)
is connective for each
n\geq 0
.
Pulling out
M\simeq\tau_{\geq-n}M
shows that
\hom_{A}(M,N)
is a sequential inverse limit
of connective spectra where all maps are
\pi_{0}
-surjections.
Thus
\hom_{A}(M,N)
is connective.
Now for general connective
N
, we can also pull out the Postnikov tower
N=\lim_{n}\tau_{\leq n}N

and see that
\hom_{A}(M,N)
is a sequential limit growing in connectivity of connective spectra, hence connective.
This proves (2) for
A
.

Now let
M\in\mathcal{C}\subseteq\textsf{LMod}(R)
. Then
\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M
is weight coconnective,
and we want to show that
M
is weight coconnective.
Let
N
be a connective
R
-module.
Then
\hom_{R}(M,\Sigma^{n}\pi_{n}N)\simeq\hom_{\pi_{0}R}(\pi_{0}R\otimes_{R}M,\Sigma^{n}\pi_{n}N)
is
n
-connective.
It follows inductively that each
\hom_{R}(M,\tau_{\leq n}N)
is connective,
and thus that
\hom_{R}(M,N)
is a sequential limit growing in connectivity of connective spectra,
hence connective. This concludes the proof of (2).
∎

Example 6.7
.

For
R=\mathbb{Z}
, we get a complete weight structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})

which is adjacent to the complete
t
-structure.
The weight coconnectives are precisely the
t
-coconnective complexes with free abelian
\pi_{0}
.

For
R=\mathbb{S}
, we obtain a left complete weight structure adjacent to the complete
t
-structure on
Sp
.
The weight coconnective spectra are precisely those for which
\mathbb{Z}\otimes X
is weight coconnective
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})
, i.e. those spectra whose
\mathbb{Z}
-homology is free in degree
0
and vanishes in degrees
\geq 1
.

Remark 6.8
.

The description of weight coconnective spectra
yields that
\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq-\infty}=\{X\in\textsf{Sp}\mid\mathbb{Z}\otimes X=0\}\neq 0
.
This is not the only reason that the standard weight structure on
Sp
is not right complete;

\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}
is also not closed under sequential inverse limits growing in weight coconnectivity,
so we cannot just quotient out
\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq-\infty}

to guarantee right completeness by (the dual of) Lemma
4.24
.
Indeed, to see this, note that
\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{S}[-n]\simeq\lim(\cdots\to\mathbb{S}[-1]\times\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{S})

is such a limit, where each term
\prod_{n=0}^{k}\mathbb{S}[-n]\in\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}
.
However,

\pi_{k}\bigg(\mathbb{Z}\otimes\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{S}[-n]\bigg)_{\mathbb{Q}}=\pi_{k}\bigg(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{S}[-n]\bigg)=\pi_{k}\bigg(\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{S}[-n]\bigg)_{\mathbb{Q}}=\bigg(\prod_{n\geq 0}\pi_{k+n}(\mathbb{S})\bigg)_{\mathbb{Q}}\neq 0

for all
k
, since there is for example an embedding

\prod_{p\geq k\text{ prime}}\mathbf{F}_{p}\hookrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}\pi_{k+n}(\mathbb{S})
,
and the element
([1],[1],[1],\dots)
spans an infinite cyclic group in the former,
so that both groups have non-trivial rationalization.
In particular, we see that
\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{S}[-n]\notin\textsf{Sp}_{w<\infty}
.

The examples discussed here only required the previously known version of Theorem
6.3

for
\kappa=\omega
.
However, in Section
10
we will see another interesting
example of a weight structure on Spectra, for whose existence we need the
\kappa=\omega_{1}
version of the Theorem.

7.
Weak
t
-structures and their completions

Unlike completions of weight structures as introduced in the previous sections,
the notion of complete
t
-structure is already well established.
In this section, we record how the right completion of a
t
-structure
can be achieved by an analogous procedure as that of the left completion of weight structures above.
In fact, we will work with the following weakening of the notion of
t
-structure,
akin to weakly weighted categories.
The entire theory parallels that of weakly weighted categories and their completions,
so we will be brief.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable category and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{\infty\}
.
A weak
t
-structure of defect
m
on
\mathcal{C}
consists of full subcategories

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
such that

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is closed under suspensions,

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
under loops, and both under retracts and extensions.

(2)

\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X_{\geq 0},Y_{\leq 0})\in\textsf{Sp}_{t\leq 0}

for
X_{\geq 0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
and
Y_{\leq 0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.

(3)

For every
X\in\mathcal{C}
there is a cofiber sequence

X_{>-m}\to X\to X_{\leq 0}
with
X_{>-m}\in\mathcal{C}_{>-m}
and
X_{\leq 0}\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.
We refer to this as a
t
-decomposition of
X
.

Here
\mathcal{C}_{>n-1}=\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}=\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\leq n}=\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{\geq-n}
.
This recovers the usual notion of
t
-structure for
m=0
.

As before, a weak
t
-structure of defect
m
is also a weak
t
-structure of any defect
m^{\prime}\geq m
.
By a weak
t
-category we mean a stable category equipped with a weak
t
-structure of defect
\infty
.

Warning 7.2
.

As in Warning
3.2
,
if
(\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}},(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})^{\textsf{op}},(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})^{\textsf{op}})
is a weak
t
-category of defect
m

and
m<\infty
, then
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})
is a weak
t
-category of the same defect.
But if
m=\infty
, then we have
t
-decompositions
X_{\geq 0}\to X\to X_{<\infty}
instead.
In this case, we say
\mathcal{C}
is a weak co-
t
-structure
18
18

18

In the literature a weight structure is sometimes
referred to as a co-
t
-structure. This is incompatible with the nomenclature here.
,
and a weak bi-
t
-structure if both
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})

and
(\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}},(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})^{\textsf{op}},(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})^{\textsf{op}})
are weak
t
-structures.

Remark 7.3
.

Analogously to Remark
3.3
,
we note that if
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})
satisfies axioms (1) and (2) of Definition
7.1

and
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}=\mathcal{C}
, then
\mathcal{C}
is a weak
t
-category via the trivial decompositions
X\xrightarrow{\id}X\to 0

for all
X\in\mathcal{C}
.
Dually, if
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}=\mathcal{C}
, then
\mathcal{C}
a weak co-
t
-category
via the trivial decompositions
0\to X\xrightarrow{\id}X
.
So if
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}=\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
, then
\mathcal{C}
is a weak bi-
t
-category.
In particular, if
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
is a stable connectivity structure
we can define
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}={(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}
.
Then
(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{(-\infty,0]})
is always a weak
t
-structure,
and
(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty},\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})
is a weak co-
t
-structure
when
\mathcal{C}_{[0,\infty)}
is closed under retracts in
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
.

There are various equivalent reformulations of the above notion,
analogous to those of Remark
3.4
.
The notion of
t
-amplitude of an exact functor between weak
t
-categories
is defined analogously to Definition
3.7
,
which yields categories
\textsf{wTCat},\textsf{wTCat}_{\leq 0},\textsf{wTCat}_{=0}
of weak
t
-categories and exact functors of bounded above
t
-amplitude,
t
-amplitude
[-\infty,0]
, and
t
-amplitude
[0,0]
.
Dual to the case of weak weight structures, we will mostly be concerned with the
right
completions of weak
t
-structures, i.e. colimit completions of their underlying coconnectivity structures.

Observation 7.4
.

Following Definition
3.9
, we say that a weak
t
-category is left resp. right saturated
if
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}={(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}
resp.
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}={}^{\perp}{(\mathcal{C}_{\leq-1})}

and saturated if it is both left and right saturated.
If
\mathcal{C}
is a left
(right)
saturated weak
t
-category,
then
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

(
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
)
is closed under limits
(colimits)
in
\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 7.5
.

In practice, the weak
t
-structures we consider will typically be left saturated in the above sense.
If we specify only a single class
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
and refer to it as defining a weak
t
-structure, we implicitly take the other class to be its saturation as above.

In general, the analogue of Lemma
3.14
,
shows that given a weak
t
-structure,
one can construct another by
left saturating

\mathcal{C}
,
i.e. by replacing
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
with the right orthogonal of
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1}
.
The identity will yield a functor of
t
-amplitude
[0,0]
in one direction,
and a functor of
t
-amplitude
[0,m]
in the other.

Example 7.6
.

If
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq a}
for some
a\in\mathbb{Z}
,
then
\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,-)\colon\mathcal{C}\to\textsf{Sp}
has
t
-amplitude in
[-\infty,-a]
.
Hence Corollary
2.22
implies that this functor preserves
sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity.

Definition 7.7
.

Analogously to Definition
3.16
we say that a full stable
subcategory
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
is right dense in the weak
t
-category
\mathcal{C}
if for every
X\in\mathcal{C}

and
n\geq 0
there exists a map
U\to X
with cofiber in
\mathcal{C}_{\leq-n}
.
The canonical example is
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
itself.
Given
X\in\mathcal{C}
, a
\mathcal{U}
-Whitehead tower for
X
is a diagram
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}

so that
\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{/X}
is a cone growing in coconnectivity in the sense of (the dual of)
Definition
2.12
, meaning that for every
n\geq 0

the cofibers
U_{k}\to X
(and hence of
U_{k}\to U_{k+1}
) are eventually
n
-coconnective.

Lemma
3.18
and its proof dualize verbatim to show the following.

Lemma 7.8
.

If
\mathcal{U}
is right dense in
\mathcal{C}
, then any
X\in\mathcal{C}
admits a
\mathcal{U}
-Whitehead tower,
and any such tower
U_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{U}_{/X}
is colimit-cofinal.

Definition 7.9
.

A weak
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is right complete if
its underlying coconnectivity structure
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0})
is colimit complete, meaning:

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{\leq-\infty}\coloneqq\bigcap_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{\leq-n}=0
.

(2)

\mathcal{C}
admits sequential colimits growing in
t
-coconnectivity.

(3)

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is closed under such sequential colimits.

We let
\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}\subseteq\textsf{wTCat}
be the full subcategory on right complete weak
t
-categories.

Observation 7.10
.

Analogously to Observation
4.3
,
the property of a weak
t
-category
\mathcal{C}
being right complete
does not depend on
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
. In particular, if
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})

is another weak
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
with the same coconnectives (e.g.
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\geq 0}={}^{\perp}{(}\mathcal{C}_{\leq-1})
), then it is also right complete.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{C}
is right complete, then
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
is right complete and idempotent complete by the dual of Lemma
2.32
.
In particular, also
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{=0}
are idempotent complete.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{U}
is right dense in
\mathcal{C}
,
then any
\mathcal{U}
-Whitehead tower
X_{\bullet}
of
X
converges, i.e.
\colim_{n}X_{n}\simeq X
.
In this sense, any object in
\mathcal{C}
may be obtained as the colimit
of a sequential diagram in
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
growing in coconnectivity.

The dual of Lemma
4.6
gives the following lemma.

Lemma 7.11
.

(1)

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be of amplitude
(a,b]

for
-\infty\leq a\leq b<\infty

between right complete weak
t
-categories.
If its restriction
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{>-\infty}

is fully faithful
(
an equivalence
)
, then so is
f
.

(2)

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a
t
-exact functor of right complete weak
t
-categories.
If the restriction
\mathcal{C}_{=0}\to\mathcal{D}_{=0}
is fully faithful
(
an equivalence
)
,
then so is the restriction
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{<\infty}
.

Definition 7.12
.

A
t
-exact functor of weak
t
-categories

\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}

exhibits
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
as the right completion of
\mathcal{C}

if
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
is right complete and
for any other right complete weak
t
-category
\mathcal{D}
,
precomposition by
\eta
induces an equivalence

(2)

\eta^{*}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow},\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,0]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}).

In other words, if
\eta
exhibits
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
as the left adjoint object to
\mathcal{C}

under the inclusion
\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}_{=0}\subseteq\textsf{wTCat}_{=0}
.

As one expects,
the main theorem on left completions of weak weight structures
has an analogue for right completions of weak
t
-structures:

Theorem 7.13
.

Any weak
t
-category of defect
m
admits a right completion
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
.
This yields a left Bousfield localization
\smash{(-)}^{\downarrow}\colon\textsf{wTCat}_{=0}\to\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}_{=0}
such that

(1)

There exists a fully faithful
t
-exact functor
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\hookrightarrow\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
19
19

19

Here we equip
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
with the
t
-structure from Proposition
7.25
with connectives
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
.

which lets us identify

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle\simeq\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})\mid\text{$\forall n\geq 0$ there is $c\in\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}$ and a map
$jc\to X$ with $n$-coconnective cofiber}\}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})\mid X\text{ admits a $\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}$-Whitehead tower}\}

\displaystyle=\{``\colim_{n}"c_{n}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\text{ growing in coconnectivity}\}.

which is the largest stable subcategory of
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})

in which
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
is right dense,
and also the smallest stable subcategory of
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})

containing
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
and closed under sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity.
Moreover, we have

\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{\leq 0}=\{``\colim_{n}"c_{n}\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{(-\infty,0]}\text{ growing in coconnectivity}\}.

(2)

The inclusion induces a
t
-exact equivalence
\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})}^{\downarrow}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
. Moreover:

(a)

The unit
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}

is
t
-exact and the left Kan extension
of
\eta_{\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}}
along
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
.

(b)

\eta_{\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}}

agrees with the Yoneda embedding

j\colon\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\hookrightarrow\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
, so is always fully faithful.
In fact, for
X\in\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
we have
\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}}(\eta X,\eta Y)
.

(c)

\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}

is right dense,
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{\geq 0}
is dense,
and we have
\mathcal{C}_{(-\infty,0]}=\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\cap\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{\leq 0}
.

(d)

\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
agrees with the Verdier projection

\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{C}_{\leq-\infty}
whenever
\mathcal{C}
already
admits sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity
and
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is closed under them.

(3)

For any
-\infty\leq a\leq b
and
-\infty<b\leq\infty
,
restriction along
\eta
induces an equivalence

\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow},\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[a,b]}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

with inverse given by left Kan extension along
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}
.
In particular, if
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a functor in
wTCat
of amplitude
[a,b]
,
then
\smash{f}^{\downarrow}=(\eta_{\mathcal{C}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{D}}f)\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\downarrow}

again has amplitude
[a,b]
.
Analogously for
(a,b]
instead of
[a,b]
.

(4)

In particular, right completion also defines a left adjoint
to the inclusions
\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\textsf{wTCat}_{\leq 0}

respectively
\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}\subseteq\textsf{wTCat}
.

We now show that in the case
m=0
the above right completion
recovers the usual right completion of
t
-structures.

Corollary 7.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable category with
t
-structure.
Then
\mathcal{C}
is right complete if and only if it is right complete
in our sense. Moreover we obtain a natural
t
-exact equivalence
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\simeq\lim_{n}\mathcal{C}_{\geq-n}
under
\mathcal{C}
,
i.e. our definition of right
t
-completion specializes to the usual one.

Proof.

The dual of Lemma
2.25

shows that the
t
-structure is right complete if and only if the underlying
coconnectivity structure is colimit complete, which is precisely
our definition of right completeness.

Next, the universal property of
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
yields a
t
-exact comparison map

\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\to\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\lim_{n}\mathcal{C}_{\geq-n}
.
Moreover, this extends to a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the bottom map is an equivalence by right completeness of
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
.
This implies that the right vertical map is essentially surjective.
But it is also fully faithful as limit of the fully faithful
functors
\mathcal{C}_{\geq-n}\to(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})_{\geq-n}
, cf. Theorem
7.13
(2b).
By 2-out-of-3 also the diagonal functor in this diagram is an equivalence,
as desired.
∎

Remark 7.15
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a
t
-category,
then from the above it is clear that the right completion
only depends on
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}=\SW(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})

(cf. Lemma
2.6
(4)).
Thus by
[
23
, Remark C.1.2.10]

there is another description of the right completion

\mathcal{C}
as the category
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})=\lim(\cdots\to\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\Omega}\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\xrightarrow{\Omega}\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
of spectrum objects in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
We view the right completion of weak
t
-structures
as a generalized way to take spectrum objects on a prestable category
and come back to this in Section
8.2
.

The following are the direct analogues of Corollaries
4.11
and
4.13
.

Corollary 7.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weak
t
-category. Then:

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{tb}\hookrightarrow(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})_{tb}

is an idempotent completion.

(2)

\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{>-\infty}=\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})\mid\text{ there is some bounded above $Y$ with }X\oplus Y\in\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\}
.

(3)

\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}=(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})_{>-\infty}

if and only if
\mathcal{C}_{tb}
is idempotent complete.
In particular, if
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
is idempotent complete
then also
(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow})_{>-\infty}
is.

Corollary 7.17
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
be weak
t
-categories
and
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
of amplitude
(-\infty,b]
for
b\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(1)

\smash{f}^{\downarrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{<\infty}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\downarrow}_{<\infty}

is fully faithful
(
an equivalence
)

exactly if
f^{\textsf{idem}}\colon(\mathcal{C}_{tb})^{\textsf{idem}}\to(\mathcal{D}_{tb})^{\textsf{idem}}
is.

(2)

\smash{f}^{\downarrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\downarrow}
is fully faithful
exactly if
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{>-\infty}
is.

(3)

\smash{f}^{\downarrow}\colon\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\to\smash{\mathcal{D}}^{\downarrow}
is an equivalence
exactly if
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\to\mathcal{D}_{>-\infty}
is a fully faithful right dense inclusion.

Here ‘equivalence’ means ‘equivalence of underlying categories’
as opposed to ‘equivalence in
wTCat
’.
20
20

20

Though in the saturated case this is implied
by the analogue of Lemma
3.12
(3),
and for
m<\infty
it also follows from the analogue of Lemma
3.14
.

Remark 7.18
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a weak co-
t
-category in the sense of Warning
7.2
,
so that
\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}}
is a weak
t
-category, then as in Remark
4.15

we can dualize the definitions and results in this section
to obtain a good theory of left completion for weak co-
t
-structures
(hence for weak
t
-structures of defect
m<\infty
).

Overall, we can summarize the various notions of completeness
introduced in this paper via the following table:

left complete

right complete

(weak)
t
-structure

(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0})
limit complete

(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0})
colimit complete

(weak) weight structure

(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0})
colimit complete

(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0})
limit complete

The proof of Theorem
7.13

is entirely analogous to the proof of Theorem
4.9
in the weight case.
For the convenience of the reader, below we spell out the analogues of the series of Lemmas and Propositions
from Section
4
which lead up to the proof of Theorem
4.9
; the proofs go through with minimal adaptions.

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weak
t
-category and
(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0})

a colimit complete stable coconnectivity structure.
Suppose that
i\colon\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is right dense
and equip
\mathcal{U}
with coconnectives
\mathcal{U}_{\leq 0}=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.
For
b\in\mathbb{Z}
, Kan extension and restriction along
i
induce an equivalence

i_{!}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\leq b}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{D})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\leq b}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}i^{*}

where
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\leq b}\subseteq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}
is the full subcategory
of functors of amplitude
\leq b
, i.e. restricting to
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\to\mathcal{D}_{\leq b}
.

Proof.

Analogous to Lemma
4.18
.
∎

.

\mathcal{D}

\mathcal{U}_{\leq 0}=\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

21

21

).

(a,b]
.

.

Corollary 7.21
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}

\mathcal{D}

.

.

Lemma 7.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be weak
t
-category of defect
m
and
\mathcal{D}
a weak
t
-category.
Suppose that:

(i)

\mathcal{D}
admits sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity and
\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}
is closed under them.

(ii)

There is a fully faithful
t
-exact embedding
j\colon\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}
.

Denote by
\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle\subseteq\mathcal{D}

the smallest full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}
which contains
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}

and is closed under sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity.
Then:

(1)

If
c_{\bullet},d_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
are growing in coconnectivity,
then any map
\colim c_{\bullet}\to\colim d_{\bullet}
in
\mathcal{D}

lifts to a map of diagrams
c_{\bullet}\Rightarrow d_{f(\bullet)}
for some
strictly increasing
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

As full subcategories of
\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle=\

\displaystyle\{\colim_{n}c_{n}\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\text{ growing in coconnectivity}\},

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}\coloneqq\

\displaystyle\{\colim_{n}c_{n}\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{(-\infty,0]}\text{ growing in coconnectivity}\}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0},

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\leq-\infty}=\

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\cap\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}=\

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{(-\infty,0]}.

Moreover,
\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}

is closed under sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity.
In case that
\mathcal{C}_{(-\infty,0]}=\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}

(e.g. if
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
is left saturated)
we have
\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle_{\leq 0}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}
.

(3)

\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle
is a stable subcategory of
\mathcal{D}

and with
\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}\coloneqq\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle\cap\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
we have:

(a)

\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle
is a right complete weak
t
-category of defect
m
.

(b)

Both inclusions
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle\subseteq\mathcal{D}
are
t
-exact, and the first is right dense.

(c)

If
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle_{\geq 0}

is dense, then both
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\subseteq\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle

and its Kan extension
\mathcal{C}\to\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle

are right completions.

(4)

If
\mathcal{D}_{\leq-\infty}=0
so that
\mathcal{D}
is right complete,
then
\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle
is the largest stable subcategory
of
\mathcal{D}
in which
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
is right dense, i.e.

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle=\

\displaystyle\{X\in\mathcal{D}\mid\text{for $n\geq 0$ there is $c\in\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}$ and a map
$c\to X$ with cofiber in $\mathcal{D}_{\leq-n}$}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{X\in\mathcal{D}\mid X\text{ admits a $\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}$-Whitehead tower}\}.

Proof.

Analogous to Lemma
4.23
.

Lemma 7.24
.

\mathcal{C}

(1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{\leq-\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{R}}

Proposition 7.25
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weak
t
-category of defect
m
.
There is a
t
-structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
such that the Yoneda embedding is
t
-exact and

(1)

\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}=\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
.

(2)

We have
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\leq m})
.
22
22

22

For
m=\infty
, the second inclusion is vacuous. If
\mathcal{C}
is also a weak co-
t
-structure
(cf. Warning
7.2
), i.e. we also have weight decompositions

X_{\geq 0}\to X\to X_{<\infty}
,
then the same proof shows that
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
.

In particular, for a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
(i.e. for
m=0
) we have equality.

(3)

There exists a stable recollement

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq-\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{RR}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{R}}

where
j_{!}
is induced by the inclusion via
\Ind(-)
,
and
j_{!},j^{*}
are
t
-exact and
j_{*}
has amplitude
[-\infty,0]
,
and
j^{*}
is a right completion.
In particular,
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
is right complete.

(4)

If
\mathcal{D}
is another weak
t
-category
and
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
a map of
t
-amplitude
[a,b]

(
(a,b]
)

for
-\infty\leq a\leq b\leq\infty

then
f_{!}=\Ind(f)\colon\Ind(\mathcal{C})\to\Ind(\mathcal{D})

is of
t
-amplitude
[a,b+m]

(
(a,b+m]
)
.

Proof.

The
t
-structure exists by
[
22
, 1.4.4.11(1)]
,
and the rest follows as in Proposition
4.25
,
with one slight caveat;
for the proof of
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\leq m})
,
the category
(\mathcal{C}_{\leq m})_{/X}

does generally not have finite colimits hence is not filtered for the same reason as
\mathcal{C}_{/X}
.
However, given any finite diagram in
(\mathcal{C}_{\leq m})_{/X}

it admits a colimit
c\in(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})_{/X}
, and then picking a
t
-decomposition
of defect
m
for the colimit yields a factorization of
c\to X

through
c\to c_{\leq m}
. Thus there is a cone on the original diagram
in
(\mathcal{C}_{\leq m})_{/X}
, showing that
(\mathcal{C}_{\leq m})_{/X}

is filtered and cofinal in
\mathcal{C}_{/X}
.
∎

Warning 7.26
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a weakly
\ell
-weighted category
which also admits a weak
t
-structure,
then the induced weight structure
on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
admits an adjacent
t
-structure
by Proposition
4.25
,
and the above Proposition shows that the weak
t
-structure
also induces a
t
-structure on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
.
In general, these do not agree.
However, if
\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0}

and
\ell=0
, then Propositions
4.25
(3) and
7.25
(1) guarantee that the two
t
-structures on
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
do in fact agree.

Proof of Theorem
7.13
.

Again, the proof is largely analogous to that of Theorem
4.9
.
Concretely, one wants to apply
7.23
to the
t
-exact Yoneda embedding

\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\hookrightarrow\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})

to deduce that
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}\coloneqq\langle\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\rangle
is the desired right completion
of
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
and
\mathcal{C}
.
To this end, we need to check that
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{\geq 0}
is dense.
By right density of
\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\subseteq\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}
it follows that a given
X\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\downarrow}_{\geq 0}

is a retract of some
c\in\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
and hence compact in
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})
.
Now
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}=\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
and the inclusion into
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})

preserves filtered colimits. So
X
is also compact in
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}=\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
,
hence lies in
(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})^{\textsf{idem}}
, as desired.
The rest of the proof adapts verbatim.
∎

8.
The setting of prestable categories

We saw in Section
2
that a prestable category
\mathcal{C}

canonically gives rise to a bounded below stable connectivity structure
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
.
In subsection
8.1
we investigate how colimit completeness
of
\SW(\mathcal{C})
can be expressed in terms of
\mathcal{C}
,
and define a colimit completion for certain kinds of prestable categories.
Moreover, in subsection
8.2
we give a construction of spectrum
objects for prestable categories which generalizes the usual one that is defined
when the prestable category admits finite limits.
We begin by recalling the following equivalent characterizations of prestability.

Proposition 8.1

(
[
23
, Section C.1.2]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category. The following are equivalent

(1)

\mathcal{C}
is pointed, admits finite colimits,
\Sigma\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
is fully faithful,
and every morphism
X\to\Sigma Y
extends to a bifiber sequence
F\to X\to\Sigma Y
.

(2)

\mathcal{C}
is a finite-colimit-closed and extension-closed full subcategory of a stable category.

Moreover, in this case:

(a)

The canonical map
\mathcal{C}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})
is fully faithful.

(b)

If
f\colon\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}
is any fully faithful
embedding where
\mathcal{D}
is stable and
f
preserves finite colimits,
then the essential image of
f
is closed under extensions in
\mathcal{D}
.

We also saw in Lemma
2.6
that a prestable category
is equivalently a choice of connectivity structure on a stable category.
By its universal property,
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is the initial stable category into which
\mathcal{C}
embeds
in a finite-colimit preserving way, and this defines a connectivity structure
\SW(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}

which is closed under retracts in
\SW(\mathcal{C})
(cf. Lemma
2.6
).
This yields a reasonable notion of connectivity on
\mathcal{C}
,
and generally we let
\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}=\SW(\mathcal{C})_{\geq n}=\{\Sigma^{n}X\mid X\in\mathcal{C}\}

for
n\geq 0
