(which is then also closed under retracts in
\mathcal{C}
).
We will always mean this notion of connectivity when talking about, say, sequential diagrams
growing in connectivity in a prestable category.
There is more to be said when
\mathcal{C}
admits finite limits:

Proposition 8.2

(
[
23
, Lemma C.1.2.9]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a prestable category. The following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
admits finite limits.

(2)

\SW(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}
is the connective part of a
t
-structure on
\SW(\mathcal{C})
.

In this case
\mathcal{C}
admits categorical truncations
by restricting the
t
-truncations on
\SW(\mathcal{C})

(cf.
[
22
, Warning 1.2.1.9]
).

8.1.
Completeness

We start by formulating colimit completeness for prestable categories.

Definition 8.3
.

A prestable category
\mathcal{C}
is colimit
(limit)
complete if

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}\coloneqq\bigcap_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}=0
.

(2)

\mathcal{C}
admits sequential colimits
(limits)
growing in connectivity.

By Lurie’s prestable Dold-Kan correspondence
[
23
, Theorem C.1.3.1]

we obtain an analogue of Example
2.16
. In particular, we get:

Lemma 8.4
.

A prestable category
\mathcal{C}
admits sequential colimits growing in connectivity
if and only if it admits geometric realizations.

Example 8.5
.

A non-trivial stable category
\mathcal{C}
, considered as a prestable category,
can never be (co)limit complete, since
\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}=\mathcal{C}
for each
n
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
be a stable connectivity structure.
Then this is colimit
(
limit
)
complete if and only if the prestable category

\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is colimit
(
limit
)
complete and the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}

preserves colimits
(
limits
)
of sequential diagrams growing in connectivity.
23
23

23

In the colimit case, this is of course equivalent to the inclusion preserving geometric realizations.

Proof.

Clearly if the stable connectivity structure is colimit
(limit)
complete,
then also
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is colimit
(limit)
complete.
For the converse, it remains to show that
\mathcal{C}
admits colimits
(limits)

of sequential diagrams growing in connectivity.
We will deal with the case of colimits, as the case of limits is dual.
So let
X_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}
be a sequential diagram growing in connectivity.
By cofinality we may assume that
\cofib(X_{n}\to X_{n+1})\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+1}
.
In particular, also
\cofib(X_{0}\to X_{n})
is connective for all
n
.
Then the diagram
\cofib(X_{0}\to X_{\bullet})
lies in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}

and is still growing in connectivity, hence admits a colimit
C\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
By assumption, this is then also a colimit diagram in
\mathcal{C}
,
and one checks that
\fib(C\to\Sigma X_{0})
is the colimit of the original
X_{\bullet}
.
We also see that if each
X_{n}
is connective, then the colimit lies in
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
.
∎

Corollary 8.7
.

A category
\mathcal{C}
is a colimit
(
limit
)
complete prestable category precisely if it is contained as the connectives in a stable category with colimit
(
limit
)
complete connectivity structure.
Concretely, we can embed
\mathcal{C}
into the Spanier-Whitehead category
\SW(\mathcal{C})

which is colimit
(
limit
)
complete if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.6
,
it only remains to check that if
\mathcal{C}
is a colimit
(limit)
complete prestable category,
then the inclusion
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})
preserves colimits
(limits)
of sequential
diagrams growing in connectivity. But this follows from the fact that the connectivity
structure on
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is bounded below; if
X_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}

is a sequential diagram growing in connectivity with colimit
X
,
and
Y\in\SW(\mathcal{C})
; then
\Sigma^{n}Y\in\mathcal{C}
for some
n\geq 0
,
and hence
\hom(X,Y)\to\lim_{n}\hom(X_{n},Y)
is a shift of the equivalence

\hom(X,\Sigma^{n}Y)\simeq\lim_{n}\hom(X_{n},\Sigma^{n}Y)
.
Analogously for limits of sequential diagrams growing in connectivity.
∎

Corollary 8.8
.

A colimit complete prestable category is idempotent complete
and admits the kinds of colimits described in Lemma
2.30
.
Also a limit complete prestable category is idempotent complete.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Corollary
8.7
and Lemmas
2.30
and
2.32
.
∎

In the case where
\mathcal{C}
even admits finite limits,
we get an analogue of Lemma
2.25
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a prestable category admitting finite limits.

(1)

For
n\geq 0
, we have
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n+1}

if and only if
\tau_{\leq n}X=0

(cf. Proposition
8.2
.)

(2)

The following are equivalent:

(a)

\mathcal{C}
is limit complete.

(b)

\mathcal{C}
is limit and colimit complete.

(c)

\mathcal{C}
is (Postnikov) complete in the sense of
[
23
, Definition C.1.2.12]
, i.e. if
\tau_{\leq n}X=0
for all
n
then
X=0
,
and
\mathcal{C}=\lim(\cdots\to\tau_{\leq 2}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\tau_{\leq 1}}\tau_{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\tau_{\leq 0}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C})
.

(d)

The
t
-structure on
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is left complete.

Proof.

If
X
is an object in a stable category
with
t
-structure, then
\tau_{\leq n}X=0
if and only if
X
is
(n+1)
-
t
-connective. Hence (1) is immediate from Proposition
8.2
.

For (2) the implication (b)
\Rightarrow
(a) is clear.
Next, (a) implies by Corollary
8.7
that also
\SW(\mathcal{C})

is limit complete. Since here the connectivity structure on
\SW(\mathcal{C})

comes from a
t
-structure, we get (d) by Lemma
2.25
.
Given left completeness of
\SW(\mathcal{C})
,
we again obtain limit-completeness of
\mathcal{C}
and hence that
\mathcal{C}_{\geq\infty}=0
, which by (1) shows that
\infty
-connective objects vanish.
Moreover, for
n\geq 0
, the
t
-truncation
\tau_{\leq n}\colon\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq n+1}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq n}

restricts to the categorical truncation
\tau_{\leq n}\colon\tau_{\leq n+1}\mathcal{C}\to\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{C}
.
We obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\SW(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{n}\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{n}\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{\leq n})_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{\leq n})_{n}}

which is vertically right adjointable since truncations
commute with taking connective covers, see
[
22
, Proposition 1.2.1.10]
.
Since the top map in the above square is an equivalence by assumption,
we see that the bottom map is fully faithful.
The vertical right adjoints are localizations hence essentially surjective,
and it follows that the bottom map is also essentially surjective,
hence an equivalence, giving (c).
Finally, for (c)
\Rightarrow
(b) we can argue as in
Lemma
2.25
.
∎

As a next step, we want to define and construct colimit completions of prestable categories.

Definition 8.10
.

A right exact functor of prestable categories
\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

exhibits
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
as the colimit completion of
\mathcal{C}

if
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
is colimit complete,
and for any other colimit complete prestable category
\mathcal{D}
,
precomposition by
\eta
induces an equivalence

\eta^{*}\colon\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow},\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}).

From the above results it should be clear that a colimit completion of
\mathcal{C}

should be a kind of colimit completion of the stable connectivity structure
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
.
In particular, if the later underlies a weak weight structure,
we can use Theorem
4.9
to achieve this via left completion of the weak weight structure.

Observation 8.11
.

Let
\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq 0}={}^{\perp}{(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}

and
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq n}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\cap\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq n}
for
n\geq 0
.
Since
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})
is closed under retracts (Lemma
2.6
),
we see that
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C},\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq 0})
satisfies
all the axioms of a weak weight structure except the existence of weight decompositions
(cf. Definition
3.1
).

Example 8.12
.

Note that
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}
could easily be zero. For example, every stable category is prestable and in this case
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}
is zero.
For another example more in the spirit of this section we let
\mathcal{C}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t[0,\infty)}

be the category of bounded above, connective spectra with finitely generated homotopy groups, then
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq n}=0
for every
n
.
To see this, we will use Anderson duality (cf. Section
10
).
Recall that
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
is a coconnective spectrum with finitely generated homotopy
groups, and for any
X\in\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
the canonical map

X\to\hom(\hom(X,I_{\mathbb{Z}}),I_{\mathbb{Z}})
is an equivalence.
Now if
X\in\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}
, then

0=\pi_{-1}\hom(X,\Sigma^{n}\tau_{\geq-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}})=\pi_{-(n+1)}\hom(X,\tau_{\geq-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}})

for
n\geq 0
. Since
X
is connective,
the last term surjects onto
\pi_{-(n+1)}\hom(X,I_{\mathbb{Z}})
,
and so we get that
\hom(X,I_{\mathbb{Z}})
is connective.
Then
X\simeq\hom(\hom(X,I_{\mathbb{Z}}),I_{\mathbb{Z}})
is coconnective,
hence concentrated in degree 0.
In fact, using Lemma
10.2
we see that
\pi_{0}X
must be free,
so
X=\mathbb{Z}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
.
But then
\hom(X,\mathbb{Z})
cannot be connective unless
n=0
,
in which case
X=0
.

Definition 8.13
.

For
0\leq\ell\leq\infty
, we say that a prestable category
\mathcal{C}
is weakly
\ell
-pre-weighted if
for each
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
n\geq 0
there exists a cofiber sequence (“weight decomposition”)

X_{<n+\ell}\to X\to X_{\geq n}

with
X_{<n+\ell}\in\mathcal{C}_{w<n+\ell}
and
X_{\geq n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}
.
A functor of weakly preweighted categories is weight exact if it is right exact
and preserves weight coconnectives.

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be prestable and
0\leq\ell\leq\infty
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
is weakly
\ell
-pre-weighted.

(2)

(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C},\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq 0})
is weakly
\ell
-weighted.

(3)

There exists a weakly
\ell
-weighted category
\mathcal{D}

and an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

The equivalence of (1) and (2) follows from Observation
8.11

and shifting the weight decompositions.
Clearly (2) implies (3), and (3) implies (1) because an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}

restricts to equivalences
\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\geq n}
for all
n\geq 0

(since both sides are the essential images of
\Sigma^{n}
on
\mathcal{C}
resp.
\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}
).
∎

Example 8.15
.

Say that
\mathcal{C}
is weight bounded above if for each
X\in\mathcal{C}
there is some
n\geq 0

so that all maps from
X
to any
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}
vanish.
Equivalently, if
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}=\mathcal{C}
.
In this case
\mathcal{C}
is weakly
\infty
-preweighted with trivial weight decompositions
by Remark
3.3
.

Another example of similar fashion is the case where
\mathcal{C}
is a stable category
considered as a prestable one. Then
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}=0
and
\mathcal{C}_{w\geq\infty}=\mathcal{C}
,
so we also trivially have weight decompositions.

Observation 8.16
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a colimit complete pre-weighted category (
\ell=0
),
then by Proposition
4.7
we have
\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})

for
\mathcal{C}_{w=0}=\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}=\mathcal{C}\cap\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq 0}
.

The following result is then essentially a consequence of Theorem
4.9
.
Given a weakly preweighted
\mathcal{C}
, we endow
\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))
with the weight structure
of Proposition
4.25
, which allows us to also talk about (weight) (co)connectivity
in
\Ind(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))
.

Proposition 8.17
.

A weakly
\ell
-pre-weighted category
\mathcal{C}
admits a colimit completion.
Concretely,

\displaystyle\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\coloneqq\

\displaystyle\{X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\mid\text{$\forall n\geq 0$ there is $c\in\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}$ and a map
$jc\to X$ with $n$-connective cofiber}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{``\colim_{n}"c_{n}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}\text{ growing in connectivity}\}

is the smallest subcategory of
\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})
containing

\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
and closed under sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
Moreover:

(1)

\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
is the connective part of the left weight completion
of
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C},\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq 0})
.
In fact, there is an equivalence of categories
\SW(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})\simeq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}
under
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
.
24
24

24

The left completion
\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}
seems to generally not be left saturated, even if
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is.
Hence the equivalence under
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
need not be compatible with the coconnectives on both sides.

(2)

The Yoneda embedding
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}\hookrightarrow\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

left Kan extends to a right exact functor
\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

which exhibits
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
as the colimit completion of
\mathcal{C}
.

(3)

Let
\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}
be the category of prestable categories and right exact functors
and
\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\uparrow}
the full subcategory on the colimit complete ones.
The above construction defines a partial left adjoint

\smash{(-)}^{\uparrow}\colon\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\textsf{wpw}}\to\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\uparrow}

to
\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\uparrow}\subseteq\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}
,
defined on the full subcategory
\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\textsf{wpw}}

of weakly pre-weighted categories.

Proof.

We apply Theorem
4.9
to the weakly
\ell
-weighted
category
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C},\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w\leq 0})

and recognize
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}=\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}_{w\geq 0}
,
which shows the other descriptions of
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
.
For (1), note that by Lemma
2.6
,
we have an equivalence
\SW(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})=\SW(\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}_{\geq 0})\simeq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}_{>-\infty}

under
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
, but since
\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}
is still right bounded, the latter agrees with
\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}
.
Clearly this equivalence identifies the two embeddings of
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
.

Since (3) follows formally from (2), it remains to show the latter.
As
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
is the connective part of a left complete weak weight structure,
it is colimit complete.
To show the remaining property of Definition
8.10
,
let
\mathcal{D}
be a colimit complete prestable category.
We know from Corollary
8.7
that
(\SW(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{D})
is a colimit complete stable connectivity structure.
Now there is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\geq 0}(\SW(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}),\SW(\mathcal{D}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\geq 0}(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\SW(\mathcal{D}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow},\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\SW(j)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}

where the vertical maps restrict along the canonical inclusions
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})

respectively
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}\subseteq\SW(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
.
We claim these vertical restrictions are equivalences.
More generally, let
\mathcal{A}
be prestable and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{B}_{\geq 0})
be a stable connectivity structure.
By
[
23
, Proposition C.1.1.7]
the restriction
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}(\SW(\mathcal{A}),\mathcal{B})\to\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})

is an equivalence.
It is straightforward to verify that this restricts to an equivalence between the full subcategories

\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{\geq 0}(\SW(\mathcal{A}),\mathcal{B})
(of functors preserving connectives, i.e. restricting to
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}_{\geq 0}
)
and
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}_{\geq 0})
.
In particular, we can pick
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{B}=(\SW(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{D})
, which yields the claim.

So to show (2), it remains to see that also the top horizontal morphism is an equivalence.
This follows by noting that both
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})

and
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\subseteq\SW(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
are left dense,
so we can use Lemma
4.18

and a 2-out-of-3 argument as in the proof of Corollary
4.20
.
∎

By Example
8.15
the above Proposition gives us a functorial
colimit completion for weight bounded above prestable categories.
In analogy with the weighted case,
one might now be tempted to simply define
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
as
\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}

for a general prestable category
\mathcal{C}
.
Via left Kan extension along the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}

the Yoneda embedding then induces a map
\mathcal{C}\to\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})
.
However, there is now in general no reason for this to factor through
\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})}^{\uparrow}
.
In fact, the natural condition to guarantee this is that every object in
\mathcal{C}

admits
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
-weight complexes, i.e. that
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})

is left dense. But this is precisely the condition that
\mathcal{C}
is weakly pre-weighted.
The following Corollary makes a version of this statement precise.

Corollary 8.18
.

For a prestable category
\mathcal{C}
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete,
and the colimit functor
\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})}^{\uparrow}\to\mathcal{C}

(left Kan extended from the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
) is an equivalence.

(2)

\mathcal{C}
is weakly pre-weighted,
and
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is a left complete weakly weighted category.

In this case, the inverse of the colimit
functor is the restricted weight complex functor

w\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{<\infty})}^{\uparrow}
.

Proof.

Let us first argue that if
\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete,
then the colimit functor exists.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}=\mathcal{C}\cap\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w<\infty}

we have
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})=\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w<\infty}
as full subcategories of
\SW(\mathcal{C})
.
This subcategory is trivially weakly weighted,
and the inclusion
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\subseteq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}
is left dense
by Theorem
4.9
.
Now if
\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete, then so is
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
,
and hence the left Kan extension of
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\to\SW(\mathcal{C})

along
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\subseteq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}

exists by Lemma
4.18
,
which also shows that the resulting functor restricts to

\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}=\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}_{\geq 0}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}
.
It is straightforward to check that this is still left Kan extended from
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
.
Hence the colimit functor exists as claimed.

Now the implication (1)
\Rightarrow
(2)
is clear from the assumption that the colimit functor is an equivalence,
and that it identifies the two evident inclusions of
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
.

Conversely, given (2) we know that
\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete,
and as we saw above this yields a colimit functor

f\colon\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})
which is left Kan extended
from its restriction to
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})=\SW(\mathcal{C})_{w<\infty}
.
On the other hand, Theorem
4.9

also yields a weight complex functor
w\colon\SW(\mathcal{C})\to\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}

which is also left Kan extended from the inclusion
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})\subseteq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}
.
Since both
f
and
w
are left Kan extended from
\SW(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})
,
it follows that they are mutually inverse,
and by Lemma
4.18
both functors preserve connectives.
In particular, the restricted colimit functor
\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})}^{\uparrow}\to\mathcal{C}
is an equivalence,
showing (1).
∎

8.2.
Spectrum objects

Given a prestable category
\mathcal{C}
, there are generally many stable categories
\mathcal{D}
and fully faithful
embeddings
\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}
for which
\mathcal{C}
defines a stable connectivity structure on
\mathcal{D}
.
The minimal choice
\SW(\mathcal{C})
was investigated above,
but as Lurie details in
[
23
, Remark C.1.2.10]
,
under the additional assumption that
\mathcal{C}
admits finite limits,
there is also a “maximal”, or better, “terminal” choice for such a
\mathcal{D}
;
this is the category

\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})=\lim(\cdots\xrightarrow{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\Omega}\mathcal{C})

of spectrum objects in
\mathcal{C}
, which we can also identify as the right completion of the
t
-structure on
\SW(\mathcal{C})
.

We shall now generalize the construction of spectrum objects to prestable categories which do not necessarily admit finite limits.
More precisely, we give a construction of
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})
for an arbitrary prestable category
\mathcal{C}

which agrees with the old construction whenever
\mathcal{C}
admits finite limits.
To state the definition, we let
\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}={(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})

and
\mathcal{C}_{\tau\leq n}\coloneqq\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t[0,n]}=\mathcal{C}\cap\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq n}
for
n\geq 0
.

Observation 8.19
.

Since
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
is a bounded below stable connectivity structure
and
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is closed under retracts,
it follows from Remark
7.3

that
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C},\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0})
is always a right bounded weak
t
-structure
with trivial
t
-decompositions.

Lemma 8.20
.

For
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
n\geq 0
, the following are equivalent

(1)

\Omega^{n}X\in\SW(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
.

(2)

X\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau\leq n}
.

(3)

X
is
n
-truncated in
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The equivalence of the first two is clear by shifting and definition.
For
X\in\mathcal{C}
, we have that
X\in\mathcal{C}_{t\leq n}
if and only if for all
Y\in\mathcal{C}

we have that
\hom(Y,X)\in\textsf{Sp}_{t\leq n}
.
But this is precisely the case if
\map(Y,X)=\tau_{\geq 0}\hom(Y,X)\in\tau_{\leq n}\textsf{An}
,
which is the definition of
X
being
n
-truncated.
∎

Definition 8.21
.

A functor of prestable categories
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is said to have truncation amplitude
\leq n

if it sends
\mathcal{C}_{\tau\leq k}
to
\mathcal{D}_{\tau\leq k+n}
for
k\gg 0
.
It has bounded truncation amplitude if there is such an
n\geq 0
.

The following is then an immediate consequence of Lemma
8.20
.

Lemma 8.22
.

f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
has truncation amplitude
\leq n

if and only if
\SW(f)
has
t
-amplitude
[0,n]
.
In particular, if
f
is even an exact functor of prestable categories admitting finite limits,
then
\SW(f)
is
t
-exact.

Definition 8.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a prestable category.
We define
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})\coloneqq\smash{\SW(\mathcal{C})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}

as the right completion of the above weak
t
-structure on
\SW(\mathcal{C})
.

In particular, all the results of Theorem
7.13
apply.
We will specialize some into the following corollary.
As usual, we equip
\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))
with the induced
t
-structure
of Proposition
7.25
.

Theorem 8.24
.

For a prestable
\mathcal{C}
,
there is a
t
-exact inclusion
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))

which identifies

\displaystyle\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})=\

\displaystyle\{X\in\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))\mid\text{$\forall n\geq 0$ there is $c\in\mathcal{C}$ and a map
$c\to X$ with $n$-coconnective cofiber}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{X\in\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))\mid X\text{ admits a $\SW(\mathcal{C})$-Whitehead tower}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{``\colim_{n}"c_{n}\in\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))\mid c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})\text{ growing in coconnectivity}\}

as the largest stable subcategory of
\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))
in which
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is right dense,
and also the smallest stable subcategory of
\Ind(\SW(\mathcal{C}))
containing

\mathcal{C}
and closed under sequential colimits growing in coconnectivity.
Moreover:

(1)

There is a fully faithful,
t
-exact and right dense inclusion
j\colon\SW(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})
.
Moreover, the inclusion
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{t\geq 0}
is dense.

(2)

The stable connectivity structure
(\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
is colimit complete
25
25

25

Recall that left completeness of the weak
t
-structure corresponds to
limit
completeness of the underlying connectivity
structure.

if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete,
in which case
\mathcal{C}=\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{t\geq 0}
by idempotent completeness.

(3)

For every right complete weak
t
-category
\mathcal{D}

and
0\leq b<\infty
, restriction induces an equivalence

\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,b]}(\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}_{\tau\leq b}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0})

where
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}_{\tau\leq b}\subseteq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}

is the full subcategory on functors of truncation amplitude
\leq b
.

(4)

Let
\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\tau<\infty}
denote the category of prestable categories
and right exact functors of bounded truncation amplitude.
The above construction defines a functor

\textsf{Sp}(-)\colon\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\tau<\infty}\to\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}

which fits into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\tau<\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\textsf{lex}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{lex}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{ex}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textsf{Sp}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textsf{Sp}(-)}

where the unlabeled arrows are the evident forgetful functors / inclusions,
and the bottom
\textsf{Sp}(-)
is the usual spectrum objects construction.
The analogous statements hold if we restrict to truncation amplitude
\tau\leq 0
,
which lets us land in
\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}_{=0}
(
t
-amplitude
[0,0]
).

Proof.

The equivalent descriptions and results of (1) are immediate
from the definition of
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})
as right
t
-completion
and Theorem
7.13
.

For (2), note first that if
\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete,
then it is idempotent complete by Corollary
8.8
so by (1) we have
\mathcal{C}=\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
.
Now by Lemma
8.6

and Corollary
8.7

it remains to show that if
\mathcal{C}
is colimit complete,
then the inclusion
\SW(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})
preserves sequential colimits growing in connectivity.
Since
\SW(\mathcal{C})
is idempotent complete by Corollary
2.31
,
we have
\SW(\mathcal{C})=\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{>-\infty}
by the right density in (1).
Now suppose that
X_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})
is a sequential diagram growing in connectivity and
X=\colim_{n}X_{n}\in\SW(\mathcal{C})
.
Since any
Y\in\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})
admits a cofiber sequence
C\to Y\to Y_{\leq 0}

where
C\in\SW(\mathcal{C})
and
Y_{\leq 0}\in\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
,
it suffices to show that the canonical map
\hom(X,Y_{\leq 0})\to\lim_{n}\hom(X_{n},Y_{\leq 0})

is an equivalence for all
Y_{\leq 0}\in\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
.
Analogously to Example
7.6

we see that
\hom(-,Y_{\leq 0})
sends connective objects to coconnective spectra,
so by right completeness of
Sp
sends the sequential cone growing in connectivity

X_{\bullet}\Rightarrow\const X
to a limit cone.
This concludes the proof of (2).

For (3), we already know from Theorem
7.13
that restriction along
\SW(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})

induces an equivalence

\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,b]}(\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,b]}(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{D}).

At the end of the proof of Proposition
8.17
, we saw
that restricting along
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\SW(\mathcal{C})
gives an equivalence

\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,\infty]}(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{D})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0})
.
It is then straightforward to verify that this further restricts to an equivalence on full subcategories
of
t
-amplitude
[0,b]
respectively truncation amplitude
\tau\leq b
:

\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}_{[0,b]}(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{rex}}_{\tau\leq b}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}).

By composing, this proves (3).

Finally, to see (4), we recognize the claimed commutative diagram as the outer edge
of the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\tau<\infty}}}
wTCat
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{ex}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}_{\textsf{prest}}^{\textsf{lex}}}}
TCat
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\textsf{TCat}}^{\downarrow}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{lex}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\SW}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textsf{Sp}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\downarrow t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\SW}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\downarrow t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textsf{Sp}(-)}

where all unlabeled functor are forgetful functors / inclusions.
Note that
\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{lex}}_{\textsf{prest}}
is indeed a full subcategory of
\textsf{Cat}^{\tau<\infty}_{\textsf{prest}}

since equivalences automatically preserve finite limits,
and exact functors of prestable categories admitting finite limits
have truncation amplitude
\tau\leq 0
.
Similarly, since
t
-categories are saturated, the analogue of Lemma
3.12

shows that
TCat
is actually a full subcategory of
wTCat

(where in the latter we consider exact functors of bounded above
t
-amplitude),
and analogously for
\smash{\textsf{TCat}}^{\downarrow}\subseteq\smash{\textsf{wTCat}}^{\downarrow}
.
Now commutativity of the left and middle squares as well as the right upper triangle is clear.
The first two top horizontal functors are our definition of
\textsf{Sp}(-)
for prestable categories.

Now note that by Corollary
7.14
the functor
\smash{(-)}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\colon\textsf{TCat}\to\smash{\textsf{TCat}}^{\downarrow}

agrees with the usual definition of right
t
-completion of
t
-categories,
so it follows from
[
23
, Remark C.1.2.10]
that
\smash{\SW(-)}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}(-)

as functors
\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{lex}}_{\textsf{prest}}\to\textsf{Cat}^{\textsf{ex}}
, i.e. that the bottom face of the diagram commutes as well.
∎

Remark 8.25
.

More generally, one could define
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})
for any category
\mathcal{C}
with finite colimits,
without assuming
\mathcal{C}
is prestable.
To this end, one first passes to the
prestable envelope
of
\mathcal{C}
,
defined as the full subcategory of
\SW(\mathcal{C})
generated under finite colimits and extensions by the essential image of the functor
\mathcal{C}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})
(which need not be fully faithful).
One then applies the above construction to this prestable envelope.

Example 8.26
.

It can happen that
\mathcal{C}
does not have any truncated objects.
For example, we can take the category
\mathcal{C}=\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}_{\geq 0}
of connective, finite spectra.
Then
\SW(\mathcal{C})=\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}
, and hence
\mathcal{C}_{t\leq n}=0
for all
n
,
since non-zero finite spectra are never bounded above in the standard
t
-structure on spectra.
26
26

26

If
X
is a finite
t
-bounded above spectrum, then so is
X/p
for any prime
p
.
It follows that
X/p
lies in the localizing subcategory generated by
\mathbf{F}_{p}
.
By the chromatic convergence theorem, we have
X/p=\lim_{n}L_{n}X/p
.
But
L_{n}\mathbf{F}_{p}=0
for all
n
, which gives
X/p=0
for all
p
,
i.e. that
X
is rational. But finite spectra have finitely generated homotopy groups,
so this yields
X=0
.

It follows that every sequential diagram
c_{\bullet}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\SW(\mathcal{C})=\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}

growing in
t
-coconnectivity must be constant, and hence
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})=\SW(\mathcal{C})
.
Even more extreme, if
\mathcal{C}
is already stable,
then it also doesn’t have any truncated objects and we find that
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})=\SW(\mathcal{C})=\mathcal{C}
.

Example 8.27
.

Let
\mathcal{C}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{\geq 0}
be the category of connective spectra with finitely generated homotopy groups.
This is the connective part of the standard
t
-structure on spectra restricted to the full subcategory

\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
of spectra with finitely generated homotopy groups.
In particular,
\mathcal{C}
is a prestable category admitting finite limits,
with
\SW(\mathcal{C})=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}
and
\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
.

Warning 8.28
.

Even if
\mathcal{C}
is pre-weighted,
so that
(\SW(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
is part of a weight structure,
the connectivity structure
(\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C}),\mathcal{C})
(or
(\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C}),\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{C})_{t\geq 0})
)
can generally fail to be part of a weak weight structure.
A concrete counterexample is given by
\mathcal{C}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{\geq 0}
, see Example
9.4
.

9.
Adjacency of weak weight and weak t-structures

In practice, weight structures often come together with an adjacent
t
-structure,
i.e. one having the same category of connective objects.
In this section we study the analogous interaction between weak weight- and weak
t
-structures, showing in particular that the existence of an adjacent weak
t
-structure is preserved under left weight completion,
but that conversely weight structures are in general not preserved by right
t
-completion.
We introduce the mixed weight- and
t
-completion process
\mathcal{A}\mapsto\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
for additive categories
\mathcal{A}
,
and deduce a reconstruction result (Theorem
H
/ Observation
9.9
) from our previous results.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category. We say that a weak
t
-structure
(\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0})
on
\mathcal{C}
is adjacent to the weak weight structure if

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0}
, in which case we simply denote this by
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
, and

(2)

the weak
t
-structure is left saturated:
\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}={(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}
.

Dually, a weak
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
is co-adjacent if
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0}={}^{\perp}{(\mathcal{C}_{\leq-1})}
.

Remark 9.2
.

If we are considering (non-weak)
t
-structures, condition (2) follows from (1).

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category with adjacent weak
t
-structure
of defect
m
.
Then also the weakly weighted category
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}

admits an adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
m

and the map
\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}
is both weight and
t
-exact.

Proof.

Let
0\leq\ell\leq\infty
be so that
\mathcal{C}
and hence
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}
are weakly
\ell
-weighted.
Since
\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}
is weight exact and the (putative) weak
t
-structures
are adjacent, for
t
-exactness it suffices to check that
t
-coconnective
objects are preserved.
So given
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}
and
X\in(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})_{w\geq 0}
,
we need to show that
\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}}(X,\eta Y)\in\textsf{Sp}_{t\leq 0}
.
Using the explicit description of
(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})_{w\geq 0}
from Theorem
4.9

and the fact that
\textsf{Sp}_{t\leq 0}
is closed under limits,
we reduce to the case that
X\in\mathcal{C}_{w[0,n]}
for some
n<\infty
.
But then Theorem
4.9
(2b) yields that

\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)\simeq\hom_{\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 6.12503pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}}(\eta X,\eta Y),

which is
t
-coconnective since
X\in\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0}
and
Y\in\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}
.

It remains to construct a
t
-decomposition for any
X\in\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}
.
By left density of
\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
in
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}

we find a cofiber sequence
c\to X\to X_{w\geq 2}

with
c\in\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}
and
X_{w\geq 2}\in(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})_{w\geq 2}
.
Picking a
t
-decomposition
c_{w>-m}\to c\to c_{t\leq 0}
,
we see that the composite
\Omega X_{w\geq 2}\to c_{t\leq 0}

vanishes, and hence we can form the diagram of cofiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega X_{w\geq 2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{w>-m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega X_{w\geq 2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{t\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\eta c_{t\leq 0}}}

where
F\in(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})_{w>-m}
,
and so
F\to X\to\eta c_{t\leq 0}
is a
t
-decomposition of defect
m
for
X
.
∎

Unfortunately, the dual assertion, that the weak weight structure is preserved by right
t
-completion, fails completely, as the following example shows.

Example 9.4
.

Let
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
be the category of spectra with finitely generated homotopy groups,
and
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{ft}}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}
the category of finite type spectra,
i.e. bounded below spectra with finitely generated homotopy groups.
We have previously seen that the standard weight structure on
Sp
restricts to
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{ft}}

(and exhibits the latter as the left weight completion of
\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}
).
However, we claim that it does not restrict to a weight structure on
\smash{(\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{ft}})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
,
or even to a weak one.
Indeed, note that
\text{KU}\in\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
. Suppose we have a weight decomposition

A_{w\leq n}\to\text{KU}\to B_{w\geq 0}
for some
0\leq n<\infty
.
Tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}
, we see that
\mathbb{Z}\otimes A_{w\leq n}
is
n
-coconnective,
whereas
\mathbb{Z}\otimes B_{w\geq 0}\in\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{\geq 0}
is connective and still has finitely generated
homotopy groups. However, this means
\pi_{2n+2}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes B_{w\geq 0})=\pi_{2n+2}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes\text{KU})=\mathbb{Q}

is finitely generated, which is absurd.
It seems that the next best category onto which the weight and
t
-structures restrict
is
\textsf{Sp}^{\omega_{1}}
, the category of spectra with countable homotopy groups.

Nevertheless, we can note that in the above example,
the
t
-structure on
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
is so that the bounded below part

\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{ft}}
admits a left complete weight structure.
We will see below that this is generally enough to determine the entire category purely from the weight heart
of the bounded below part, see Observation
9.9
.
The dual notion of a weakly weighted category
where
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
admits an adjacent weak
t
-structure
in fact already guarantees the existence of the adjacent weak
t
-structure on the entire category.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be weakly weighted and let
\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}\coloneqq{(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}
.
If there exists some
0\leq m\leq\infty
such that every
X\in\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}

admits an adjacent
t
-decomposition
X_{w>-m}\to X\to X_{t\leq 0}

of defect
m
, then every
X\in\mathcal{C}
does, so
\mathcal{C}
has an adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
m
.

Proof.

For
X\in\mathcal{C}
there is some
0\leq n<\infty
, a weight decomposition

X_{w\leq n}\to X\to X_{w\geq 1}
and a
t
-decomposition

(X_{w\leq n})_{w>-m}\to X_{w\leq n}\to(X_{w\leq n})_{t\leq 0}
.
Since the map

\Omega X_{w\geq 1}\to(X_{w\leq n})_{t\leq 0}

vanishes, we can build the diagram of cofiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega X_{w\geq 1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X_{w\leq n})_{>-m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{>-m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega X_{w\geq 1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{w\leq n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X_{w\leq n})_{t\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X_{w\leq n})_{t\leq 0}}}

The right vertical cofiber sequence yields the desired
t
-decomposition of defect
m
for
X
.
∎

In the remainder of this section, we investigate stable categories which admit adjacent
weight and weak
t
-structures and are colimit complete for both,
i.e. left complete for the weight structure and right complete for the (weak)
t
-structure.
One of the canonical examples is the unbounded derived category of a Grothendieck abelian category
with enough projectives, but also examples we have already seen above, such as
\textsf{LMod}(R)
for a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring
R
.

Definition 9.6
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category.
Since
\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}
is right bounded, it trivially admits a weak
t
-structure
by Remark
7.3
.
This allows us to define

\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\coloneqq\smash{(\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))

For the last equivalence,
we used that
\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}})
and the definition
of
\textsf{Sp}(-)
from Definition
8.23
.
We also define
\widehat{K}^{\textsf{op}}(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{op}})^{\textsf{op}}
.

Observation 9.7
.

Note that:

(1)

\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
admits a right complete weak
t
-structure,
and
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}\simeq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))

(cf. Proposition
4.7
) admits an adjacent (in that weight- and
t
-connectives agree)
left complete weight structure with weight heart
\mathcal{A}^{\textsf{idem}}
.

(2)

If the left complete weighted category
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}

happens to admit an adjacent
t
-structure, then
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
agrees with the usual
right completion of said
t
-structure.
In fact, in this case it follows that
\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})
is a prestable category
admitting finite limits and
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))

(cf.
[
23
, Proposition C.1.2.9, Remark C.1.2.10]
).

(3)

Using the definition of Spectrum objects of a prestable category
from section
8.2
, we can even write

\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})=\textsf{Sp}(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A}))
in full generality.

Remark 9.8
.

Recall the notation
K(\mathcal{A})
from Definition
5.3

and note that

K(\mathcal{A})_{w>-\infty}\simeq\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})_{t>-\infty}

or equivalently
K(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0}\simeq\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})_{t\geq 0}
.
The definitions of both
K
and
\widehat{K}
then let us write

K(\mathcal{A})\simeq\smash{(\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})_{t>-\infty})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\smash{(K(\mathcal{A})_{w>-\infty})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}(K(\mathcal{A})_{w\geq 0}).

In this sense, we can think of
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
as the right
t
-completion of
K(\mathcal{A})
,
and dually of
K(\mathcal{A})
as the right weight completion of
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
.
Let us note however that for reasons similar
to the discussion around Corollary
8.18

there will generally not exist functors
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})\to K(\mathcal{A})

or
K(\mathcal{A})\to\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
that are completions.
For one,
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})
need not even admit a weak weight structure
as the example of
\mathcal{A}=\{\bigoplus_{\textsf{fin}}\mathbb{S}\}
from Example
9.4
shows
(note
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A})=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
).
Similarly,
K(\mathcal{A})
need not admit an adjacent weak
t
-structure.
Nevertheless, we will see in Section
10

that it can happen that we do have functors in both directions.

Theorem 9.9
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
be a stable connectivity structure
and define
\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0}={}^{\perp}{(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}

as well as
\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}={(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}
.
There is an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})

restricting to
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\simeq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})_{\geq 0}

if and only if

(1)

(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0})
is a right complete
weak
t
-structure,

(2)

(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty},\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}\cap\mathcal{C}_{w\leq 0})

is a left complete weight structure.

Proof.

Given an equivalence
f\colon\mathcal{C}\simeq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})

restricting to an equivalence
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\simeq\widehat{K}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})_{\geq 0}
,
we can simply transfer all the structure from
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})

through this equivalence.

Conversely, suppose that (1) and (2) are satisfied.
By Theorem
5.1
the evident map
\mathcal{C}_{w=0}\to\mathcal{C}

uniquely extends to a weight exact map
\Stab(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})\to\mathcal{C}

which is an equivalence onto
\mathcal{C}_{wb}
.
By Theorem
4.9
,
this uniquely extends to a weight exact equivalence

\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\smash{\mathcal{C}_{wb}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty}
.
Finally, viewing
\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}
as
a trivial weak
t
-category
via Remark
7.3
then lets us use Theorem
7.13

to a
t
-exact equivalence
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})=\smash{(\smash{\Stab(\mathcal{C}_{w=0})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\smash{(\mathcal{C}_{>-\infty})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\mathcal{C}
, as desired.
∎

Example 9.10
.

By Proposition
6.6
, for a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring
R

there is a
t
-exact equivalence

\widehat{K}(\{R\}^{\oplus})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\textsf{LMod}(R)

which restricts to a weight exact equivalence
\smash{\Stab(\{R\}^{\oplus})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\SW(\mathcal{P}^{\Delta^{\textsf{op}}}(\{R\}^{\oplus}))\simeq\textsf{LMod}(R)_{>-\infty}
.

Example 9.11
.

There is a
t
-exact equivalence
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}\simeq\widehat{K}(\{\bigoplus_{\textsf{fin}}\mathbb{S}\})

and similarly
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})^{\textsf{fg}}\simeq\widehat{K}(\{\bigoplus_{\textsf{fin}}\mathbb{Z}\})
.

Example 9.12
.

We will see in Proposition
10.3
that there is also a
t
-exact equivalence

\widehat{K}^{\textsf{op}}(\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus})\simeq\textsf{Sp}

restricting to a weight exact equivalence
\smash{\Stab(\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}_{t<\infty}

where
\textsf{Sp}_{t<\infty}=\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}<\infty}
is equipped with the Anderson weight structure
from Proposition
10.3
.

Recall from Corollary
5.9
that if
\mathcal{A}
is an abelian category with enough projectives,
we can understand the bounded below derived category of
\mathcal{A}

as the left weight completion of
\Stab(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
admits a left complete and right bounded
t
-structure.
Now if
\mathcal{A}
is instead a Grothendieck abelian category,
then there is a model structure on
\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
constructed in

[
22
, Proposition 1.3.5.3]
with underlying equivalences the quasi-isomorphisms
and whose underlying
\infty
-category is the unbounded derived category
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
.
The fibrant replacement functor defines a left Bousfield localization
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

which precisely inverts the quasi-isomorphisms.
By
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.5.21]
the derived category
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
admits a right complete
t
-structure,
which is generally not left complete,
see e.g.
[
28
]
.
However, if
\mathcal{A}
furthermore admits enough projectives, we have the following result.

Proposition 9.13
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a Grothendieck abelian category with enough projectives.
Then we have a
t
-exact equivalence
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}

which exhibits
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
as the right
t
-completion of
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
.
In particular, we have an equivalence
\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})\simeq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
,
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is
t
-complete and left weight complete.

Proof.

By the above discussion,
it only remains to establish the
t
-exact equivalence

\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}
.
The proof is essentially unwinding the definitions of the categories
and their
t
-structures as given in
[
22
, Section 1.3.2,1.3.5]
.

Namely, recall that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})=N_{dg}(\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\circ})\subseteq N_{dg}(\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A}))\eqqcolon\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

where
\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\circ}\subseteq\textsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
is the full subcategory
on (bi-)fibrant objects in the model structure constructed
in
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.5.3]
.
The fibrant replacement yields a left Bousfield localization

L\colon\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

with fully faithful right adjoint
i
.
In particular, the unit of this adjunction is a pointwise quasi-isomorphism
(these are the weak equivalences in the model structure).

Now by
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.5.18]
there is a
t
-structure
on
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
with
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{t\geq 0}

consisting of those complexes with homology concentrated in non-negative
degrees, and dually for
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})_{t\leq 0}
.
Since the unit of the adjunction
L\dashv i

is a pointwise quasi-isomorphism,
it follows that the composite
iL
is
t
-exact.

The
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is descended from
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

in the sense that
X\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is (co)connective
if and only if
iX
is (co)connective.
By the above, it then follows that both
L
and
i
are
t
-exact.
Moreover, the induced
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

is right complete by
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.5.21]
.

We also have an obvious inclusion

j\colon\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\coloneqq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}^{-}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})\subseteq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})
, and the
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})
is defined analogously as for
\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A})

so that
j
is
t
-exact, and it is left complete
by
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.3.16]
.

Finally,
[
22
, Proposition 1.3.5.24]
shows
that the composite
Lj\colon\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

is fully faithful with image
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}
.
By the above, it is also
t
-exact,
and thus induces a
t
-exact equivalence

\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty}
.
Since
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is right complete, we get

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\smash{(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{>-\infty})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\smash{\mathcal{D}^{-}(\mathcal{A})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}

so that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is the right
t
-completion of a left
t
-complete category,
so both left and right
t
-complete.
∎

Corollary 9.14
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a Grothendieck abelian category with enough projectives.
Then

K(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})\simeq\textsf{Ch}_{\infty}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{K}(\mathcal{A}_{\textsf{proj}})\simeq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

10.
Weight structures on Spectra

We saw in Example
6.7
that the standard weight structure on Spectra is left weight complete.
One of the main results of this section is the identification of its (right) weight completion as modules over the Steenrod algebra
A
.
In fact, we will see in Proposition
10.12

that right weight completion lets us pass from
Sp
to
\textsf{LMod}(A)
,
whereas right
t
-completion let us pass back from
\textsf{LMod}(A)
to
Sp
.
Along the way, it will be convenient to introduce another more exotic weight structure on spectra
which is, in a suitable sense, dual to the standard one.
It is
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated by the single object
\mathbb{Z}
in the sense of Theorem
6.3
.
We call it the Anderson weight structure because its weight heart identifies with the full subcategory
\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
on arbitrary sums of the Anderson dual spectrum
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let us begin by recalling the construction of
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
and its relevant properties.

Construction 10.1
.

If
D
is a divisible abelian group
then by the Brown Representability Theorem there exists a spectrum
I_{D}\in\textsf{Sp}

so that we have natural isomorphisms
\pi_{n}\hom(X,I_{D})=\Hom(\pi_{-n}X,D)
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Moreover, this is weakly functorial in that a map of divisible abelian
groups
f\colon D\to D^{\prime}
lifts to a map of spectra
F\colon I_{D}\to I_{D^{\prime}}

so that the map
\pi_{n}\hom(-,F)

corresponds to
\Hom(\pi_{-n}(-),f)
under the above natural isomorphisms, for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Despite its notation, the Anderson dual spectrum
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
does not arise via this construction, as
\mathbb{Z}
is not divisible.
Instead, the above yields a map of spectra
I_{\mathbb{Q}}\to I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}}
which on
\pi_{0}
is the canonical quotient map
\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
,
and we define
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\coloneqq\fib(I_{\mathbb{Q}}\to I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}})
.
Here
I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}}
is the Brown-Comenetz dual spectrum introduced in
[
11
]

and is infamous for its many curious properties.
On the other hand,
I_{\mathbb{Q}}
is simply the Eilenberg MacLane spectrum of
\mathbb{Q}
.
The Anderson dual spectrum was introduced in
[
1
]
to deduce a universal coefficient theorem
for complex
K
-theory.

We will also need the following Lemma.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
J
be a set and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
There is a natural short exact sequence of abelian groups

0\to\Ext^{1}(\pi_{-(n+1)}X,\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z})\to\pi_{n}\hom(X,\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})\to\Hom(\pi_{-n}X,\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z})\to 0

which splits unnaturally. Moreover:

(1)

Under the isomorphism
\pi_{n}\hom(X,\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})\cong\pi_{0}\hom(X,\Omega^{n}\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})
, the second map takes
\pi_{-n}
.

(2)

Let
\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus},\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
denote the full subcategories of
Sp

on sums of spheres respectively sums of
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
’s.
Then the adjunction
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-\dashv\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},-)
restricts to an adjoint equivalence of categories

\Thick(\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus})\simeq\Thick(\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus})
.

Proof.

For
J
a singleton, this is the usual UCT sequence for the Anderson
dual spectrum, see e.g.
[
16
, Lemma 2.4]
.
The case of general
J
is shown in the same way once we know
the natural isomorphisms

\pi_{n}\hom(X,\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Q})\cong\Hom(\pi_{-n}X,\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Q})\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{n}\hom(X,\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}})\cong\Hom(\pi_{-n}X,\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}).

Consider a collection of divisible abelian groups

(D_{j})_{j\in J}
, and lift each of the maps
D_{j}\to\bigoplus_{j}D_{j}

to a map
I_{D_{j}}\to I_{\bigoplus_{j}D_{j}}
.
We claim that together these induce an equivalence

\bigoplus_{j}I_{D_{j}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}I_{\bigoplus_{j}D_{j}}
.
Indeed, we can check this after applying
\pi_{0}\hom(X,-)
for an arbitrary
finite spectrum
X
, where it follows from the following commutative diagram,
where we also use compactness of
\pi_{0}X\in\textsf{Ab}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{j}\pi_{0}\hom(X,I_{D_{j}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}\hom(X,\bigoplus_{j}I_{D_{j}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}\hom(X,I_{\bigoplus_{j}D_{j}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{j}\Hom(\pi_{0}X,D_{j})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Hom(\pi_{0}X,\bigoplus_{j}D_{j})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

The addendum (1) is clear from the construction.
Finally, to see (2), it suffices to check that the adjunction restricts to an adjoint equivalence

\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus}\simeq\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}
.
Note that by the above, for any set
J
and integer
n

we obtain a map of short exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{J}\Ext(\pi_{-(n+1)}I_{\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{J}\pi_{n}\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},I_{\mathbb{Z}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{J}\Hom(\pi_{-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ext(\pi_{-(n+1)}I_{\mathbb{Z}},\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{n}\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Hom(\pi_{-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}},\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the outer vertical maps are isomorphisms since
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
has
finitely generated homotopy groups.
We conclude that the canonical map
\bigoplus_{J}\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},I_{\mathbb{Z}})\to\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})

is an equivalence.
It follows that the adjunction
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-\dashv\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},-)
restricts to the full subcategories

\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus}\rightleftarrows\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}
.
Together with the fact that tensoring with
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
induces the equivalence
\hom(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},I_{\mathbb{Z}})
,
we then conclude that
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-\colon\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus}\to\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}
is fully faithful and hence an equivalence.
∎

Using the above, it is not hard to show that the functor
\hom(-,I_{\mathbb{Z}})\colon\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{op}}\to\textsf{Sp}

restricts to an involution on the full subcategory
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
of spectra with finitely generated
homotopy groups, i.e. we have an equivalence
\hom(-,I_{\mathbb{Z}})\colon(\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}})^{\textsf{op}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
,
see e.g.
[
16
, Theorem 2.9]
.
27
27

27

In fact, in forthcoming work
[
29
]
we show that for
X\in\textsf{Sp}
, the canonical map
X\to\hom(\hom(X,I_{\mathbb{Z}}),I_{\mathbb{Z}})
into the double dual is an equivalence if and
only if

X\in\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}
.

We can now construct the Anderson weight structure on Spectra
mentioned in the introduction,
which uses the case
\kappa=\omega_{1}
of Theorem
6.3
.

Proposition 10.3
.

There is a weight structure
w^{\prime}
on spectra
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated by the single object
\mathbb{Z}
, satisfying

(1)

\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 0}=\{X\in\textsf{Sp}\mid\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}\}
.

(2)

\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}=\{X\in\textsf{Sp}\mid\text{$\pi_{k}(X)=0$ for $k>0$ and $\pi_{0}(X)$ free}\}
.

(3)

\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}=0}=\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}
is the full subcategory of
Sp
on direct sums of
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(4)

The weight structure is right complete.

(5)

\bigcap_{n\geq 0}\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq n}=\{X\mid\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)=0\}
.

We will refer to this weight structure as the Anderson weight structure on spectra.

Proof.

Applying Theorem
6.3
, point (1) is clear.
Let
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
be the full subcategory on
t
-coconnective
spectra with free
\pi_{0}
.
Note that this is closed under finite limits,
extensions, retracts, sums.
The fact that it is also closed under colimits of ordinal-indexed diagrams
as in Lemma
3.10
(4) follows from Lemma
A.6
.
Since
\mathbb{Z}\in\mathcal{C}
, we thus get
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
.
For the reverse inclusion,
we simply note that if
A
is an abelian group,
then
A[-1]
lies in
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
as fiber of a map
between sums of
\mathbb{Z}
’s.
By induction, we see that any
(-1)
-t-coconnective
and bounded below spectrum lies in
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
.
Since
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
is also closed under sequential colimits
of maps whose cofiber lies in
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
,
we conclude that if
X
is
(-1)
-t-coconnective,
then
X=\colim_{n}\tau_{\geq-n}X\in\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
.
From this we deduce
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
,
and thus (2). This immediately yields (5),
and (4) by combining Remark
2.24

and (the dual of) Lemma
2.25
.

It remains to show (3).
Note that the standard
t
-structure on spectra lets us conclude
that
\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)
is
t
-coconnective whenever
X
is
t
-coconnective.
But now if
X\in\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}=0}
, then
\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)
is concentrated in a single degree,
where it is given by the free abelian group

\pi_{0}\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)\simeq\pi_{0}\map(\mathbb{Z},X)\simeq\pi_{0}\map(\mathbb{Z},\pi_{0}X)\cong\pi_{0}X.

Next, we can deduce from Lemma
10.2
that
\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}=0}
.
Now pick an isomorphism
\pi_{0}X\cong\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z}
.
By Lemma
10.2
we have a split short exact sequence

0\to\Ext(\pi_{-1}(X),\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z})\to\pi_{0}\hom(X,\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})\to\Hom(\pi_{0}X,\bigoplus_{J}\mathbb{Z})\to 0

we then find a map of spectra
f\colon X\to\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}}

which is a
\pi_{0}
-isomorphism.
We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}\hom(\mathbb{Z},\pi_{0}X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}\hom(\mathbb{Z},\pi_{0}\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}\hom(\mathbb{Z},\bigoplus_{J}I_{\mathbb{Z}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{0}f)_{*},\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}

showing that
\hom(\mathbb{Z},f)
is a
\pi_{0}
-isomorphism.
But by the above argument, both source and target of this map
are concentrated in degree
0
, so
\hom(\mathbb{Z},\fib(f))=0
.
But then
\fib(f)\in\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}\cap\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 1}=0
,
so
f
is an equivalence, as desired.
∎

We note that
\bigcap_{n\geq 0}\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq n}=\{X\mid\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)=0\}
contains for example all finite
p
-complete spectra
28
28

28

Suppose
X
is finite
p
-complete. By chromatic convergence,
we have
\hom(\mathbf{F}_{p},X)=\lim_{n}\hom(\mathbf{F}_{p},L_{n}X)=\lim_{n}\hom(L_{n}\mathbf{F}_{p},L_{n}X)=0
, since
L_{n}\mathbf{F}_{p}=0
for all
n
.
Since
\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)
is
p
-complete, this shows it vanishes.
.
So this weight structure is very different from the standard one.

Corollary 10.4
.

Let
X\in\textsf{Sp}
be
b
-coconnective and suppose that
\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)
is

a
-connective for integers
a\leq b
.
Then
X\in\textsf{Sp}_{a\leq w^{\prime}\leq b+1}
, so
X
admits a finite filtration
with subquotients equivalent to shifts of direct sums of
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
’s.
If
X
has moreover finitely generated homotopy groups,
we can pick these sums to be finite,
and in particular
X
lies in the thick subcategory generated by
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Corollary 10.5
.

The functor
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-\colon\textsf{Sp}\to\textsf{Sp}

restricts to
\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}\to\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
,
and its right adjoint
\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},-)

restricts to
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 0}\to\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}
.
Both further restrict to mutually inverse weight exact equivalences

I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-\colon\textsf{Sp}_{-\infty<w<\infty}\simeq\textsf{Sp}_{-\infty<w^{\prime}<\infty}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},-)
.

Proof.

The last point is immediate from Lemma
10.2
(2)
and the characterisations of the weight hearts of the standard
respectively Anderson weight structure as
\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus}

respectively
\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus}
.

Suppose
X\in\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}
, so that
\mathbb{Z}\otimes X
is coconnective
with free
\pi_{0}
.
Then
A\otimes X
is
1
-
t
-coconnective for any
A\in\textsf{Ab}
,
and hence if
Y
has homotopy groups concentrated in degrees
[-n,0]
,
also
Y\otimes X
is 1-
t
-coconnective.
We conclude that
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X=\colim_{n}\tau_{\geq-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X

is a sequential colimit growing in
t
-coconnectivity
of 1-coconnective spectra. Thus
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X
is 1-coconnective,
and
\tau_{[0,1]}(I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X)=\tau_{[0,1]}(\tau_{\geq-1}I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X)
.
However
\tau_{\geq-1}I_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathbb{Z}
, and so
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X
is indeed
t
-coconnective
with free
\pi_{0}
, hence Anderson-coconnective.

Now let
X\in\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 0}
, so that
\hom(\mathbb{Z},X)
is connective.
Then
\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},X)=\lim_{n}\hom(\tau_{\geq-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}},X)

and an analogous analysis as in the first point shows that this is a sequential
limit growing in connectivity of connective spectra, hence connective.
∎

Remark 10.6
.

As in the standard weight structure on
Sp
(cf. Remark
6.8
), point (5) of Proposition
10.3
is not the only reason that the Anderson weight structure is not left complete,
so we cannot just quotient out
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq\infty}

to guarantee left completeness by Lemma
4.24
.

To see this, recall that
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathbf{F}_{p}=0
.
Indeed, by the defining fiber sequence
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{Q}\to I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}}
,
this follows from
I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathbf{F}_{p}=0
, which was shown in
[
19
, Lemma 7.1]
.
Since
\mathbf{F}_{p}
is of finite type, we can pick a weight complex
for
\mathbf{F}_{p}
in the standard weight structure of
Sp

which exhibits
\mathbf{F}_{p}=\colim_{n}X_{n}

for finite spectra
X_{n}
where
\cofib(X_{n}\to X_{n+1})
is a finite sum
of
\mathbb{S}^{n+1}
.
But then
X_{\bullet}\otimes I_{\mathbb{Z}}
is a sequential diagram
of Anderson-connective spectra growing in Anderson-connectivity
whose colimit is 0.
In particular, for any
k\geq 1
, we have that

(I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X_{n}/X_{k})_{n\geq k}
is a sequential diagram
in
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq k+1}
which is growing in Anderson-connectivity,
but whose colimit is
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\Sigma X_{k}
.
Since
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-
is a weight exact equivalence
from standard-weight-bounded spectra to Anderson-weight-bounded spectra,
and
\Sigma X_{k}
is 1-connective but not 2-connective,
we see that also
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes X_{k}
lies in
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 1}

but not in
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 2}
by Corollary
10.5
, since
\Sigma X_{k}
is weight bounded.
By shifting, this yields for every
k\geq 1
an example
of a sequential diagram growing in Anderson-connectivity
of Anderson-connective spectra whose colimit is not
(-k)
-Anderson-connective.

Remark 10.7
.

We can use Remarks
6.8
and
10.6

to see that Corollary
10.5
is in some sense optimal;

I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-
does not restrict to
\textsf{Sp}_{w\geq 0}\to\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq 0}

and
\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},-)
does not restrict to
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}\to\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}
.
For the former, using the notation of Remark
10.6

we see that
\mathbf{F}_{p}/X_{k}
is
(k+1)
-connective, but
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathbf{F}_{p}/X_{k}\simeq I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\Sigma X_{k}

is only Anderson 1-connective, so
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-
does not restrict to
\textsf{Sp}_{w\geq 0}\to\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\geq-k}
for any
k
.
For the latter, we note that
X=\prod_{n\geq 0}I_{\mathbb{Z}}[-n]\in\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}
but
\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},X)=\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{S}[-n]
is not even weight bounded above in the standard weight structure on spectra by the other remark.

Corollary 10.8
.

The Anderson weight structure restricts to the full subcategory
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}

of bounded above spectra with finitely generated homotopy groups.
Taking Anderson duals gives a weight-exact equivalence
(\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty})^{\textsf{op}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}
,
and this allows us to exhibit
\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}

and hence also the composite

I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-\colon\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}=\Thick(\mathbb{S})\simeq\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}

as right completions.
Dually, the composite
\hom(I_{\mathbb{Z}},-)\colon\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq\Thick(\mathbb{S})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}

is a left completion.

Proof.

We saw in Example
4.22

that
\Thick(\mathbb{S})=\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}
is a left completion.
Since Anderson duality restricts to an involution
(\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}})^{\textsf{op}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}

of
t
-amplitude
[-1,0]
, we obtain compatible equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Thick(\mathbb{S})^{\textsf{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty})^{\textsf{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Now
\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})
agrees with the stable envelope generated by the additive category
consisting of finite sums of
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and hence the inclusion
\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
