is weight exact.
The top horizontal map is induced by the functoriality of
\Stab(-)
hence also weight exact.
It follows that if we define a weight structure on
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}

via transferring the one on
(\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty})^{\textsf{op}}
,
then the right vertical map is a right completion.
Since
Sp
with the Anderson structure is already right complete,
we obtain an induced weight exact
f\colon\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}\to\textsf{Sp}

which restricts to the inclusion on
\Thick(I_{\mathbb{Z}})
.
By Corollary
4.14

also
f
is fully faithful and clearly identifies with the obvious inclusion.
In particular, the “transferred” weight structure on
\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{t<\infty}

is restricted from the Anderson weight structure,
so all the claims follow from the above.
∎

We thank Vova Sosnilo for a fruitful discussion from which the following result emerged.
Let
\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
denote the full subcategory consisting of those spectra
with only finitely many nontrivial homotopy groups, each of which is finitely generated.
Equivalently, this is the thick subcategory of
Sp
generated by
\mathbb{Z}
.
Since
\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}))
is compactly generated, we can use Theorem
6.3

to endow it with the weight structure compactly generated by the single object
j\mathbb{Z}
.

Proposition 10.9
.

The inclusion
\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})\to\textsf{Sp}

\Ind
-extends to a functor
L\colon\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})\to\textsf{Sp}

whose right adjoint
R\colon\textsf{Sp}\to\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
is a left completion for the Anderson weight structure on spectra.
Under the equivalence
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}},\textsf{Sp})

we have
RX=\hom(-,X)|_{\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}}}
.

Proof.

Let us begin by showing that
R
is fully faithful on
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}<\infty}=\textsf{Sp}_{t<\infty}
,
or equivalently that the counit
\varepsilon\colon LR\Rightarrow\id

is an equivalence on
X\in\textsf{Sp}_{t<\infty}
.
It is fully faithful on
\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
by definition.
Moreover, we claim that
R
and hence both source and target of
\varepsilon

preserve uniformly bounded above filtered colimits.
Using the explicit description of
R
,
this reduces to showing that
\hom_{\textsf{Sp}}(\mathbb{Z},-)
preserves uniformly bounded above filtered colimits,
which is an instance of Lemma
2.34
.
So to conclude that
\varepsilon
is an equivalence on
\textsf{Sp}_{t<\infty}
,
it remains to observe that it is generated by
\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
under such filtered colimits;
one first obtains arbitrary Eilenberg–Mac Lane spectra,
and then uses the Whitehead tower to obtain any bounded above spectrum.

As a next step,
we note that since
R\mathbb{Z}=j\mathbb{Z}
and
Lj\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}

and both
L
and
R
preserve all the operations
under which the weight coconnectives are generated
from
\mathbb{Z}
resp.
j\mathbb{Z}
(Theorem
6.3
(2)),
it follows that both
L
and
R
preserve weight coconnectives.
We now deduce via Lemma
3.12
(2) that
R
also preserves
connectives, so
R
is weight exact.

Since
R\colon\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}\hookrightarrow\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})_{w\leq 0}
,
hits the generator, and preserves the relevant operations,
we even get that it is an equivalence
\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}\leq 0}\simeq\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})_{w\leq 0}
.

In view of Corollary
4.20
it remains to see that
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})

is left complete, but this is an instance of Theorem
6.3
(6),
as
\mathbb{Z}
is a compact generator of
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
.
∎

Corollary 10.10
.

The above weight structure on
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})

is both left and right complete.

Proof.

It is the left completion of a right complete
weight structure, so we can use Proposition
4.16
.
∎

The last result also shows that

\smash{\Stab(\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\updownarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\updownarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\updownarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\updownarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\smash{(\smash{\Stab(\{I_{\mathbb{Z}}\}^{\oplus})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\smash{(\textsf{Sp}_{w^{\prime}<\infty})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$\hss}\uparrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}).

The image of
I_{\mathbb{Z}}
under these equivalences is
RI_{\mathbb{Z}}=\hom(-,I_{\mathbb{Z}})|_{\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}}}
, which corresponds to

I_{\bullet}\coloneqq``\colim_{n}"\tau_{\geq-n}I_{\mathbb{Z}}

under the equivalence
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})\simeq\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}},\textsf{Sp})
.

Corollary 10.11
.

The colimit preserving functor

I_{\bullet}\otimes-\colon\textsf{Sp}\to\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})

exhibits
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
as the right weight completion of
Sp

with respect to the standard weight structure
w
.
In particular (cf. the dual of Theorem
4.9
(2b)),
it is an equivalence on bounded below objects:

\textsf{Sp}_{>-\infty}\simeq\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})_{>-\infty}.

Proof.

By the above, and Corollary
10.5

we see that
I_{\bullet}
is weight exact and an equivalence on hearts.
Since both source and target are already left complete,
Lemma
4.6
(2) shows that the map is also an equivalence on weight bounded below objects.
We conclude by the dual of Corollary
4.20
.
∎

Anderson Duality induces an involution
\hom(-,I_{\mathbb{Z}})\colon\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}}\simeq\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
, which in turn induces an involution

\Phi\colon\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}})\simeq\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})

which sends
j\mathbb{Z}\mapsto j\mathbb{Z}
. In particular, if we also endow

\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}})
with the weight structure compactly
generated by
j\mathbb{Z}
, then
\Phi
is weight exact.
In particular, also the composite
\Phi^{-1}\circ(I_{\bullet}\otimes-)

is a right weight completion.
Under the identification
\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}})=\Pro(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}))^{\textsf{op}}

we have that
\Phi^{-1}(I_{\bullet})=``\lim_{n}"j\tau_{\leq n}\mathbb{S}
.
We can also describe the composite functor
\Phi^{-1}\circ(I_{\bullet}\otimes-)
as

\textsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{I_{\bullet}\otimes-}\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\Phi^{-1}}\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})^{\textsf{op}})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{}\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}),\textsf{Sp}),\ X\mapsto(C\mapsto X\otimes C)

Upon identifying
\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}),\textsf{Sp})
with left modules over the “integral Steenrod algebra”
A\coloneqq\hom(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})\in\Alg(\textsf{Sp})

by restriction to the generator
\mathbb{Z}\in\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
,
the above composite functor becomes taking homology.

Proposition 10.12
.

The category
\textsf{LMod}(A)
of left modules over the integral Steenrod
algebra admits a weight structure such that

(1)

The equivalence
\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}))\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A)

is weight exact.
In particular, the weight structure on
\textsf{LMod}(A)

is compactly generated by
A
.

(2)

\textsf{LMod}(A)_{w\geq 0}
consists of the
A
-modules
which are underlying connectives.

(3)

The functor
\mathbb{Z}\otimes-\colon\textsf{Sp}\to\textsf{LMod}(A)

is a right weight completion,
and in particular restricts to an equivalence

\textsf{Sp}_{w>-\infty}\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A)_{w>-\infty}

with inverse given by the right adjoint
\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},-)
.

(4)

Since the weight structure is compactly generated, it admits an adjacent
t
-structure.
With respect to this, the right adjoint
\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},-)\colon\textsf{LMod}(A)\to\textsf{Sp}

is a right
t
-completion. Hence

\smash{\textsf{Sp}}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$w$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A)\quad\text{and}\quad\smash{\textsf{LMod}(A)}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$t$\hss}\downarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 7.60004pt$t$\hss}\downarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}\simeq\textsf{Sp}.

In particular, we also have
\widehat{K}(\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus})\simeq\textsf{Sp}

and
K(\{\mathbb{S}\}^{\oplus})\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A)
,
and the above equivalences are an instance of Remark
9.8
where we do have comparison functors in both directions.

Proof.

The image of
\mathbb{S}
under the composite map
\textsf{Sp}\to\textsf{Fun}^{\textsf{ex}}(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}),\textsf{Sp})\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A)
is precisely
\mathbb{Z}
,
and the image of
j\mathbb{Z}
under the equivalence

\Ind(\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp}))\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A)
is precisely
A
.
Hence, transferring the weight structure through these equivalences,
we recognize it as being compactly generated by
A
.
From this points (1)-(3) follow.
To see (4), we need to argue that
\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},-)
is
t
-exact.
By adjacency of the
t
-structures and the weight-exact equivalence from (3)
we see that both
\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},-)
and its left adjoint
\mathbb{Z}\otimes-
preserve
t
-connectives.
The analogue of Lemma
3.12
(2)
shows that
\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},-)
is then actually
t
-exact,
and we deduce from Corollary
7.21
that it is a right
t
-completion.
∎

One can also formulate Proposition
10.9
in a similar manner by identifying
\Ind\mathrm{Coh}(\textsf{Sp})
with right modules
\text{{RMod}}(A)\simeq\textsf{LMod}(A^{\textsf{op}})
.
Under this identification the right completion functor
of Proposition
10.12
becomes

\textsf{Sp}\to\text{{RMod}}(A)\qquad X\mapsto\hom(\mathbb{Z},X).

The Anderson duality equivalence
\Phi
then becomes the observation that we have an equivalence of algebras

A\simeq A^{\textsf{op}}
induced by applying the functoriality of Anderson duality to
A\coloneqq\hom(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})

using that
\mathbb{Z}
is Anderson selfdual.

Remark 10.13
.

For any ring spectrum
R
, there is a natural weight structure on
\textsf{LMod}(R)
whose connective objects are those whose underlying spectra are connective; this structure is compactly generated by
R
itself. If
R
is coconnective, then the weight coconnectives are contained in the underlying coconnectives, but are not so easy to describe explicitly (apart from being generated from
R
under operations). Moreover, this weight structure admits an adjacent
t
-structure, since the connective objects are closed under colimits and extensions. Note that this is in a sense dual to the more commonly used
t
-structure in which coconnective objects are those whose underlying spectra are coconnective (see e.g.
[
12
]
).

However, it is in general difficult to describe the
t
-coconnective objects or the associated connective cover functor. In some cases, the situation even degenerates: if
R
admits a unit in positive degree, then the only connective object is zero. On the other hand, if
R
is connective, this recovers the standard
t
-structure.

We now consider the situation of Proposition
10.12
, where
R=A
is the integral Steenrod algebra, which is coconnective. In this case the connective cover functor admits a concrete description. Namely, it is given by

M\;\mapsto\;\mathbb{Z}\otimes\bigl(\tau_{\geq 0}\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},M)\bigr).

In particular we find that the
t
-coconnective objects are those
M
such that
\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},M)
is connective.

To see this formula, one uses the adjunction
\mathbb{Z}\otimes-\colon\textsf{Sp}\rightleftarrows\textsf{LMod}(A)\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\hom_{A}(\mathbb{Z},-)

together with the fact that every connective object in
\textsf{LMod}(A)
is of the form
\mathbb{Z}\otimes X
for some connective spectrum
X
.

Remark 10.14
.

In Corollary
10.5
we saw that the functor

I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-:\textsf{Sp}\to\textsf{Sp}

restricts to an equivalence on weight bounded objects, where the source is equipped with the
standard weight structure and the target with the Anderson weight structure. However, this functor is not weight exact in general (see Remark
10.7
). If it were weight exact, it would induce an equivalence on weight completions fitting into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LMod}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{RMod}(A).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\otimes-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{hom}(\mathbb{Z}{,}-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces?}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

However the dashed arrow does not exist (see the computation for
\mathbb{Z}\in\textsf{Sp}
below).
On the other hand, there is an equivalence of weight completions

\Psi\colon\textsf{LMod}(A)\simeq\text{{RMod}}(A),

induced by the equivalence
A\simeq A^{\textsf{op}}
, which acts as the identity on underlying spectra. In fact we obtain a lax commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LMod}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{RMod}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\otimes-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{hom}(\mathbb{Z}{,}{-})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\not\simeq}

which strongly commutes on weight bounded objects, but not in general.
Namely, for
\mathbb{Z}\in\textsf{Sp}
we have

\Psi(\mathbb{Z}\otimes\mathbb{Z})\not\simeq\mathrm{hom}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}\otimes I_{\mathbb{Z}}),

since the target is equivalent to
\mathbb{Q}
whereas the source is not. Still we want to think of the equivalence
\Psi
as the ‘correct’ extension of Corollary
10.5
.
It is natural to ask whether this square can nevertheless be understood as arising from the functor
I_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes-
, despite its failure to be weight exact– more precisely, whether non-weight-exact (or more generally non-weight-bounded) functors can still induce functors on weight completions in some weaker sense. At present, we do not know a satisfactory answer to this question.

11.
Weight and
t
-structures on Tate categories

In this section we apply our results to Tate categories.
We show that if
\mathcal{C}
is a weakly weighted category,
then
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
always admits a weight structure
whose heart recovers Drinfeld’s Tate vector spaces for
\mathcal{C}=\Perf(k)

where
k
is a field, cf.
[
14
]
.
Moreover, we give sufficient conditions
under which there exists an adjacent weak
t
-structure
on
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
or its left completion,
as well as a concrete example showing that generally
one can only hope for a
weak
adjacent
t
-structure
(Example
11.11
).
The motivation is that
\smash{\Tate(\mathcal{C})}^{\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow$}}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$w$}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\raisebox{-3.18884pt}{\hskip 7.17496pt$t$}\hss}\updownarrow$}}\mkern 12.0mu}}=\widehat{K}(\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{w=0})

yields an interesting category of Tate objects which is entirely
determined by the weight heart, as we saw in Section
9
.
In forthcoming work
[
29
]
, for certain algebraic examples of
\mathcal{C}
such as
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})^{\omega}
,
we relate this to a full subcategory of condensed
\Ind
-objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
Let us begin by recalling the notion of Tate objects and Tate categories.

Definition 11.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small stable category.
We let
\Tate(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\Ind(\Pro(\mathcal{C}))

denote the smallest stable category spanned by
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
and
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
.

This was investigated by Hennion
[
18
]
, although we warn
the reader that he worked in an idempotent complete context,
and he would refer to our Tate objects as elementary Tate objects.

Proposition 11.2

(
[
18
, Theorem 3]
)
.

The category
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
admits an equivalent description
as the full subcategory of
\Ind(\Pro(\mathcal{C}))

on those objects
X
which sit in an extension

X^{p}\to X\to X^{i}
, where
X^{p}\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})
and
X^{i}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category.
Recall from Proposition
4.25
that
\Ind(\mathcal{C})

attains an induced weight structure compactly generated by
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
.
Dually,
\Pro(\mathcal{C})=\Ind(\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}})^{\textsf{op}}
attains a weight structure
cocompactly cogenerated by
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
, in that

\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}=\{X\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})\mid\hom(X,c)\in\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}\text{ for }c\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}\}

and
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
is the smallest class containing
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}

and closed under finite colimits, extensions, retracts, products and sequential
limits along maps whose fiber already lies in the class.

Theorem 11.3
.

For a weakly
\ell
-weighted
\mathcal{C}
,
there exists a weight structure on
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
such that:

(1)

The inclusions
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}),\Pro(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\Tate(\mathcal{C})
are weight exact.

(2)

For
-\infty\leq a\leq b\leq\infty
where at least one of
a,b
is finite,
the category
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{[a,b]}
is the retract-closure
29
29

29

This retract-closure
is taken in
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
, which generally does not have many retracts.

of the full subcategory
on those Tate objects
X^{p}\to X\to X^{i}
with
X^{p}\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{[a,b]}

and
X^{i}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{[a,b]}
.

(3)

For every
X\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})
there is a weight decomposition
X_{\leq 0}\to X\to X_{\geq 1}

where both
X_{\leq 0}
and
X_{\geq 1}
are actually (not just retracts of) extensions

X_{\leq 0}^{p}\to X_{\leq 0}\to X_{\leq 0}^{i}
and
X_{\geq 1}^{p}\to X_{\geq 1}\to X_{\geq 1}^{i}
.

(4)

If
\ell=0
, then
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{=0}

is the retract-closure of the full subcategory
on Tate-objects of the form
\bigoplus_{i}c_{i}\oplus\prod_{j}d_{j}

with
c_{i},d_{j}\in\mathcal{C}_{=0}
(where we view
\bigoplus_{i}c_{i}
as
\Ind
- and
\prod_{j}d_{j}
as
\Pro
-object).

(5)

If
\mathcal{D}
is another weakly weighted category
and
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
an exact functor of weight amplitude
[a,b]
,
then
\Tate(f)\colon\Tate(\mathcal{C})\to\Tate(\mathcal{D})

is of weight amplitude
[a-\ell,b+\ell]
,
and similarly for the (half)-open intervals.

Proof.

We define
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
and
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
so that (2) is satisfied
when
a
or
b
is infinite,
which automatically guarantees (1).
Now let
X_{\leq 0}\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
and
Y_{\geq 0}\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
. We need to show that
\hom(X_{\leq 0},Y_{\geq 0})
is connective.
Since this is closed under retracts and extensions in both variables,
we may reduce to the cases where
X_{\leq 0}
and
Y_{\geq 0}

are pure
\Pro
- or
\Ind
-objects. The two cases where they are both
\Pro
-
or both
\Ind
-objects follow from the weight structures on
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
and
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
.
Writing
X^{p}_{\leq 0}=``\lim_{i}"c_{i,\leq 0}
and
Y^{i}_{\geq 0}=``\colim_{j}"d_{j,\geq 0}
, we have

\hom_{\Tate(\mathcal{C})}(X^{p}_{\leq 0},Y_{\geq 0}^{i})\simeq\colim_{i,j}\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{i,\leq 0},d_{j,\geq 0})

which is a filtered colimit of connective spectra, hence connective.
The remaining case is
\hom(X^{i}_{\leq 0},Y^{p}_{\geq 0})
.
But the class of
X^{i}_{\leq 0}\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}

for which this hom spectrum is connective
is closed under the operations by which
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}

is generated from
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
(cf. Proposition
4.25
).
Since
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
is also contained in this class
by the weight structure on
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
,
we see that the class agrees with all of
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
.
This concludes the proof of orthogonality of
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}

and
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}
.

Next, we need to verify that weight decompositions exist.
So let
X\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})
sit in an extension
X^{p}\to X\to X^{i}
.
Using the weight structures on
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
and
\Ind(\mathcal{C})
,
we pick weight decompositions
X^{i}_{\leq 0}\to X^{i}\to X^{i}_{\geq 1}

and
X^{p}_{\leq 0}\to X^{p}\to X^{p}_{\geq 1}
.
By the orthogonality we just verified,
the composite map
X^{i}_{\leq 0}\to\Sigma X^{p}_{\geq 1}
vanishes,
so that we obtain factorizations as indicated in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq 1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{\geq 1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{\geq 1}}}

where all sequences are (defined to be) fiber sequences.
This gives extensions
X^{p}_{\leq 0}\to X_{\leq 0}\to X^{i}_{\leq 0}

showing
X_{\leq 0}\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}
,
and dually
X_{\geq 1}\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 1}
,
so that
X_{\leq 0}\to X\to X_{\geq 1}
is a weight decomposition
in
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
. This also proves (3).

To check (2) for finite
a
and
b

(the case where one is infinite being true by definition),
it suffices to consider the case

[a,b]=[0,n]
by shifting.
Then given
X_{[0,n]}\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{[0,n]}
we can assume (up to retracts)
that
X_{[0,n]}
admits an extension

X^{p}_{\geq 0}\to X_{[0,n]}\to X^{i}_{\geq 0}
.
As above we can pick weight decompositions

X^{p}_{[0,n]}\to X^{p}_{\geq 0}\to X^{p}_{\geq n+1}

and analogously for
X^{i}_{\geq 0}
,
and form the diagram of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{[0,n]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{[0,n]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{[0,n]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{\geq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{\geq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\geq n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{\geq n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{\geq n+1}}}

The bottom map in the left vertical fiber sequence vanishes
by orthogonality, which exhibits
X_{[0,n]}

as retract of
F
, which in turn sits in an extension

X^{p}_{[0,n]}\to F\to X^{i}_{[0,n]}
. This proves (2).

To see (4), by (2) we know that if
X_{=0}\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{=0}

then it is a retract of some
Y_{=0}
which admits
an extension
Y^{p}_{=0}\to Y_{=0}\to Y^{i}_{=0}
.
But since
Y^{i}_{=0}\to\Sigma Y^{p}_{=0}
necessarily vanishes,
this fiber sequence splits, and we get
Y_{=0}\simeq Y^{p}_{=0}\oplus Y^{i}_{=0}
. We conclude by the descriptions of
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{=0}
and
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{=0}
in the case of
\ell=0
, using Proposition
4.25
(4) and its dual.

Finally, (5) follows from (2), the fact that
\Tate(f)
is exact,
and
\Ind(f)
respectively
\Pro(f)
having weight amplitude

[a-\ell,b]
respectively
[a,b+\ell]
by Proposition
4.25
(6) and its dual. The argument for (half-)open intervals is analogous.
∎

Corollary 11.4
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a weighted category equipped with a weight exact equivalence
D\colon\mathcal{C}^{\textsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{C}
,
then this extends to a weight exact equivalence
\Tate(D)\colon\Tate(\mathcal{C})^{\textsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Tate(\mathcal{C})

which yields a commutative diagram of weight exact functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C})^{\textsf{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Tate(\mathcal{C})^{\textsf{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C})^{\textsf{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Tate(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Ind(\mathcal{C})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

In other words, the extended equivalence acts on extensions as

``\lim_{i}"c_{i}\to X\to``\colim_{j}"d_{j}\qquad\mapsto\qquad``\lim_{j}"Dd_{j}\to DX\to``\colim_{i}"Dc_{i}.

Lemma 11.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly
\ell
-weighted category for some
\ell<\infty
.
Then the map
\eta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}

induces equivalences

\smash{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\uparrow}
of amplitude
[0,\ell]

and

\smash{\Tate(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\uparrow}
of amplitude
[-\ell,\ell]
.
30
30

30

The analogous statement that there is an amplitude
[-\ell,0]
equivalence
\smash{\Ind(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{\Ind(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\uparrow}
is less interesting but also true by Proposition
4.25
.

In particular, the left completion of
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
resp.
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
,
only depends on
\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
resp.
\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
up to equivalence in
wWCat
(or
\textsf{wWCat}_{=0}
in the case
\ell=0
).

Proof.

Recall from Theorem
4.9
(2) that
\eta_{<\infty}\colon\mathcal{C}_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}

is a fully faithful weight exact functor with dense image.
It follows that the inclusion induces an equivalence

\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{\leq n})\simeq\Pro((\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq n})
for every
n
.
From this we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{\leq\ell})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{\leq\ell}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{\leq 2\ell})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro((\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq 0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro((\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq\ell})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq\ell}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pro((\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq 2\ell})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical maps are induced by
\eta
,
and the horizontal inclusions come from the dual
of Proposition
4.25
(3).
So the functor
\Pro(\eta)
is of amplitude
[0,\ell]
(by the dual of Proposition
4.25
(6))
and restricts to an equivalence
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}
.
By Corollary
4.13
this yields an equivalence

\smash{\Pro(\eta)}^{\uparrow}\colon\smash{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\uparrow}
of amplitude
[0,\ell]
.

Similarly, since
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq 0}

is a dense full subcategory, the concrete description of
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}

as generated by
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}

immediately yields that the induced map

\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\leq 0}\to\Ind(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{\leq 0}

is an equivalence.
Shifting and taking the union, this yields
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\simeq\Ind(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}
.

By exactness of
\Tate(\eta)
, the description of
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}

from Theorem
11.3
(2), and the cases
of
\Pro
and
\Ind
above,
\eta
induces
a fully faithful dense inclusion
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\hookrightarrow\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}
.
Since
\Tate(\eta)
also has amplitude
[-\ell,\ell]
by Theorem
11.3
,
Corollary
4.13

shows that it remains to check that
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}\subseteq\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{<\infty}

is also left dense.
To this end, let
X\in\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
and
n\geq 0
.
By Theorem
11.3

we can construct a weight decomposition

X_{\leq n}\to X\to X_{\geq n+1}

with an extension
X^{p}_{\leq n}\to X_{\leq n}\to X^{i}_{\leq n}
.
Now
\Tate(\eta)
restricts to an equivalence on weight bounded above
pure
\Ind
- or
\Pro
-objects, and hence
X^{p}_{\leq n},X^{i}_{\leq n}

lie in its essential image. Since it is also fully faithful (and exact)
on this subcategory, also
X_{\leq n}
lies in the image of
\Tate(\eta)
.
Thus
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{<\infty}
is left dense in
\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
,
and we conclude as mentioned above.
Finally, the last statement follows from Lemma
3.14
(2),
where in the case
\ell=0
we note that all the weight structures are saturated
anyways so we can instead use Lemma
3.12
(3)
to deduce that the inverse equivalences are also weight exact.
∎

Next, we investigate under what conditions one can expect an adjacent
weak
t
-structure on
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
.

Theorem 11.6

(
[
26
, Lemma 4.1, Theorem 5.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a countable stable category,
meaning
\mathcal{C}
has a countable set of isomorphism classes of objects,
and for any two objects there are only countably many homotopy
classes of maps between them.
Then every object
X\in\Ind(\mathcal{C})
sits in a cofiber sequence

\bigoplus_{j}d_{j}\to\bigoplus_{i}c_{i}\to X

where
d_{j},c_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 11.7
.

If
R
is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring such that
\pi_{*}R
is countable, then
\Perf(R)
is countable stable.

Proposition 11.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly weighted category with adjacent weak
t
-structure
of defect
m<\infty
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
is countable.
Then
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
admits an adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
2m+1
.
Moreover, we have inclusions
\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0})\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 2m+1})
and the inclusion
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C})
is weight- and
t
-exact.

Proof.

Recall that by Lemma
9.5
it is enough to produce

t
-decompositions of the desired defect for objects in
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{w<\infty}\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})
(the inclusion holds by the dual of Proposition
4.25
(3)).
Given such an
X\in\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty})
,
we note that by the dual of Theorem

11.6
there exists a fiber sequence

X\to\prod_{i}c^{i}\to\prod_{j}d^{j}

with
c^{i},d^{j}\in\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
.
Pick
t
-decompositions
c^{i}_{w>-m}\to c^{i}\to c^{i}_{t\leq 0}

for all
i
and
d^{j}_{w>-2m}\to d^{j}\to d^{j}_{t\leq-m}
for all
j
.
Now

\hom_{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}(\prod_{i}c^{i}_{w>-m},\prod_{j}d^{j}_{t\leq-m})=\prod_{j}\bigoplus_{i}\hom_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{i}_{w>-m},d^{j}_{t\leq-m})

is
(-1)
-coconnective since
\mathcal{C}_{w>-m}=\mathcal{C}_{t>-m}

and coconnective spectra are closed under products and sums.
Hence we may construct the diagram of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{w>-(2m+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i}c^{i}_{w>-m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{j}d^{j}_{w>-2m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i}c^{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{j}d^{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{t\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i}c^{i}_{t\leq 0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{j}d^{j}_{t\leq-m}}}

Once we show that
\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0})\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}={(\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 1})}^{\perp}

it will follow that the left vertical fiber sequence
is a
t
-decomposition of defect
2m+1
for
X
,
establishing the claimed adjacent weak
t
-structure on
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
.
Since
t
-coconnective spectra are closed under limits,
it suffices to show that
\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
.
For
c\in\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 0}
,
the class of
Z\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{w\geq 0}
for which

\hom_{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}(Z,c)

is
t
-coconnective is closed under extensions, retracts, products
and sequential limits of maps with fiber already in the class.
It also contains
\mathcal{C}_{w\geq 0}=\mathcal{C}_{t\geq 0}
,
and thus consists of all
Z\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{w\geq 0}
,
showing that
c\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
.
Note that this also shows that
\mathcal{C}\to\Pro(\mathcal{C})

is
t
-exact, by adjacency and weight exactness.

Moreover, if
X\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
,
then by shifting, the above constructs a
t
-decomposition

X_{w>0}\to X\to X_{t\leq 2m+1}
where
X_{t\leq 2m+1}\in\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 2m+1})

(since this category is closed under finite limits in
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
).
The map
X_{w>0}\to X
vanishes, exhibiting
X
as retract
of
X_{t\leq 2m+1}
.
But
\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 2m+1})
is already idempotent complete,
so this shows
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}\subseteq\Pro(\mathcal{C}_{t\leq 2m+1})
.
∎

Proposition 11.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a weakly
\ell
-weighted category with adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
m
,
where
\ell,m<\infty
.
If
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}
is countable,
then
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
admits an adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
2m+2+\ell
such that:

(1)

\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
is contained in the retract-closure
of those Tate-objects sitting in an extension of
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 2m+1}

and
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 2m+2+\ell}
.

(2)

The inclusion
\Ind(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\Tate(\mathcal{C})
is weight- and
t
-exact,
whereas the inclusion
\Pro(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\Tate(\mathcal{C})
is weight exact
but of
t
-amplitude
[0,\ell]
.

Proof.

Let us begin by showing (2).
Since all the (putative) weak
t
-structures are adjacent and we already know weight exactness
of both inclusions by Theorem
11.3
,
we only need to show the statement for
t
-coconnectives.
By the description of
\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0}

and the fact that coconnective spectra are closed under extensions and retracts,
this reduces to checking that
\hom(X^{p}_{w\geq 0},X^{i}_{t\leq 0})

and
\hom(X^{i}_{w\geq\ell},X^{p}_{t\leq 0})
are coconnective spectra.
For the former, we use that the class of
Y\in\Pro(\mathcal{C})
for which

\hom(Y,X^{i}_{t\leq 0})
is coconnective contains
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}

and is closed under extensions, retracts, finite colimits, products, and sequential limits
along maps whose fiber already lies in the class.
It thus contains all of
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{w\geq 0}
, as desired.
The latter claim follows directly from the fact that
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{\geq\ell}\subseteq\Ind(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})

by Proposition
4.25
(3).

Next, we provide
t
-decompositions of Tate objects.
Recall that
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
admits an adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
2m+1

by Proposition
11.8
.
Given
X\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})
with extension
X^{p}\to X\to X^{i}
,
we have
t
-decompositions
X^{i}_{w\geq\ell+2}\to X^{i}\to X^{i}_{t\leq\ell+1}
in
\Ind(\mathcal{C})

and
X^{p}_{w>-(2m+1)}\to X^{p}\to X^{p}_{t\leq 0}
in
\Pro(\mathcal{C})
.
By (2) we get that the map
X^{i}_{w\geq\ell+2}\to\Sigma X^{p}_{t\leq 0}

vanishes, which allows us to consider the following diagram of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{w>-(2m+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{w\geq\ell+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{w>-(2m+1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{t\leq\ell+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{i}_{t\leq\ell+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma X^{p}_{t\leq 0}}}

The left vertical fiber sequence is then a
t
-decomposition for
X
of defect
2m+2+\ell
.
This establishes the adjacent weak
t
-structure of defect
2m+2+\ell
on
\Tate(\mathcal{C})
.
Note also that if
X\in\Tate(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 0}
,
then by shifting, the above constructs a
t
-decomposition

X_{w>0}\to X\to X_{t\leq 2m+2+\ell}

which exhibits
X
as a retract of
X_{t\leq 2m+2+\ell}
,
which in turn sits in an extension of an object in
\Ind(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 2m+2+\ell}

and an object in
\Pro(\mathcal{C})_{t\leq 2m+1}
.
∎

Corollary 11.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a countable weighted category
such that
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
admits an adjacent weak
t
-structure
of defect
m
, and
m<\infty
.
Then
\smash{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}
respectively
\smash{\Tate(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}
admit adjacent
weak
t
-structures of defect
2m+1
respectively
2m+2
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{w<\infty}\hookrightarrow(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{w<\infty}

is fully faithful with dense image,
it follows that also
(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})_{w<\infty}
is countable,
and hence
\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow}
satisfies the assumptions of Propositions
11.8
and
11.9
,
so that
\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
and
\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})
admit adjacent weak
t
-structures
of defects
2m+1
respectively
2m+2
.
By Lemma
11.5
we have equivalences
\smash{\Pro(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{\Pro(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\uparrow}

and
\smash{\Tate(\mathcal{C})}^{\uparrow}\simeq\smash{\Tate(\smash{\mathcal{C}}^{\uparrow})}^{\uparrow}
in
\textsf{wWCat}_{=0}
,
so we conclude by Lemma
9.3
.
∎

Example 11.11
.

\textsf{Sp}^{\omega}
is a countable weighted category
and
\textsf{Sp}^{\omega,\uparrow}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{ft}}=\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{fg}}_{>-\infty}

admits an adjacent
t
-structure.
Thus
\smash{\Pro(\textsf{Sp}^{\omega})}^{\uparrow}
respectively
\smash{\Tate(\textsf{Sp}^{\omega})}^{\uparrow}

admit adjacent weak
t
-structures of defect
1
respectively
2
.
Here, a weak
t
-structure on the left completion is really the best one can
hope for: an adjacent weak
t
-structure on
\Pro(\textsf{Sp}^{\omega})

corresponds under the equivalence
\Pro(\textsf{Sp}^{\omega})\simeq\textsf{Sp}^{\textsf{op}}

induced by Spanier-Whitehead duality to a co-adjacent weak
t
-structure
on
Sp
. However, if
X
is a spectrum such that
\hom(X,Y)\in\textsf{Sp}_{t\leq 0}

for all
Y\in\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}
, then already
X=0
!
Indeed, this is because
\mathbb{Z}\otimes I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}}=0
(
[
19
, Lemma 7.1]
)
so that
\Sigma^{n}I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}}\in\textsf{Sp}_{w\leq 0}
for all
n
,
and
0=\pi_{0}\hom(X,\Sigma^{n}I_{\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}})=\Hom(\pi_{n}X,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})

for all
n
implies
X=0
.

Appendix A
Mittag-Leffler conditions for ordinal-indexed inverse limits

In this appendix we give an analogue of a Mittag–Leffler condition
which for example guarantees that an ordinal indexed inverse diagram
of connective spectra has a connective limit.
This is crucial for proving Theorem
6.3
in the case
\kappa>\omega
.

Recall the notion of connectivity of anima and maps of anima
from Example
2.3
.
Concretely, an anima is
0
-connective if it is nonempty,
and a
0
-connective map is a
\pi_{0}
-surjection.
For
n\geq 1
, an
n
-connective anima satisfies
\pi_{k}(X)\cong\pi_{k}(*)

for
k\leq n-1
, and a map of anima is
n
-connective
if its fiber is
n
-connective for any choice of base point.
Note that if
X\to Y
is an
n
-connective map and
Y
is
n
-connective,
then also
X
is
n
-connective.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
\gamma
be an ordinal,
n\geq 0
, and
X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma^{\textsf{op}}\to\textsf{An}
a diagram satisfying

(1)

X_{0}
is
n
-connective.

(2)

For each successor ordinal
\alpha+1<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\alpha+1}\to X_{\alpha}

is
n
-connective.

(3)

For each limit ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\lambda}\to\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}

is
n
-connective.

Then all the
X_{\alpha}
for
\alpha<\gamma

and also
X_{\gamma}\coloneqq\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}X_{\alpha}
are
n
-connective.

Let us first show that we can reduce this proposition to the following
weaker lemma, which we learned from Maxime Ramzi, see
[
31
, Theorem 1.1]
. For the convenience of the reader we reproduce and elaborate
on its proof below.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\gamma
be an ordinal,
n\geq 0
, and
X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma^{\textsf{op}}\to\textsf{An}
a diagram satisfying

(1)

X_{\alpha}
is nonempty for all
\alpha<\gamma
.

(2)

For each successor ordinal
\alpha+1<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\alpha+1}\to X_{\alpha}

is a
\pi_{0}
-surjection.

(3)

For each limit ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\lambda}\to\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}

is a
\pi_{0}
-surjection.

Then
X_{\gamma}\coloneqq\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}X_{\alpha}
is nonempty.

Proof of Prop.
A.1
assuming Lemma
A.2
.

Note that Lemma
A.2

is just the
n=0
case of the Proposition,
with the following stronger assumption in place of (1):

(1’)

X_{\alpha}
is
n
-connective for all
\alpha<\gamma
.

Let us first show how this implies the cases for
n\geq 1

where we also assume (1’) instead of (1).

For the case
n=1
, the
n=0
-case immediately yields that
\lim X_{\bullet}
is
0
-connective, hence nonempty.
To show it is 1-connective, we need to argue that
\pi_{0}\lim X_{\bullet}=*
.
Given two points in
x,y\colon*\to\lim X_{\bullet}
, let
P_{x,y}\lim X_{\bullet}
be the space of paths,
which we need to prove is nonempty.
The base points
x,y
induce two base points at each level
X_{\alpha}
which are preserved by the structure maps.
We will also denote these by
x,y
.
Then
P_{x,y}\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}X_{\alpha}\simeq\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}P_{x,y}X_{\alpha}
,
and the diagram
P_{x,y}X_{\bullet}
satisfies the assumptions of the
n=0
case,
so that its limit is non-empty. This settles the case
n=1
.
For
n\geq 2
, we can do a similar reduction to
n=1
by taking iterated loop spaces,
where we now already know that
\pi_{0}\lim X_{\bullet}=*
so there is only one choice of basepoint.

Thus the assumptions (1’)+(2)+(3) imply the main claim for all
n\geq 0
.
Let us now show that we can replace (1’) with (1),
for which we need to show that under assumptions (1)-(3),
all the
X_{\alpha}
are
n
-connective.
We do this by transfinite induction.
Namely, condition (2) guarantees that if
X_{\alpha}
is
n
-connective
then also
X_{\alpha+1}
is. Moreover, if
\lambda<\gamma

is a limit ordinal so that for all
\alpha<\lambda

we have already shown that
X_{\alpha}
is
n
-connective,
then
X_{\bullet}|_{\lambda}
satisfies (1’)+(2)+(3)
so that
\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}
is
n
-connective.
By (3) we see that
X_{\lambda}
sits in a fiber sequence
between two
n
-connective anima, and is thus itself
n
-connective
(for
n\geq 1
there is no issue with base points,
and for
n=0
(3) precisely tells us that we have
\pi_{0}
-surjections,
so
X_{\lambda}
is nonempty).
By transfinite induction, this shows that for all
\alpha<\gamma
,
the anima
X_{\alpha}
is
n
-connective, as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

Note that if
\gamma=\beta+1
is not a limit ordinal,
then
\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}X_{\alpha}=X_{\beta}
, and we are done by (1).
So suppose from now on that
\gamma
is a limit ordinal.

By straightening-unstraightening,
the diagram
X_{\bullet}
induces a right fibration of quasi-categories

p\colon\int X_{\bullet}\to\gamma
, and we can identify

\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}X_{\alpha}
with the Kan-complex of sections

\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\gamma,\int X_{\bullet})
,
which we have to prove is nonempty.
More generally, for each
\beta\leq\gamma

we can identify the Kan complex
\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\beta,\int X_{\bullet})

with the limit
\lim_{\alpha<\beta}X_{\alpha}
.
Here we implicitly identify an ordinal
\alpha

with the nerve
N(\alpha)
of the 1-category given by the poset
\alpha
.

Because the transition maps
\alpha\to\beta

are monomorphisms of simplicial sets and hence cofibrations in Quillen model structure whenever
\alpha\leq\beta
,
the expression
\beta=\colim_{\alpha<\beta}\alpha

is true both strictly (i.e. in simplicial sets) and homotopically (i.e. in
\textsf{Cat}_{\infty}
).
Since
\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(-,\int X_{\bullet})
also sends colimits
of simplicial sets to limits,
we conclude that for each
\beta\leq\gamma
,
the strict limit
\lim_{\alpha<\beta}\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\alpha,\int X_{\bullet})

is a Kan-complex representing the limit
\lim_{\alpha<\beta}X_{\alpha}
.

For
\alpha<\beta<\gamma
, consider the following diagram of pullbacks of
simplicial sets

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\beta,\int X_{\bullet})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}(\beta,\int X_{\bullet})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\alpha,\int X_{\bullet})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}(\alpha,\int X_{\bullet})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}(\beta,\gamma)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textsf{Fun}(\alpha,\gamma)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\inc}

Note that since
p
is a right fibration,
also
p_{*}
is a right fibration by
[
25
,
Tag 00TQ
]
.
Since
N(\alpha)\to N(\beta)
is a monomorphism
of simplicial sets,
[
25
,
Tag 00TP
]
tells us that also
R
is a right fibration.
Since right fibrations are stable under pullback,
we conclude that all vertical maps in this diagram are right fibrations,
and thus that all the strict pullbacks present the corresponding
(homotopy-)pullbacks taken in
\textsf{Cat}_{\infty}
.

Assumption (2) now tells us that the right fibration

\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\beta+1,\int X_{\bullet})\to\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\beta,\int X_{\bullet})
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, and thus surjective on 0-simplices
by the defining lifting property against
\{1\}\to\Delta^{1}
.
Similarly, assumption (3) tells us that
\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\lambda+1,\int X_{\bullet})\to\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\lambda,\int X_{\bullet})

is surjective on 0-simplices.
Hence, passing to
0
-simplices in the
\gamma^{\textsf{op}}
-indexed diagram

\beta\mapsto\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\beta,\int X_{\bullet})

thus yields a diagram
Y_{\bullet}\colon\gamma^{\textsf{op}}\to\textsf{Set}
satisfying:

(1)

Each
Y_{\alpha}
is nonempty for
\alpha<\gamma
.
Indeed, since
\gamma
is a limit ordinal,
we know that
\alpha+1<\gamma
.
Now
Y_{\alpha+1}
is the set of 0-simplices
of a Kan complex representing the nonempty anima
X_{\alpha}

and is thus nonempty.
Since we have a map
Y_{\alpha+1}\to Y_{\alpha}
,
also the latter is nonempty.

(2)

For any
\alpha<\gamma
,
the map
Y_{\alpha+1}\to Y_{\alpha}
is surjective.
Note that here we use both assumptions (2) and (3)
(the latter in the case
\alpha=\lambda
).

(3)

For any limit-ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
we have
Y_{\lambda}\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}Y_{\alpha}
,
since
\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\lambda,\int X_{\bullet})\cong\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}\textsf{Fun}_{/\gamma}(\alpha,\int X_{\bullet})
is a strict limit
in simplicial sets, which passes to 0-simplices.

We need to show that
\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}Y_{\alpha}\neq\varnothing
.
We define a compatible system of elements
y_{\alpha}\in Y_{\alpha}

via transfinite induction.
Pick any
y_{0}\in Y_{0}
.
Having defined
y_{\alpha}\in Y_{\alpha}
, we let
y_{\alpha+1}\in Y_{\alpha+1}
be any preimage of
y_{\alpha}
under the surjection
Y_{\alpha+1}\to Y_{\alpha}
.
If
\lambda\leq\gamma
is a limit ordinal so that we have defined
the compatible family
y_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha<\lambda
,
then this precisely defines us a point in
\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}Y_{\alpha}\cong Y_{\lambda}
.
By transfinite induction, we obtain a point in
Y_{\gamma}\coloneqq\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}Y_{\alpha}
.
∎

Corollary A.3
.

Let
R
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring,

\gamma
an ordinal,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma^{\textsf{op}}\to\textsf{LMod}(R)
a diagram satisfying

(1)

X_{0}
is
n
-connective (in the canonical
t
-structure on
\textsf{Mod}(R)
).

(2)

For each successor ordinal
\alpha+1<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\alpha+1}\to X_{\alpha}
has
n
-connective fiber.

(3)

For each limit ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\lambda}\to\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}

has
n
-connective fiber.

Then all the
X_{\alpha}
for
\alpha<\gamma

and also
\lim_{\alpha<\gamma}X_{\alpha}
are
n
-connective.

Proof.

Since connectivity, limits and colimits are detected and preserved by the forgetful functor
U\colon\textsf{LMod}(R)\to\textsf{Sp}
,
we may as well assume that
R=\mathbb{S}
so that
\textsf{LMod}(R)=\textsf{Sp}
.
But then we can reduce to Proposition
A.1

by considering
\Omega^{\infty-k}
for
k\gg 0
.
∎

Remark A.4
.

In the case where the diagram
X_{\bullet}
takes values in
\textsf{Sp}_{t=0}\simeq\textsf{Ab}
(though the limits are still taken in
Sp
)
the second condition simply becomes that the relevant maps are all surjections.
That such inverse systems of abelian groups have vanishing
\lim^{(n)}
-terms for
n>0

was shown in
[
27
, Corollary A.3.14]
.

Recall the orthogonal complement notation from Section
1
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable category and
\mathcal{D}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
a full subcategory.
Then:

(1)

{}^{\perp}\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\perp}

are closed under retracts and extensions.

(2)

If
\mathcal{D}
is closed under looping
(
suspensions
)

then
{}^{\perp}\mathcal{D}

(
\mathcal{D}^{\perp}
)
is closed under all colimits

(
all limits
)

and
\mathcal{D}^{\perp}

(
{}^{\perp}\mathcal{D}
)

is closed under finite limits
(
finite colimits
)

and colimits
(
limits
)

of diagrams
X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma\to\mathcal{C}

(
X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma^{\textsf{op}}\to\mathcal{C}
)
for some ordinal
\gamma
satisfying

(a)

For a successor ordinal
\alpha+1<\gamma
,
the map
X_{\alpha}\to X_{\alpha+1}
has cofiber in
\mathcal{D}

(
the map
X_{\alpha+1}\to X_{\alpha}
has fiber in
\mathcal{D}
)
.

(b)

For a limit ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
the map
\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}\to X_{\lambda}

has cofiber in
\mathcal{D}

(
the map
X_{\lambda}\to\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}

has fiber in
\mathcal{D}
)
.

Proof.

This follows from the analogous closure properties of

\textsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}

and
\textsf{Sp}_{t\leq 0}\subseteq\textsf{Sp}
.
For the ordinal-indexed (co)limits,
this is Corollary
A.3
.
∎

The following lemma is needed to identify the weight coconnectives
in the Anderson weight structure on spectra, see Proposition
10.3
.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\gamma
be an ordinal and
A_{\bullet}\colon\gamma\to\textsf{Ab}

a diagram of abelian groups. Suppose that

(1)

Each
A_{\alpha}
is a free abelian group.

(2)

For a successor ordinal
\alpha+1<\gamma
,
the map
A_{\alpha}\to A_{\alpha+1}
is injective
with free abelian cokernel.

(3)

For each limit ordinal
\lambda<\gamma
,
the map
\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}A_{\alpha}\to A_{\lambda}

is injective with free abelian cokernel.

Then
A_{\gamma}\coloneqq\colim_{\alpha<\gamma}A_{\alpha}

is also free abelian.

Proof.

If
\gamma
is a successor ordinal, we are done by (1),
so assume from now on that it is a limit ordinal.
Extend
A_{\bullet}
to a diagram
\gamma+1\to\textsf{Ab}

by taking the colimit.
We will construct a diagram
X_{\bullet}\colon\gamma+1\to\textsf{Set}

where each map
X_{\alpha}\to X_{\beta}
is an injection
for
\alpha<\beta\leq\gamma
,
together with a natural isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}[X_{\bullet}]\cong A_{\bullet}
.

Since
A_{0}
is free, we have
A_{0}\cong\mathbb{Z}[X_{0}]
for some set
X_{0}
.
Suppose we have constructed a diagram
X_{\bullet}\colon\alpha+1\to\textsf{Set}

together with a natural isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}[X_{\bullet}]\cong(A_{\bullet})_{\alpha+1}
. To extend this to
\alpha+2
,
we note that there is a short exact sequence
A_{\alpha}\to A_{\alpha+1}\to C

where
C
is a free abelian group. Thus the sequence splits,
which allows us to extend the basis
X_{\alpha}
of
A_{\alpha}

to a basis
X_{\alpha+1}
of
A_{\alpha+1}
, giving an extension
of
X_{\bullet}
and the natural isomorphism to
\alpha+2
.

Now if
\lambda<\gamma
is a limit ordinal
so that we have constructed the
lift up to stage
\alpha
for all
\alpha<\lambda

then we can use the fact that

\textsf{Fun}(\lambda,\textsf{Set})\cong\lim_{\alpha<\lambda}\textsf{Fun}(\alpha,\textsf{Set})

and the concrete description of limits in the 1-category
of 1-categories to obtain the extension
X_{\bullet}\colon\lambda\to\textsf{Set}

of injections with a natural isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}[X_{\bullet}]\cong A_{\bullet}|_{\lambda}
.
Now assumption (3) guarantees by the same argument
as above that we can extend the basis
\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}X_{\alpha}

of
\colim_{\alpha<\lambda}A_{\alpha}

to a basis of
A_{\lambda}
, and thus obtain an extension
of
X_{\bullet}
and the natural isomorphism to
\lambda+1
.
This concludes the transfinite induction.
∎
